#5287. Tether Says It Froze More Than $1.5B Linked to the Binance Theft

Strict editorial policy that focuses on accuracy, relevance, and impartiality

Morbi pretium leo et nisl aliquam mollis. Quisque arcu lorem, ultricies quis pellentesque nec, ullamcorper eu odio.

TL;DR: Tether said on November 2 that it froze more than $1.5 billion in USDT linked to the late-October Binance attack, working with investigators and industry partners. Tether’s announcement gives the company’s account of its involvement; it does not independently establish the ultimate ownership or recoverability of every frozen token.

It is tempting to present freezing power as either an unqualified feature or a fatal flaw. The more useful comparison is with the protections and limits users expect from banks and payment providers. In both worlds, legal compliance can restrict access, yet the remedy process and transparency of decisions matter just as much as the initial intervention.

Tether said on November 2 that it froze more than $1.5 billion in USDT linked to the late-October Binance attack, working with investigators and industry partners. The company said it moved quickly after receiving actionable intelligence. The claim describes issuer-enforced restrictions on specified token balances; a freeze is not the same thing as completed asset recovery or money already returned to victims. The dated original disclosure is the starting point for the figures and product scope discussed here.

Centralised stablecoins can include smart-contract functions that allow the issuer to block transfers by designated addresses. This gives law-enforcement partners a tool that does not exist in the same form for native Bitcoin. But it also makes a particular promise implicit in the product: the ability to transact depends in part on an issuer retaining authority over the transfer rules.

The case illustrates a genuine trade-off. The power to immobilise suspected stolen funds may increase the chance that investigators preserve value while they work. The same central control raises questions about mistaken identification, appeals, transparency and the geographic reach of private restrictions. Stablecoins are often used because they feel like simple digital dollars, yet their governance can be as consequential as their transaction speed.

The phrase “digital dollar” can obscure important differences between products. A stablecoin holder faces an issuer and its reserve arrangements; an exchange user faces trading and custody arrangements; a merchant faces conversion and settlement partners. A good assessment follows that chain of obligations instead of assuming a one-dollar target eliminates counterparty risk.

For additional perspective, it is worth reading our existing reporting on Binance’s earlier phased withdrawal restart and the September-end Tether reserve disclosure. They explain related market structure and should not be mistaken for evidence that every new proposal is already live.

Tether’s announcement gives the company’s account of its involvement; it does not independently establish the ultimate ownership or recoverability of every frozen token. Some illicit proceeds may have moved through assets or networks outside the issuer’s control. And a publicly reported freeze total cannot be added to other recovery figures without checking whether they cover the same funds.

Stablecoin adoption depends heavily on ordinary financial services at the edges. Funding accounts, meeting identity rules, accessing banking partners and redeeming balances remain necessary even if a token transfer settles quickly. A project that solves those steps can be useful, but that usefulness cannot be established from headline issuance or a partnership announcement alone.

Watch for validated recoveries, legally authorised unfreezing or transfer procedures and clearer accounting of overlapping industry freeze figures. The longer-term issue is whether issuers can develop fast, reviewable processes that protect innocent users while meeting legitimate law-enforcement demands. A successful freeze is the start of an asset-preservation story, not necessarily its final chapter.

#5286. Hyperliquid (HYPE) Bulls in Action Amid 703% Liquidation Imbalance

Hyperliquid is witnessing sustained sell pressure across its spot and derivatives markets over the past weeks, although the market is gradually showing signs of a potential relief.

While the market has remained weak since last month, the latest data from Coinglass shows that momentum is gradually being restored, but long traders are still getting wiped out from the Hyperliquid derivatives market.

With the market already showing signs of a potential relief, it appears that bullish leveraged traders are still getting hit by HYPE's current mixed price action more than short traders.

According to the data, a total of $4.49 million in HYPE positions were liquidated over the last 24 hours. Notably, long positions accounted for $3.99 million, which marks 88.9% of the total liquidations seen during the same period.

This means that short traders were barely affected, as further data showed that short liquidations only stood at $496,730, marking just 11.1% of the total liquidations.

As such, the fast-growing crypto asset has witnessed a liquidation imbalance of 703% over the last 24 hours, as long liquidations were substantially higher than short liquidations.

Apparently, this means that traders betting on HYPE's price surge have suffered nearly eight times the losses recorded by bearish traders.

While the intense sell-off seen across the broader crypto market is gradually beginning to ease, Hyperliquid's hourly price chart has suddenly flipped into green territory.

However, HYPE has continued to show mixed price action, hovering around $86 as of the time of writing, following a 2.26% decline in its price over the last 24 hours.

With sentiment already turning positive, it appears that HYPE's long traders may not have timed their bets properly, causing the massive wipeouts of bullish positions.

#5285. Binance restores access across nine networks – Can deposits keep pace with exits?

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

After the $6.3 billion hack, which shook the entire community, Binance has resumed cryptocurrency withdrawals for several major digital assets.

This includes Bitcoin [BTC], Ethereum [ETH], Ripple [XRP], Solana [SOL], Dogecoin [DOGE], Chainlink [LINK], Cardano [ADA], Litecoin [LTC], and Shiba Inu [SHIB].

According to the exchange’s announcement, withdrawals for these assets reopened at 04:00 UTC on the 3rd of November across nine supported networks.

That said, the exchange has also begun restoring withdrawals for selected assets across additional networks, including Sui, Arbitrum, Optimism, TRON, and Base. However, the process will take place gradually as Binance completes its security checks across different networks and assets.

Remarking on the same, the Binance team noted,

We are prioritizing tokens based on user demand over the past week, restoring each network-asset pair carefully after completing rigorous security checks. Additional assets will be added as they become ready.

Adding more to this, one of CryptQuant’s analysts noted,

Withdrawals are being processed correctly, with no further security issues and today shows a net outflow totaling -16,021 BTC so far.

This coincided with AMBCrypto’s recent report that Binance witnessed significant withdrawal activity after reopening its services on the 1st of November. In fact, within the first two hours, users withdrew approximately $733 million worth of assets. All in all, the exchange has replenished approximately 95% of the affected assets using its own funds.

They further confirmed that BTC, ETH, USDT, BNB, SOL, USDC, XRP, USD1, RLUSD, and ZEC are fully backed at a minimum 1:1 ratio.

Although the large outflows raised concerns about customer confidence, continued deposits and gradually declining withdrawal volumes suggested that activity was slowly normalizing.

Now, while Binance buckles up to resume activity after the hack, the exchange also recorded approximately $3.29 billion in net outflows over the past 24 hours, marking a decline of more than 50% from its earlier intraday peak of nearly $7 billion, according to DeFiLlama data.

The decline suggests that the initial rush to move funds away from Binance may be easing following the reported security breach. Meanwhile, the exchange’s remaining on-chain holdings stood at approximately $127.78 billion.

This comes on the heels of Binance’s latest Proof of Reserves (PoR) report, dated 31st October, which revealed changes in the cryptocurrency holdings maintained by users on the exchange.

The most notable development was a sharp decline in USDT balances, alongside falling Ethereum and BNB holdings. However, Bitcoin holdings recorded a slight increase.

However, fear still remains, as the Binance hacker has reportedly started mixing stolen cryptocurrency to make the funds harder to trace.

So far, the stolen assets have passed through approximately 12,000 wallets across 11 blockchain networks in more than 40,000 transactions. Lastly, around $3.24 billion remains in theft-linked addresses, primarily in Bitcoin and Ethereum, while approximately $2.85 billion has been frozen.

#5284. Strategy Adds 811 Bitcoin and Repurchases $208.2M of STRC Shares

Strict editorial policy that focuses on accuracy, relevance, and impartiality

Morbi pretium leo et nisl aliquam mollis. Quisque arcu lorem, ultricies quis pellentesque nec, ullamcorper eu odio.

TL;DR: Strategy announced on November 2 that it had acquired another 811 Bitcoin for roughly $65.5 million and repurchased approximately $208.2 million of its STRC preferred shares. The dollar amounts are those disclosed by Strategy for the specified transactions.

The timing is notable because the company had separately disclosed large Bitcoin holdings at the end of October. A new acquisition can be checked against that earlier snapshot, but readers should not merge numbers from different dates without adjustment. Preferred-stock capital structure is less eye-catching than the coin count, yet it can be decisive when market conditions change.

Strategy announced on November 2 that it had acquired another 811 Bitcoin for roughly $65.5 million and repurchased approximately $208.2 million of its STRC preferred shares. The disclosure combines two very different capital-allocation decisions: increasing BTC holdings and reducing a class of outstanding preferred equity. Investors should assess both parts rather than treat the announcement as a simple Bitcoin purchase notice. The dated original disclosure is the starting point for the figures and product scope discussed here.

Purchasing Bitcoin increases the company’s direct exposure to the asset. Repurchasing preferred shares changes the number of securities outstanding and can affect financing obligations, investor returns and how management uses available capital. STRC is not common equity, so its buyback has different implications from repurchasing ordinary shares; the security’s terms and price matter.

Treasury-company headlines often focus on how many coins were added, but financing costs help determine whether that strategy creates durable shareholder value. The simultaneous buyback is a useful reminder that a Bitcoin-heavy balance sheet must still be funded and managed. If new securities are issued at one point and repurchased later, the relevant comparison involves proceeds, purchase prices and future distributions.

Fund flows and corporate Bitcoin holdings share one characteristic: they are easy to count and harder to interpret. A securities wrapper reports its own creations and redemptions; a public company reports transactions inside a particular capital structure. Neither dataset captures every spot-market participant. A useful analysis establishes the reporting date, identifies what was measured and asks how much the disclosed activity represents relative to the wider market.

This development also connects to two subjects our newsroom has been following: Strategy’s earlier capital-management review and the company’s expanded Bitcoin financing framework. Those background reports help separate established facts from a new, still-developing announcement.

The dollar amounts are those disclosed by Strategy for the specified transactions. They do not, on their own, establish the company’s total current market value, future BTC acquisitions or returns to every class of shareholder. Nor should an accounting gain on Bitcoin be treated as cash generated by ordinary operations without looking at the underlying financial statements.

The critical operational issue is access to liquid Bitcoin exposure. A fund shareholder may own a listed security, while a corporate treasury holder owns shares in a company with liabilities and management decisions. Those two instruments can behave differently even when Bitcoin is the most visible asset behind both. Treating the wrapper as identical to the coin erases meaningful risks.

Next, monitor additional regulatory filings, financing terms and the ratio of Bitcoin acquisitions to securities issuance or retirement. For a business whose public narrative is built around BTC accumulation, the balance-sheet mechanics are increasingly part of the investment thesis. The quantity of Bitcoin is clear; the economic cost of obtaining and maintaining that exposure takes more work to understand.

#5283. Solana Officially Scores 1,000 Days Without Outage

The Solana network has hit a significant milestone in its quest to overcome its early hurdles, successfully going 1,000 days without a network-wide outage.

This feat stands in stark contrast to Solana's past, when the blockchain experienced some notable network disruptions. One of the more significant outages occurred in September 2021, when a spike in bot traffic from Grape Protocol IDO transactions led to a surge in transactions that overwhelmed validators and hindered consensus, resulting in a roughly 17-hour network outage.

The Solana network experienced similar problems in April 2022, with a large traffic spike due to an NFT mint that led to a roughly 7-hour outage. Another outage happened in June 2022, when a bug involving durable nonce transactions caused block production to stop for roughly 4.5 hours. Finally, in September 2022, the network experienced a fork due to a bug in the consensus layer, which led to a halt in block production lasting over 6 hours.

The final major outage occurred back in February 2023, when abnormal block forwarding overwhelmed Solana's Turbine block propagation system, leaving validators stuck in a vote-only state and leading to a coordinated restart. This would prove to be a monumental event for Solana in terms of its engineering progress.

Solana has seen a string of updates and engineering innovations by its developers in an effort to improve the network's ability to avoid the failures that had plagued it in the past. Some of these features include stake-weighted quality of service to better prioritize traffic from staked validators. The network also saw networking innovations such as QUIC that provide more flexibility in dealing with spam and congestion. There have also been updates to deduplication logic following the February 2023 outage. Additionally, the network has seen innovations such as a local fee market and other scheduling improvements to discourage users and bots from spamming the network with duplicate transactions.

Recently, Solana has emphasized that network resilience should be taken seriously. In a September 2026 update, the project shared that the network has seen 100% uptime since February 2024 and has continued to handle large amounts of traffic. It also highlighted a major infrastructure provider experiencing a routing failure in August 2026 that caused validators representing nearly 29% of the network's stake to go offline, but this did not result in a halt in block production. The project uses this as an example of increased fault tolerance.

For Solana, this 1,000-day milestone is not just a success in terms of network uptime. It's a statement and a demonstration to the world that a network once mocked for outages can now support serious financial and on-chain use cases.

#5282. North Korea Calls the Binance Hack Claims “Absurd” and Points at the US, as $3.24 Billion in Stolen Crypto Still Waits to Be Laundered

Bitcoin news: North Korea calls Binance hack claims “absurd” as $3.24B in stolen crypto, mostly BTC and ETH, sits in hacker wallets.

In Bitcoin news on Tuesday, November 3, North Korea rejected US claims that its hackers stole $6.3 billion from Binance, as Kim Yo Jong called the FBI’s attribution “absurd rhetoric” and suggested that Washington itself might be behind the heist.

The statement, carried by the state news agency KCNA, came three days after the FBI named TraderTraitor, a North Korean state hacking unit, as the culprit.

NORTH KOREA SAYS CLAIMS THAT IT WAS BEHIND BINANCE HEIST IS "FABRICATION" – KCNA – RTRS

— Tree News (@TreeNewsFeed) November 2, 2026

It changes little on the ground. Blockchain firms Chainalysis, TRM Labs and Elliptic had already tied the theft to North Korea, and the money is still moving.

That is why it matters for Bitcoin. Elliptic counts $3.24 billion still sitting in wallets linked to the attackers, mostly in Bitcoin and Ether, and it saw the first stolen BTC go through a mixer on Monday.

BTC slipped 1.8% to $76,729 on the day, back on its 200-day average, and the Crypto Fear & Greed Index dropped from 37 to 26.

So what exactly did North Korea say, and how much of the stolen money could still hit the market?

That the US is making it up, and might be the real thief. Kim Yo Jong, Kim Jong Un’s sister and a senior ruling party official, made four points:

She also rejected the FBI’s blame for the $1.5 billion Bybit hack in February 2025, saying the US “has attempted to spread misunderstanding of the DPRK.”

None of this is new. North Korea has denied any link to crypto theft every time, and the statement offered nothing to back its claim against the US.

DISCOVER: Binance Hacker Sits on $1.4 Billion in Bitcoin, Triple a Week of ETF Buying, as Route 2 FI Buys the Dip

The FBI and three blockchain firms, each with its own trail. The FBI’s statement on Saturday, November 1 said it had watched TraderTraitor convert stolen assets into ether and expected more laundering.

The private investigators point the same way:

None of the firms has published the off-chain evidence behind its attribution, and no court has ruled on it.

Half of it is still in the hackers’ hands. Elliptic’s latest count puts the theft at $6.43 billion, slightly above the $6.3 billion most reports cite.

The hackers are moving slower than after Bybit, when they cleared all the stolen ether in ten days. In the first four days here, they used about 12,000 wallets on 11 blockchains, one new wallet every 25 seconds.

Elliptic expects the rest to be turned into Bitcoin over the coming months and cashed out through OTC brokers and exchanges. That is $3.24 billion of potential supply, drip-fed into a market already in fear.

It is sitting right on it. BTC trades at $76,729 on Binance, down 1.8% in 24 hours, after dipping to $76,389 on Tuesday morning. The 200-day EMA (exponential moving average) is at $76,502, and the October 30 post-hack low of $76,326 is just below.

That floor has now held for five days. The bounces haven’t, though: Monday’s push stalled at $78,878, well short of the 50-day EMA at $80,105.

In Bitcoin news, North Korea’s denial moves nothing. The $3.24 billion still in the hackers’ wallets does, and the first sign of it heading to exchanges would put $76,326 under pressure.

#5281. Tether holds about 150,000 Bitcoin, Ardoino says—What do its reserves show?

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

During a Plan ₿ Forum presentation in Lugano, Tether CEO Paolo Ardoino indicated the company owns nearly 150,000 Bitcoin. That amount would be worth about $11.5 billion with the Bitcoin price reported on November 3.

This figure is higher than Tether’s most recent USDT reserve disclosure, as the number shows its broader Bitcoin exposure.

“We have give or take 150,000 bitcoin ourselves,” Ardoino said, according to his disclosure during his speech at the Lugano event. He discussed the holdings while outlining Tether’s support for Bitcoin and its mining investments.

Ardoino did not give any acquisition schedule or breakdown identifying which Tether entities hold the coins. The timing and composition of the approximately 150,000 BTC position, therefore, remain unclear.

Tether has pursued Bitcoin purchases for several years. About three years ago, it announced its plans to start allocating, regularly, up to 15% of its net realized operating profits to Bitcoin.

Tether published its latest reserve report, providing a view of its USDT reserves. The company released the reserves of its latest report [for September 30] on October 30.

It lists around $8.37 billion in Bitcoin, and with today’s valuation per coin, the firm holds roughly 100,060 BTC.

Tether has long-term proprietary investments that are held separately from token reserves. Ardoino’s presentation has not made the connection between these investment returns and his Bitcoin estimate clear, yet.

There is roughly 50,000 BTC remaining unaccounted for between the CEO’s 200,000 to 220,000 BTC approximation vs the reserve report at face value.

Bitcoin holdings constitute part of Tether’s reserve portfolio. Its direct and indirect exposure to U.S. Treasury bills reached $132.4 billion at the end of September.

The total reserve assets was $191.45 billion, with liabilities amounting to $183.50 billion. The net surplus reached about $7.95 billion, a notable increase from $4.11 billion in June.

#5280. 6.7 Million Stellar (XLM) Stolen in Massive $40 Million Mercado Bitcoin Breach

Disclaimer: The opinions expressed by our writers are their own and do not represent the views of U.Today. The financial and market information provided on U.Today is intended for informational purposes only. U.Today is not liable for any financial losses incurred while trading cryptocurrencies. Conduct your own research by contacting financial experts before making any investment decisions. We believe that all content is accurate as of the date of publication, but certain offers mentioned may no longer be available.

Brazilian crypto platform Mercado Bitcoin has confirmed the detection and containment of an issue in one of its systems. As a precaution, the exchange has temporarily suspended deposits and withdrawals, assuring clients of the safety of their assets and the absence of a data leak.

The incident occurred shortly after the platform passed a local licensing deadline.

According to independent blockchain company Hypernative, suspicious outflows from wallets linked to the exchange exceeded $40 million (approximately 217 million reais). Analysts recorded the movement of assets on the Ethereum, Bitcoin, Solana, Tron and Stellar (XLM) networks.

In total, the activity spanned at least 10 blockchains, also including Polygon, Arbitrum, Avalanche and BNB Smart Chain. The first suspicious transfers were recorded at around 02:15 UTC and involved Bitcoin deposit addresses attributed to the exchange.

Among the flagged assets were 106 BTC and 2,852 ETH. The hackers carried out direct token transfers, which indicates a compromise of operational reserves rather than segregated client assets.

Among the withdrawn assets, blockchain analysts recorded a large volume of Stellar tokens: about 6.7 million XLM were consolidated at addresses linked to the suspicious transactions.

As part of the investigation and coordination of efforts with the authorities, Mercado Bitcoin published a specific Stellar destination address to help the community and security firms track the movement of assets in real time.

The move is aimed at monitoring the movement of tokens and preventing their laundering through third-party services.

At the moment, the exchange is conducting an investigation, tracking the assets and promising to provide regular updates. The exchange has not yet confirmed the amount involved, disclosed which system was compromised or given a date for resuming deposits and withdrawals.

Until the platform fully resumes normal operations and the security of its infrastructure is confirmed, users are advised to refrain from making new deposits.

#5279. BitMine Reports 6.06M ETH Holdings, With More Than 5.22M Staked

Strict editorial policy that focuses on accuracy, relevance, and impartiality

Morbi pretium leo et nisl aliquam mollis. Quisque arcu lorem, ultricies quis pellentesque nec, ullamcorper eu odio.

TL;DR: BitMine Immersion Technologies reported holdings of 6,060,506 ETH as of November 1 in a November 2 filing exhibit. Cryptocurrency balance sheets are sensitive to valuation dates.

The filing’s detailed token count is valuable precisely because it can be compared with later disclosures. It also exposes a blind spot in quick “ETH treasury” comparisons: two companies with identical token balances could have very different liquidity, financing and operational risk. Staking arrangements are an essential part of that distinction.

BitMine Immersion Technologies reported holdings of 6,060,506 ETH as of November 1 in a November 2 filing exhibit. The company said 5,227,037 ETH were staked, alongside smaller Bitcoin holdings, cash and other positions. These are issuer-disclosed balances at a specified date, not independently observed live holdings or a claim about Ethereum’s total currently liquid supply. The dated original disclosure is the starting point for the figures and product scope discussed here.

Staking means committing ETH to help secure Ethereum’s proof-of-stake network through validators or staking arrangements. Rewards depend on protocol conditions and operating choices, while access to staked assets can involve withdrawal queues or third-party arrangements. A substantial staked position may produce ongoing reward income, but it is not equivalent to holding the same amount in immediately transferable cash.

BitMine’s size makes its liquidity choices important. A large corporate ETH owner may hold a long-term position, but the market will still examine potential issuance, borrowing, staking income and the ability to rebalance. The issuer says its holdings represent a notable share of ETH’s supply; the comparison is a company calculation and should be tied to the supply and price assumptions used.

Ethereum has multiple sources of demand and risk: trading, staking, stablecoin settlement, application fees and long-term treasury allocation. A change in one part of that system should be tested against the others instead of promoted into a universal explanation. The market often overreacts when one publicly available metric is mistaken for the whole network economy.

For additional perspective, it is worth reading our existing reporting on BitMine’s earlier ETH accumulation and staking disclosure and our Ethereum market reporting. Those background reports help separate established facts from a new, still-developing announcement.

Cryptocurrency balance sheets are sensitive to valuation dates. A dollar figure derived from the company’s ETH price assumption can change materially without any coins moving. Staking rewards should also be separated from share-price performance and company operating costs. A large token position does not by itself tell shareholders whether their ownership is increasing or being diluted.

For investors, the right comparison is between liquidity, expected holding period and obligations. Staked ETH, ETF shares and treasury holdings may all track the same underlying asset, but their exit routes and operating constraints differ. A useful report therefore asks not simply how much ETH is involved, but how readily the exposure can be changed when conditions deteriorate.

Look for future reports distinguishing unstaked liquidity, locked or queued balances, reward income, cash uses and equity issuance. Those measures will tell investors more about treasury resilience than the headline ETH number alone. For Ethereum, the interesting network question is whether institutional-sized staking concentrations affect how observers assess operator diversity and risk.

#5278. Ethereum exchange reserves hit 2016 low – As Bitmine adds another $11.22M ETH

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Ethereum [ETH] exchange reserves dropped to their lowest levels since 2016, raising hopes of a supply-driven recovery despite the prevailing market weakness.

According to CryptoQuant Community insights, the total ETH exchange reserves declined to roughly 14.5 million ETH on November 2. This decline reportedly marked a 58.6% reduction from the 2020 peak of 35 million ETH.

Over that period, the exchanges have in fact lost around 20.5 million ETH from their cumulative balances.

Notably, Binance registered a similar drop, with reserves falling 37.1% from their 2025 peak of 4.9 million ETH, with its holdings reaching 3.08 million ETH, revisiting levels last seen in late 2020.

It is also worth noting that the reserve drop also coincided with reports of Binance’s October 29 hack, which allegedly caused significant losses.

For context, these security concerns could have encouraged investors to transfer their holdings into self-custody wallets, rather than necessarily indicating stronger accumulation or renewed buying demand.

Institutional accumulation has also strengthened despite the token’s recent struggles, with Tom Lee’s Bitmine expanding its ETH holdings.

Lookonchain recently reported on X that Bitmine purchased another 5,000 ETH valued roughly $11.22 million, bringing the total acquisition to 6.065 million ETH, worth nearly $13.7 billion.

The institution now controls around 4.96% of Ethereum’s total supply, strengthening its significant exposure to the asset.

Meanwhile, CoinGlass analytics highlighted that Ethereum’s Spot netflows remained negative, registering approximately $7.91 million in net outflows at press time.

Bitmine’s continued purchases, together with the negative Spot netflows, reinforced the declining immediate selling pressure picture.

Traders on the derivatives market also showed renewed optimism despite Ethereum’s recent market weakness. In particular, ETH’s OI-Weighted Funding Rate had recovered to 0.0101% at press time, returning to positive territory after an earlier brief negative reading.

This recovery implied that long traders were paying shorts, which reflected fresh demand for bullish leveraged positions.

Besides, Binance’s top trader Long/Short Ratio revealed a considerable preference for long positions, with the long accounts representing 70.2% of top traders, compared to 29.8% holding short positions.

These readings together suggested that market participants were increasingly optimistic for price recovery despite the prevailing downward trend.

Ethereum’s defense of the $2,243 support level validated the hopes of a price rebound despite the prevailing bearish market outlook.

Following the defense, Ethereum’s market value sat at $2,254 as of writing time, positioning itself around the middle of its descending channel.

More importantly, the RSI indicator stood at 33.23, approaching oversold conditions and raising the possibility of selling exhaustion.

The Money Flow Index (MFI) meanwhile reached 37.18, highlighting relatively weak capital inflows despite the successful defense of the support level.

Overall, Ethereum’s declining exchange reserves, Bitmine accumulation, and bullish derivatives positioning favored the possibility of a rebound.

A continued buying above $2,243 could strengthen that outlook, while reclaiming the $2,385 resistance would provide stronger confirmation of a sustained recovery.

#5277. Trump Iran Strikes Push Bitcoin Under $77K as Oil Jumps

Home » Crypto Market News » Trump Iran Strikes Push Bitcoin Under $77K as Oil Jumps

Surging oil and cascading liquidations send Bitcoin below short-term holder cost basis.

The crypto market faced renewed selling pressure on Tuesday, November 3, as geopolitical tensions flared again in the Middle East. Total crypto market capitalization fell 1.7% following reports of military actions on Iran’s Qeshm Island, reviving fears of a prolonged conflict and pushing oil higher.

The broader market impact of the Donald Trump Iran strikes pushed crude oil prices up by more than 1.6%, with Brent crude above $106.95 a barrel, as hopes for an immediate diplomatic resolution quickly faded.

As energy supply fears triggered a classic “risk-off” environment across global financial assets, Bitcoin led the crypto slide.

Bitcoin price fell 1.9% over 24 hours to trade near $76,950, extending its 7-day losses to 4%. Altcoins felt equal pain, with Ethereum (ETH) slipping 1.2% to $2,265 and Solana (SOL) declining 2.5% to $92.25.

The sharp move caught overly bullish derivatives traders off guard. According to CoinGlass, $382.48 million in leveraged positions were liquidated across crypto exchanges within 24 hours.

Long positions accounted for $341.81 million of the total wipeout, with Bitcoin traders absorbing nearly $100 million in forced closures.

The recent pullback highlights underlying fragility within the crypto market. A report from CryptoQuant noted that Bitcoin’s rebound faded after reaching $78,700 on Monday, when hopes for US-Iran talks and Binance restarting withdrawals after the historic hack supported sentiment. The rally stalled below $79,000, and a later recovery attempt failed as the Nasdaq closed down 1.1%.

BTC has also slipped below the $77,140 cost basis of 1–3 month holders, which raises the risk of selling into rebounds. CryptoQuant’s Apparent Demand metric has dropped into negative territory, below its 30-day average.

CryptoQuant’s report said some Bitcoin spot buying persists but has not absorbed futures selling and liquidations. However, as U.S. midterm elections take place and Trump Iran strikes continue, the macroeconomic backdrop remains uncertain and could fuel market volatility.Key

CryptoQuant analysts see $76,600–$76,700 as key support. Bitcoin needs to reclaim $77,100–$77,200, then $77,400–$77,600 for sustained recovery. Falling liquidations and steady spot buying would help support a recovery.

However, as US midterm elections take place and Trump strikes on Iran continue, economic uncertainty remains high, leaving markets vulnerable to further volatility.

Why is crypto down? Because the Binance hack, two days of heavy ETF outflows and today’s US midterm elections hit an already weak market at once. #5275. Ripple Partner Mercado Suspends Withdrawals After Confirming 'Security Incident'

Leading Brazilian cryptocurrency exchange and Ripple blockchain payments partner Mercado Bitcoin has announced a temporary freeze on cryptocurrency deposits and withdrawals after acknowledging a security breach.

As the firm revealed in an official announcement, the security breach did not cause any harm to its users or compromise their personal data.

“We detected and quickly mitigated a security issue in one of our systems this morning,” Mercado Bitcoin said.

Mercado Bitcoin stressed that the incident did not harm any customers or lead to a data breach.

Nevertheless, as a precautionary measure, the firm decided to temporarily halt deposits and withdrawals while its security teams conduct further investigations into the issue and try to locate any assets that could have been affected.

Additionally, Mercado Bitcoin said that it is working with the appropriate authorities to investigate the incident.

In the latest update, Mercado Bitcoin revealed that it continues to work on restoring its operations to normal.

“We are still working to safely restore operations and will keep our customers updated on our official channels,” the exchange announced.

Interestingly, the company also released some cryptocurrency wallet addresses related to suspicious transactions linked to the incident.

The move aims to assist blockchain investigators and other industry players in keeping track of possibly compromised assets on different networks.

According to preliminary analysis shared by blockchain security firm Hypernative, the incident allegedly involved more than $40 million in suspicious cryptocurrency transactions across various blockchain networks.

The list includes Bitcoin (BTC), Ethereum (ETH), Solana (SOL), Tron (TRX), Polygon, Arbitrum, Avalanche, BNB Smart Chain and Stellar (XLM).

Meanwhile, Tether CEO Paolo Ardoino revealed that more than 4.5 million USDT tied to the security incident were frozen by the stablecoin issuer.

Mercado Bitcoin is known for having a strong partnership with San Francisco-based blockchain firm Ripple.

In October 2024, Ripple announced that Mercado Bitcoin, the Brazilian cryptocurrency exchange, would become the first customer in Brazil to utilize its managed end-to-end payments solution.

The partnership aimed to enable cross-border transactions and optimize treasury operations between Brazil and Portugal.

Apart from that, Ripple also revealed that the two firms planned to extend the service to enable international payments for corporate and retail users of Mercado Bitcoin.

#5274. Aave Freezes New tBTC Supply and Borrowing After Mint-and-Redeem Pause

Strict editorial policy that focuses on accuracy, relevance, and impartiality

Morbi pretium leo et nisl aliquam mollis. Quisque arcu lorem, ultricies quis pellentesque nec, ullamcorper eu odio.

TL;DR: Aave contributors said the protocol had frozen additional tBTC supply and borrowing in its Ethereum Core market after Threshold’s temporary pause of the token’s mint-and-redeem functions. It would be misleading to conclude that tBTC has lost its Bitcoin backing solely because mint and redemption were paused.

This case demonstrates why collateral quality is more complicated than an asset ticker. A wrapped token can track the price of Bitcoin under normal conditions yet carry an extra dependency on bridges, custodians and redemption operations. Lending protocols have to manage those risks separately from the volatility of the underlying coin.

Aave contributors said the protocol had frozen additional tBTC supply and borrowing in its Ethereum Core market after Threshold’s temporary pause of the token’s mint-and-redeem functions. The Aave response was activated on October 31. The restriction is specific to that asset and market; the notice did not describe a blanket freeze of unrelated Aave lending pools. The dated original disclosure is the starting point for the figures and product scope discussed here.

Wrapped Bitcoin assets represent claims or accounting mechanisms that connect Bitcoin exposure to another chain. If the mint or redemption bridge is temporarily unavailable, liquidity can change even while the token continues to trade. Lending markets react by limiting new positions that might increase risk, while allowing existing debt repayment and collateral withdrawal subject to normal account health.

An important point for borrowers is that pausing new tBTC activity does not itself change the liquidation threshold. Existing users can still face liquidation if collateral values or borrowing positions move enough to breach their health requirements. That is why a precautionary freeze and a liquidation event should not be treated as synonyms.

Decentralised lending introduces risk factors that are easy to miss if readers focus only on collateral prices. Market parameters, oracle design, token wrappers, governance powers and liquidity all affect what a user can actually do. Restrictions can reduce new exposure while leaving existing accounts sensitive to market movements. The correct interpretation starts with the exact affected pool and the functions that remain available.

This development also connects to two subjects our newsroom has been following: Aave’s first externally curated V4 lending markets and Aave’s proposed liquidation-related safeguards. They explain related market structure and should not be mistaken for evidence that every new proposal is already live.

It would be misleading to conclude that tBTC has lost its Bitcoin backing solely because mint and redemption were paused. The governance discussion describes a protective response to a specific operating condition, not a definitive finding of insolvency or fraud. The impact on an individual position depends on its collateral mix, borrowed assets and access to market liquidity.

A protocol may be automated, but its risk policy still has to be defined and maintained by people. New assets bring new dependencies; bridges and wrapped tokens add operational points of failure. Users need clear information about repayment, collateral withdrawal and liquidation conditions. Without that detail, a statement about “pausing DeFi” can be almost meaningless.

Watch Threshold’s official timeline for restoring mint and redemption, liquidity conditions for tBTC and any Aave governance proposal to amend market parameters. Users with existing positions should read the precise market notice rather than rely on headlines about a “DeFi freeze”. Clear, asset-specific communication is particularly important when markets move faster than governance procedures.

#5273. Strategy adds 811 Bitcoin as leverage falls to 3% – Room for bigger buys?

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Strategy is continuing to increase the size of its Bitcoin [BTC] treasury. This comes with the latest addition of 811 BTC during the last week of October and the first week of November.

This purchase took its total holdings to 849,327 Bitcoins. Strategy also purchased $208 million of STRC. This demonstrated additional utilization of the company’s capital structure to fund both shareholder and treasury-related activities.

It’s worth noting that Strategy still holds $4.9 billion in USD, providing liquidity for future purchases and financial obligations.

By controlling over 4% of the total supply of Bitcoin in circulation today, Strategy is limiting the number of coins available for purchase by all market participants.

While Strategy may continue to add to its existing inventory of BTC and create greater institutional demand for them. Moreover, it will also have an increasing degree of sensitivity to changes in their value due to its increased exposure.

Strategy’s declining leverage demonstrates the changing financial condition of Strategy as its Bitcoin treasury has grown. At the beginning of January 2023, the strategy’s overall leverage was approximately 108%.

As such, Strategy was then more sensitive to its obligation to repay debt compared to its current reserves.

However, by November, Strategy’s overall leverage had declined to just 3%. Even though Strategy held significantly more Bitcoin at that time, the decline in leverage is a reflection of a much healthier balance sheet on the part of Strategy.

These resources give it more room to manage debt and fund operations without immediately selling Bitcoin.

Thus, Strategy will be able to accumulate Bitcoin through the entire range of prices. This includes down markets and maintaining the demand for Bitcoin among institutions while minimizing its own exposure to price fluctuations.

Strategy has continued to develop its treasury strategy. However, safety of custody for institutional investors is a major component of increasing investor demand.

Institutional investors that have large holdings of Bitcoins need to be able to protect their investments by taking measures to avoid loss through theft or unauthorized transactions.

Many institutional investors now use cold storage systems and multi-signature wallet structures. The multi-signature system requires consent of more than two parties before any transaction can occur.

Safety and transparency of custody and reporting will be key in maintaining the confidence of institutional investors in Bitcoin. Those elements could also be used to help sustain the interest of institutional investors regardless of the current market conditions.

Strategy currently holds 849,327 BTC in its treasury, representing more than 4% of all the Bitcoins that exist.

Strategy cut leverage to 3%, but custody risks could threaten institutional demand.

#5272. NEAR Intents Head Names 3 Survival Rules for Crypto Wallets

Disclaimer: The opinions expressed by our writers are their own and do not represent the views of U.Today. The financial and market information provided on U.Today is intended for informational purposes only. U.Today is not liable for any financial losses incurred while trading cryptocurrencies. Conduct your own research by contacting financial experts before making any investment decisions. We believe that all content is accurate as of the date of publication, but certain offers mentioned may no longer be available.

The end of October 2026 was marked by the largest incident in the history of the crypto market — the $6.3 billion hack of the Binance exchange. This case exposed the vulnerability of the instant exchange (swaps) infrastructure.

While the automated systems of the North Korean group TraderTraitor withdrew $242 million via the Tron blockchain in the first two days, decentralized bridges and liquidity aggregators de facto became the main tool for legalizing the stolen funds.

Against this backdrop, the head of NEAR Intents Alex Shevchenko issued a warning to cryptocurrency wallet developers. His position is pragmatic: focusing exclusively on finding the best rate for the user turns services into unwitting accomplices in cybercrimes.

Since around $3.5 billion still remains under the hackers’ control after a wave of emergency freezes, regulatory pressure on the Web3 sector will inevitably increase. To minimize risks, the head of NEAR Intents formulated three basic survival rules for modern crypto wallets when choosing integration partners:

For wallet developers, the problem is structural. A single swap often passes through several protocols at once — an aggregator, a bridge and multiple liquidity pools — and each hop dilutes responsibility.

If any link in this chain processes tainted funds, the wallet through which the transaction was initiated shares the reputational damage and, increasingly, the legal exposure.

This is why due diligence on integration partners is shifting from an optional step to a core product requirement.

Investments in compliance and integration security are no longer a boring formality. The market has reached a point where the reputational and legal cleanliness of a service is valued far higher than saving a few cents on fees.

#5271. Hedera’s Banking Push Leaves HBAR Waiting For Demand

Home » Trendsetter News » Hedera’s Banking Push Leaves HBAR Waiting For Demand

Hedera’s banking integrations are expanding, but a rapid demand rise for HBAR remains unproven.

Hedera collects only a few hundred dollars in network fees on a typical day despite HBAR’s multibillion-dollar valuation, according to an analyst examining the gap between institutional adoption and token demand.

Fire Hustle’s central question is whether banks using Hedera will need meaningful HBAR holdings – or simply benefit from its inexpensive infrastructure.

The analyst points to Taurus integrating Hedera into a platform serving more than 40 banks and regulated institutions. That gives those clients access to HBAR custody, staking and token issuance, but access is not evidence that banks have launched products or accumulated tokens.

Hedera denominates transaction fees in dollars and collects payment in HBAR. When the token’s price rises, fewer tokens are needed to cover the same dollar-denominated charge.

That makes costs predictable for businesses. It also weakens the assumption that rising transaction counts must produce proportionately stronger token demand, particularly when fees remain small.

“Who has to hold HBAR and how much,” the analyst asks. The video identifies staking and operational token inventories as possible demand sources, although it does not establish how much HBAR institutions would need to retain.

The infrastructure argument centers on CLPR, which the video describes as technology allowing networks to verify one another’s records cryptographically without an intermediary holding funds.

According to Fire Hustle, Hashgraph contributed it to the Linux Foundation and opened an early-adopter program targeting payments, foreign exchange and collateral movement.

She highlights Swift and Wells Fargo joining the foundation on the same day as the contribution. But the qualification matters: “There’s no official announcement that Swift is using Clipper.” Shared foundation membership does not establish a deployment.

The video also places Hedera among participants in a UK tokenization initiative, citing a live on-chain repo trial targeted for April 2027 and led by BlackRock and NatWest. It does not establish that Hedera or CLPR will power that trial; other named participants include Ripple, Coinbase and Circle.

#5270. BTCPay Server 2.4.7 Security Release Adds Urgency for Self-Hosted Merchants

Strict editorial policy that focuses on accuracy, relevance, and impartiality

Morbi pretium leo et nisl aliquam mollis. Quisque arcu lorem, ultricies quis pellentesque nec, ullamcorper eu odio.

TL;DR: BTCPay Server’s 2.4.7 release published in late October includes security fixes following a broad review of reported issues. Disclosure of a serious issue is not proof that it has been exploited in the wild.

The appeal of self-hosting is genuine control over funds and infrastructure. The hidden cost is that responsibility cannot be outsourced to a hosted provider’s security team. For small merchants, having a routine patch window and recovery procedure can be more valuable than sophisticated features they never have time to maintain.

BTCPay Server’s 2.4.7 release published in late October includes security fixes following a broad review of reported issues. The project’s release materials describe findings across several severities. That makes the update relevant to businesses operating their own Bitcoin payment servers, particularly hosts that manage multiple merchants or expose administrative functions to the internet. The dated original disclosure is the starting point for the figures and product scope discussed here.

A self-hosted payment processor reduces dependence on an intermediary, but it does not remove the responsibilities of software operations. Administrators must handle updates, backups, access permissions, plugin compatibility and infrastructure monitoring. A vulnerability in payout handling or account permissions can be especially serious when a server is shared by multiple organisations.

The story is less about a dramatic exploit claim than about maintenance discipline. A security advisory gives operators something concrete to act on: identify the version running, review the release notes and apply the upgrade through a tested process. The goal is to fix known weaknesses without creating avoidable downtime or losing merchant configuration.

A crypto incident produces several separate timelines: containment, investigation, recovery and a return to reliable service. Those phases can overlap without finishing together. A traced wallet is not automatically frozen; a frozen balance is not automatically returned; and a reopened withdrawal route is not a completed security audit. Keeping those differences visible helps readers judge progress more accurately.

For additional perspective, it is worth reading our existing reporting on Binance’s earlier staged withdrawal restart and Bitcoin payment infrastructure and node software context. Those background reports help separate established facts from a new, still-developing announcement.

Disclosure of a serious issue is not proof that it has been exploited in the wild. Nor does an updated application automatically secure an outdated operating system, compromised administrator account or badly configured plugin. Teams should separate the product patch from other elements of their threat model and avoid promising complete protection after one version change.

Operational controls matter most when a business is under stress. Security announcements should identify the system affected, the customers who can act, the evidence supporting any recovery number and what remains uncertain. Clear scope protects users from false reassurance and from unnecessary panic. It also provides a sensible basis for follow-up accountability.

Operators should review the official release page for the precise upgrade guidance, create verified backups and check post-update payment and payout flows. Where merchants share infrastructure, permission boundaries deserve explicit testing. Customers should not be told payments are secure solely because a version number changed; sensible monitoring remains necessary.

#5268. Binance hack: Fake job assignment helped attackers reach wallet systems, exchange says

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Binance says a fake recruitment assignment helped attackers gain access to employee computers before the October 29 hack. Its initial findings describe a campaign that eventually reached an internal service used to authorize wallet transactions, affecting approximately $6.34 billion in assets.

The November 3 update expands Binance’s earlier account of a compromised engineer’s laptop. It also explains why suspending withdrawals failed to stop every transfer: attackers had established permissions on Ethereum that required separate action to revoke.

According to Binance, a software engineer was approached through a legitimate job-search platform in September. Someone posing as a project director arranged interviews with a supposed HR representative and the CEO of a fictitious company.

Near the end of the second interview, the engineer was asked to download a coding assignment onto a work computer.

Binance says the assignment contained malicious software that ran during setup. It replaced part of a legitimate development tool and connected to an attacker-controlled server, escaping detection by the device’s security tools at the time.

The exchange also disclosed an earlier June incident involving a tampered software installation downloaded by another engineer. Binance says it replaced that device and changed related credentials, although the incident exposed information about its wallet infrastructure team.

On October 20, the attackers used the compromised computer and an internal AI tool to gain control of two colleagues’ computers, Binance said.

They discovered a credential allowing software deployment within the wallet infrastructure. On October 29, they used the stolen access to deploy software and launch the theft.

The attackers reached Binance’s wallet-signing service, enabling transfers outside the normal withdrawal process.

On Ethereum, they also used EIP-7702, a feature allowing a wallet to delegate certain functions to software. That permission let attacker-controlled code continue moving assets after Binance suspended withdrawals.

Binance says its wallet’s security design complicated emergency revocation. The team developed a specialized procedure and cleared the delegation at 00:10 UTC on October 30.

Binance says it has rebuilt affected wallet systems and its internal AI tool, replaced compromised devices, and disabled related credentials.

It reports approximately $2.85 billion frozen or intercepted, with the amount subject to reconciliation and changing prices.

The company says it has reserved more than enough funds to cover the loss and is purchasing outstanding affected assets. Independent security firms will review its initial findings as the investigation continues.

#5267. Lisk Officially Shuts Down Blockchain After 10 Years

Long-standing blockchain project Lisk has officially ended its blockchain operations after 10 years of existence, moving on to a new financial platform for businesses.

Lisk announced on Nov. 2 the official shutdown of its blockchain after 10 years of existence. This marks the end of one of the longest-lived blockchains in the crypto world.

"Today, the Lisk Chain has officially shut down," the project said in an X post.

With the blockchain shutdown, Lisk has ended its Portal and Bridge services. The rest of the blockchain's infrastructure is also being powered down.

The blockchain shutdown marks a significant milestone in the evolution of the Lisk project, which was launched in 2016 and has seen several changes over the years.

The Lisk team, however, has noted that the shutdown of the blockchain does not spell the end of the Lisk project.

The company is transforming into a new financial platform, one that enables businesses to manage both traditional currencies and stablecoins in one place.

"For 10 years, Lisk built onchain with developers, stakers and holders that made Lisk what it is," Lisk said.

The Lisk project has made it clear that its native token, LSK, will continue to exist even as the blockchain closes shop.

According to the team, LSK will still exist on Ethereum as the loyalty token for its new business financial platform.

In addition, the project announced plans to burn approximately 5.23 million LSK tokens left in its staking rewards pool.

The total figure includes unallocated rewards to users as well as unclaimed earned rewards.

The LSK tokens are set to be burned, permanently removing them from circulation. This will also lower the overall supply of the cryptocurrency.

This comes after a large burn of LSK tokens in September. At the time, Lisk burned 100 million LSK tokens after making the decision to wind down its decentralized autonomous organization (DAO) through a governance vote.

The burn event reduced the LSK token's supply from 400 million to 300 million LSK.

Lisk intends to introduce new use cases for LSK in 2027. This will allow businesses to earn rewards in the token and use LSK for platform fees.

Home » Exchanges » Binance News » Binance Draws $2.3 Billion Inflows After Record $6.3 Billion Hack

Binance’s trading volume orchestrates a rapid comeback as $2.3 billion inflows hit the recovering platform.

Binance has swung back to net inflows of roughly $2.3 billion over 24 hours after beginning to restore customer access following a security breach estimated at roughly $6.3 billion, according to market-tracking data. The reversal came after a period of heavy withdrawals and reflects an early, if incomplete, return of confidence in the exchange.

The incident is being described as the largest theft in crypto’s history. Binance said its initial investigation found that a compromised laptop used by a DevOps engineer enabled unauthorized withdrawals of Bitcoin, Ether and other assets across multiple networks.

Efforts to contain the stolen assets have focused on tracing wallets and blocking transfers across the industry. Estimates of the amount frozen vary, but reports indicate that between roughly $2.7 billion and $3 billion of the funds were stopped or restricted shortly after the hacking incident.

JUST IN: 🚨 Binance net inflows flip positive as market activity surges.The exchange records a massive $2.3 billion in net inflows amid expanding user withdrawals.$BNB #Binance #Crypto

Ripple also said it froze $200 million in RLUSD connected to wallets linked to the suspected exploit. The intervention highlighted the role that issuers of centrally administered stablecoins can play in limiting the movement of funds after a major breach.

Binance has said it will cover customer losses. The exchange’s reopening of withdrawals appears to have been the critical test: hourly deposits began exceeding withdrawals as access expanded, and Binance led centralized exchanges in net inflows during the latest 24-hour period.

BNB sold off sharply after the hack, at one point falling about 8.7% within an hour. The token was hit harder than several other large cryptocurrencies because of its direct connection to the Binance ecosystem, even though the reported exploit did not target BNB Chain itself.

Futures positioning also turned more defensive, with funding rates slipping negative as traders increased short exposure. BNB later found support near its 200-day moving average, but it remains below pre-hack levels and faces resistance around the $733 area cited by technical analysts.

The broader crypto market has stayed under pressure, with Bitcoin near $77,000 and several major altcoins recording steeper monthly declines. Risk appetite has been strained by exchange-traded fund outflows, geopolitical concerns and uncertainty around monetary policy, magnifying the shock from the Binance breach.

#5265. NYSE Adds Four Transfer Agents to Planned Tokenized Securities Venue

Strict editorial policy that focuses on accuracy, relevance, and impartiality

Morbi pretium leo et nisl aliquam mollis. Quisque arcu lorem, ultricies quis pellentesque nec, ullamcorper eu odio.

TL;DR: The New York Stock Exchange said on November 2 that Broadridge, Computershare, Equiniti and Superstate had joined the network supporting its proposed tokenized securities venue. There is a major distinction between an issuer-sponsored tokenized security and a synthetic token that tracks a stock price without representing an actual company share.

Broadridge separately described plans to provide on-chain governance services, underlining how much of a securities market happens after a trade. Corporate records have to survive splits, dividends, shareholder meetings and ownership disputes. If tokenization ignores those workflows, its speed gains may produce new headaches instead of an easier market.

The New York Stock Exchange said on November 2 that Broadridge, Computershare, Equiniti and Superstate had joined the network supporting its proposed tokenized securities venue. They add to previously announced relationships with Securitize and tZERO. The planned platform is not being presented as a fully operating public marketplace today; a launch depends on further steps, including regulatory approval. The dated original disclosure is the starting point for the figures and product scope discussed here.

Transfer agents do the often-invisible work of maintaining official shareholder records and supporting issuer functions. Their participation matters because a token representing a security needs clear ownership, corporate-action handling and investor rights as well as a way to change hands. Putting a token on a ledger does not automatically deliver proxy voting, dividends, reconciliations or legal recognition.

The news shifts tokenization from a discussion of trading speed toward institutional plumbing. The NYSE says the partners collectively have extensive issuer relationships, which could simplify future adoption if product designs are approved. It also suggests that incumbent infrastructure firms are not being bypassed; they are being brought into a possible new recordkeeping and settlement model.

A tokenized instrument must connect technical records with enforceable financial rights. Trading software can move a token quickly, but an issuer, custodian, transfer agent or settlement bank may still have to recognise the result. This is why tokenization announcements need to explain which part of the lifecycle is changing, not merely advertise that a blockchain is involved.

This development also connects to two subjects our newsroom has been following: DTCC’s tokenized securities infrastructure rollout and tokenized fund redemptions tested over a weekend. Those background reports help separate established facts from a new, still-developing announcement.

There is a major distinction between an issuer-sponsored tokenized security and a synthetic token that tracks a stock price without representing an actual company share. The NYSE announcement concerns a planned structure involving regulated participants. It does not establish that a retail customer can buy every listed stock as a 24-hour token immediately or that shareholder rights will be identical under every arrangement.

Real settlement infrastructure is judged by whether it works on bad days, not just during presentations. Reconciliation, asset eligibility, funding, corporate actions and recovery from errors remain central. Continuous technical operation can improve coordination, yet it does not create market liquidity or remove the obligations of regulated participants.

Watch for rule filings, regulatory decisions, supported asset classes, custody standards and the precise rights attached to tokenized shares. Operational details about voting and corporate actions will be as important as the underlying ledger. Until those details emerge, the partnerships demonstrate preparation and potential reach, not completed migration of Wall Street equity trading onto blockchains.

It is important to note that even though the indicator approached oversold territory, the bulls were yet to demonstrate a convincing price reversal.

For now, sellers retained control, leaving the $0.0600 support as the decisive level for XPL’s next move.

#5262. Hong Kong Launches Post-Quantum Preparation Toolkit for Banks

Strict editorial policy that focuses on accuracy, relevance, and impartiality

Morbi pretium leo et nisl aliquam mollis. Quisque arcu lorem, ultricies quis pellentesque nec, ullamcorper eu odio.

TL;DR: The Hong Kong Monetary Authority and the Hong Kong University of Science and Technology announced a post-quantum cryptography assessment toolkit on November 2. Post-quantum migration is not the same thing as making every blockchain or bank system instantly quantum-secure.

Good security planning is often unglamorous. Discovering where keys are issued, which partners rely on them and when equipment will be replaced is difficult work, but it makes future transitions possible. The banking sector has a reason to start early because data captured today can remain sensitive long after the original connection has ended.

The Hong Kong Monetary Authority and the Hong Kong University of Science and Technology announced a post-quantum cryptography assessment toolkit on November 2. The initiative is aimed at helping banks understand where current cryptographic systems might need upgrades as quantum capabilities develop. It is a preparation programme, not a declaration that quantum computers have broken banking or cryptocurrency encryption. The dated original disclosure is the starting point for the figures and product scope discussed here.

Large organisations use cryptography in certificates, customer authentication, interbank links, software updates and the storage of sensitive data. Migrating those systems can take years because algorithms, devices, vendors and legacy applications must remain interoperable. A toolkit can help identify dependencies, prioritise sensitive data and test readiness for future standards before a forced emergency transition.

The HKMA has pointed to gaps in sector readiness and ambitions to improve preparedness by 2030. The most immediate benefit may be governance: teams can assign ownership of the inventory and agree which systems require the longest lead time. That is more useful than debating a speculative date on which a general-purpose quantum computer could break a particular cryptographic scheme.

A crypto incident produces several separate timelines: containment, investigation, recovery and a return to reliable service. Those phases can overlap without finishing together. A traced wallet is not automatically frozen; a frozen balance is not automatically returned; and a reopened withdrawal route is not a completed security audit. Keeping those differences visible helps readers judge progress more accurately.

For additional perspective, it is worth reading our existing reporting on Binance’s earlier staged withdrawal restart and Bitcoin payment infrastructure and node software context. Those background reports help separate established facts from a new, still-developing announcement.

Post-quantum migration is not the same thing as making every blockchain or bank system instantly quantum-secure. Different primitives face different levels of risk, and some proposed methods affect key size, verification performance or compatibility. The toolkit’s output is an assessment input, not a substitute for independent engineering and formal testing.

Operational controls matter most when a business is under stress. Security announcements should identify the system affected, the customers who can act, the evidence supporting any recovery number and what remains uncertain. Clear scope protects users from false reassurance and from unnecessary panic. It also provides a sensible basis for follow-up accountability.

Watch whether participating institutions publish migration road maps, begin procurement changes and test post-quantum authentication with core systems. For crypto readers, it is a reminder to separate near-term operational security from longer-term cryptographic research. No credible reading of the announcement implies that Bitcoin private keys are currently being routinely cracked by quantum machines.

#5261. Binance Restores Withdrawals for Ripple's RLUSD Stablecoin

Cryptocurrency exchange Binance has fully enabled withdrawals for Ripple’s RLUSD stablecoin on the Ethereum network. This move is part of the exchange's gradual service recovery process after the massive $6.3 billion hack.

On Nov. 3, the exchange released an update on the next stage of its withdrawal restoration plan, with a new list of cryptocurrencies available for withdrawal.

Interestingly, the new list of assets includes Ripple’s US dollar-backed stablecoin RLUSD and a number of other stablecoins.

This announcement follows Binance’s gradual restoration of withdrawals, which began on Nov. 1 after a brief halt due to one of the largest hacks in the crypto industry.

As per the latest recovery update from Binance, the exchange began its second significant withdrawal restoration phase at 04:00 UTC on Nov. 3.

Apart from RLUSD, the cryptocurrency exchange is now gradually re-enabling withdrawals for dozens of cryptocurrencies on multiple blockchain networks, including Ethereum, BNB Smart Chain, Solana, XRP Ledger, Sui, Arbitrum, Optimism, Tron and Base.

For Ethereum, the list of cryptocurrencies now available for withdrawal includes Shiba Inu (SHIB), Chainlink (LINK), Worldcoin (WLD), Ondo (ONDO) and Ethena (ENA).

At the same time, the cryptocurrency exchange has also added XRP and a number of other top crypto assets to its list of withdrawals that will soon be re-enabled on BNB Smart Chain.

The crypto exchange emphasized that not all of the listed assets will be enabled for withdrawal at once. Instead, each asset and network combination will be re-enabled individually after the necessary security and operational checks are completed.

“We will gradually re-enable more assets & networks in stages once we complete the relevant security checks,” Binance said.

At the same time, the crypto exchange also warned that there may be delays due to traffic during this restoration process. Meanwhile, cryptocurrency deposits remain functional.

Initially, Binance started re-enabling withdrawals on Nov. 1 for top cryptocurrencies, including Bitcoin (BTC), Ethereum (ETH), XRP, Solana (SOL), Dogecoin (DOGE) and Zcash (ZEC), as well as major stablecoins USDT and USDC.

The latest withdrawal restoration update comes only a few days after the cryptocurrency exchange suffered a massive hack on Oct. 29, which resulted in the theft of about $6.3 billion in crypto.

The FBI officially identified the perpetrators of the hack as North Korean hackers linked to the infamous TraderTraitor operation on Oct. 31.

The U.S. agency revealed that hackers have been converting stolen cryptocurrencies mostly into Ethereum as a potential path toward laundering the assets through decentralized finance platforms and crypto swap services.

More withdrawal restorations will follow as Binance moves forward with its security checks and recovery processes.

#5260. Ethereum for banks: What ETHGas’s advance transaction bookings offer

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

ETHGas is selling banks and trading firms the possibility to reserve space in an Ethereum block beforehand. Its Institutional Blockspace offer aims to fulfil customer wishes on the choice of validators, as they settlet transactions on public network.

Ethereum handles its transactions in blocks. Each block has a limited capacity, and users pay fees to place their transactions.

The ETHGas facility includes a marketplace where purchasers can commit to purchasing processing capacity in the future [i.e., book a place in a future batch].

Its institutional offering will add controls for regulated businesses, including selecting eligible validators and screening for sanctions compliance. ETHGas sells it as way for institutions to “use public Ethereum with additional controls”.

It may help firms schedule their money transfers and trading activities if they can use such information. Furthermore, its economic value is based on whether the operator sticks to its promises.

ETHGas needs validators willing to sell their future capacity.

In April, the company signed up  ether.fi , which committed to staking approx. $3B worth of ETH on its High Performance Staking Service for three years and gaining exclusive use of ETHGas’s preconfirmation platform during the term.

Buyer protection remains an important question.

The ETHGas documentation shows that some missed inclusion commitments will be compensated, but claims protection related to builder-initiated transactions does not extend to transactions sent directly.

Even when time and bandwidth are equal, contract details are still relevant to prospective clients.

Information on delivery obligations, compensation terms, and performance records would help those who want to use it see and decide if they see advance subscriptions as something that has value for them and can address their demands.

#5259. After 24 Million Launches, Just 312 Memecoins Are Above $100,000

Home » Altcoins » After 24 Million Launches, Just 312 Memecoins Are Above $100,000

The rarest breed of meme currencies launched on Pump.Fun stay above a $100,000 market capitalization.

Pump.fun’s explosive memecoin-creation activity is colliding with a stark outcome: of roughly 24 million launches on the platform, only 312 tokens have climbed above a $100,000 valuation threshold. The gap has sharpened scrutiny of how much lasting value the memecoin launchpad is producing.

The numbers underscore the extreme attrition built into rapid-fire token launches. Vast volumes of new memecoins can create attention and trading bursts, but only a tiny fraction appear to develop enough liquidity, demand or staying power to clear even relatively modest valuation levels.

Pump.fun has become closely associated with the low-cost, high-speed creation of memecoins. That model can lower barriers for creators and traders, while also producing an overwhelming supply of projects competing for the same pool of capital.

JUST IN: THERE HAVE BEEN 24.4M COINS LAUNCHED ON PUMPFUN312 ARE CURRENTLY ABOVE $100K MARKET CAP https://t.co/t0Qvv6Oqj3

With only 312 tokens reportedly surpassing $100,000 out of 24 million launches, the implied success rate is exceptionally small. The figure does not by itself establish whether individual launches were designed for long-term development, short-term trading or experimentation, but it highlights how quickly most tokens fade from view.

The DeFi platform is now facing broader questions about whether transaction activity and launch counts translate into a durable business model. High turnover may generate engagement, yet it can also leave traders exposed to thin liquidity and sharp price reversals once early interest disappears.

Concerns around memecoins have been amplified by the weak performance of PUMP amid softer market sentiment. When a platform-linked token struggles during a risk-off period, investors tend to look more closely at the underlying economics: user retention, trading demand and the quality of projects emerging from the ecosystem.

Pump.fun’s figures offer a blunt reminder for the wider crypto market: token creation is easy, but building liquidity and relevance is not. Investors weighing exposure to launchpad ecosystems will likely focus less on raw volume and more on whether those platforms can consistently produce assets that survive beyond the first burst of speculation.

#5258. Coinbase Data Shows XRP May See 8.7% Price Move

Options market data for cryptocurrencies published by major US crypto exchange Coinbase indicates that a big (approximately 8.7%) price swing for XRP could be expected by Nov. 8.

At the time of writing, XRP and Solana are tied in terms of the biggest expected price move (according to options-implied volatility), both showing an 8.7% expected move.

Ethereum (ETH) has an expected 7.7% move. Meanwhile, Bitcoin (BTC) is the asset with the lowest expected price move (according to implied volatility) among the top four assets (5.8%).

It is important to note that the above numbers do not necessarily mean that a big upward move in the price of XRP is expected.

They only mean that options traders are expecting significant moves (up or down).

According to Coinbase data, an 8.7% price move (up or down) could be possible within the specified timeframe.

The current price of XRP is around $1.25.

An 8.7% move up would mean the price of XRP could reach around $1.36.

An 8.7% move down would mean the price of XRP could reach around $1.14.

That would mean the implied price range for XRP is around 1.14–1.36.

It is worth noting that Coinbase's numbers are based on "implied volatility," which is a measure based on options prices that reflects the market's expectations for future price swings.

Coinbase is referring to a "one standard deviation" or 1σ move. In the typical normal distribution context, this means there's about a 68% chance that the asset will end up in the specified range.

That does not mean XRP will necessarily be in that price range, nor that there's a 50/50 chance of an 8.7% rally or fall.

What it means is that the options market provides an estimate of the size of the expected move, but it does not necessarily indicate the direction of the move.
