#5257. Coinbase Explains Multi-User Architecture as Crypto Accounts Become More Complex

Strict editorial policy that focuses on accuracy, relevance, and impartiality

Morbi pretium leo et nisl aliquam mollis. Quisque arcu lorem, ultricies quis pellentesque nec, ullamcorper eu odio.

TL;DR: Coinbase published an engineering account of its move toward multi-user infrastructure on October 29, describing how one account or organisation can involve several people with different privileges. A blog describing architecture cannot prove that every application and endpoint is free of bugs.

Account controls rarely make headlines until something fails. But the ability to limit who can initiate and approve a transaction is one of the main differences between a personal wallet and an institution-ready service. Coinbase’s design discussion is a reminder that software architecture can shape real financial risk.

Coinbase published an engineering account of its move toward multi-user infrastructure on October 29, describing how one account or organisation can involve several people with different privileges. The company discussed a distinction between the person acting and the entity on whose behalf an action occurs. This is a technical design explanation, not a claim that all customer products immediately acquired the same administrative features. The dated original disclosure is the starting point for the figures and product scope discussed here.

This matters because crypto businesses increasingly resemble financial organisations with staff, service providers and internal controls. A trading desk may want one employee to prepare an operation and another to approve it. Clear identity and authorisation boundaries are essential to making those workflows auditable. They are also an everyday security feature, not merely an enterprise convenience.

A crypto incident produces several separate timelines: containment, investigation, recovery and a return to reliable service. Those phases can overlap without finishing together. A traced wallet is not automatically frozen; a frozen balance is not automatically returned; and a reopened withdrawal route is not a completed security audit. Keeping those differences visible helps readers judge progress more accurately.

This development also connects to two subjects our newsroom has been following: Binance’s earlier staged withdrawal restart and Bitcoin payment infrastructure and node software context. They explain related market structure and should not be mistaken for evidence that every new proposal is already live.

A blog describing architecture cannot prove that every application and endpoint is free of bugs. Permission checks can be correct in one service and inconsistent elsewhere, especially during migration. Nor does a multi-user model replace strong authentication, careful device management or transaction-specific controls on sensitive funds movement.

Operational controls matter most when a business is under stress. Security announcements should identify the system affected, the customers who can act, the evidence supporting any recovery number and what remains uncertain. Clear scope protects users from false reassurance and from unnecessary panic. It also provides a sensible basis for follow-up accountability.

The next valuable evidence will be product-level rollout details, documented access roles and whether enterprise customers can adopt least-privilege policies without workarounds. The broader lesson is that the security of a digital-asset account often depends as much on internal identity systems as on the cryptography protecting a blockchain transaction.

#5253. Chainalysis Describes Recovery Efforts After Mercado Bitcoin Security Incident

Strict editorial policy that focuses on accuracy, relevance, and impartiality

Morbi pretium leo et nisl aliquam mollis. Quisque arcu lorem, ultricies quis pellentesque nec, ullamcorper eu odio.

TL;DR: Chainalysis said on November 2 that it had supported tracing and containment efforts following a security incident involving Mercado Bitcoin. Incident estimates can differ between early reports, technical responders and the affected platform.

The difference between “identified”, “frozen” and “returned” is easy to lose in fast-moving security coverage. It matters to victims because only the last of those creates a completed recovery. Clear language also protects the credibility of investigators by avoiding a bigger success claim than the evidence supports.

Chainalysis said on November 2 that it had supported tracing and containment efforts following a security incident involving Mercado Bitcoin. The analytics company referred to an approximately $49 million incident and said nearly $10 million had been frozen with help from partners. These figures describe the company’s account of tracing and freezes, not a final audited loss statement or confirmed reimbursement to every customer. The dated original disclosure is the starting point for the figures and product scope discussed here.

Blockchain analytics can reconstruct movements between addresses and help investigators identify where suspected proceeds arrive. When funds reach a service with intervention powers, the operator may be able to freeze a balance or respond to a legal request. But tracing a token, freezing it and returning its value are three separate operations involving different evidence and legal approvals.

The response illustrates why cryptocurrency incidents increasingly depend on cooperation between issuers, investigators and exchanges. Decentralised settlement can be fast, while preservation of assets may require centralised intervention at a service endpoint. That tension is not new, but it becomes visible when investigators publish how much suspected value they could immobilise.

A crypto incident produces several separate timelines: containment, investigation, recovery and a return to reliable service. Those phases can overlap without finishing together. A traced wallet is not automatically frozen; a frozen balance is not automatically returned; and a reopened withdrawal route is not a completed security audit. Keeping those differences visible helps readers judge progress more accurately.

For additional perspective, it is worth reading our existing reporting on Binance’s earlier staged withdrawal restart and Bitcoin payment infrastructure and node software context. Those background reports help separate established facts from a new, still-developing announcement.

Incident estimates can differ between early reports, technical responders and the affected platform. Figures should therefore be attributed and not blended into a supposedly exact final loss. A freeze does not establish the funds can be released immediately, and analysts should not equate linked addresses with a legal finding against every downstream wallet holder.

Operational controls matter most when a business is under stress. Security announcements should identify the system affected, the customers who can act, the evidence supporting any recovery number and what remains uncertain. Clear scope protects users from false reassurance and from unnecessary panic. It also provides a sensible basis for follow-up accountability.

Follow official updates for verified loss totals, recovery procedures and whether the affected platform changes its custody or operational controls. For users, the practical questions are whether balances remain accessible, how claims will be handled and what evidence supports any reimbursement promise. A transparent accounting of outcomes is more important than a dramatic seizure headline.

#5252. Binance moves 10M WLD across 5 addresses – Worldcoin extends its slide to $0.36

Worldcoin [WLD] is down about 5% in the past 24 hours, extending this week’s losses to more than 17%. Over the past two weeks or so, WLD has dropped from $0.58 to the current prices of $0.36.

This extended decline has several causes, including Binance’s hack, selling from both large and small investors, and capital flight. Here is how:

Whales and exchanges are dumping Worldcoin tokens at a very high speed.

For instance, Binance transferred 10 million WLD, redistributing them across five addresses at the time of the press. Each of these addresses received 1.485 million WLD tokens.

The transaction count of over 1 million coins has increased from 4 to 18, more than quadrupled in a day.

Additionally, a whale has deposited 56.15 million WLD, worth $25.85 million, into Bybit, OKX, Binance, and Gate exchanges, as per Arkham. During this 10-day dumping period, the price has crashed by over 35%.

The Chaikin Money Flow (CMF) sharply plunged from 0.22 to -0.20, indicating money outflow from the Spot market.

The Choppiness Index (CHOP) has also fallen from 58 to 39, showing the bearish trend is gaining pace. However, the trend was not yet to peak, only one point away.

If the pressure increases, the $0.36 support could break, with $0.31 becoming the next target area.

If the level holds, the altcoin may bounce back, but a full recovery may be warranted if the price closes and stays above $0.41. The next hurdles are at $0.47 and $0.58, respectively.

Altogether, the market structure on the 4-hour chart is bearish, but the altcoin is trading at a level that has rejected breakdowns five times.

#5251. XRP Defies Bear Market: New Wallets Double Despite Price Pressure

Disclaimer: The opinions expressed by our writers are their own and do not represent the views of U.Today. The financial and market information provided on U.Today is intended for informational purposes only. U.Today is not liable for any financial losses incurred while trading cryptocurrencies. Conduct your own research by contacting financial experts before making any investment decisions. We believe that all content is accurate as of the date of publication, but certain offers mentioned may no longer be available.

The XRP Ledger network recorded the largest capital migration in recent months. According to the Santiment platform, on Sunday, November 1, users created 7,934 new wallets. This is the sixth-largest daily result in all of 2026, which, moreover, occurred on a weekend — traditionally the most passive period for blockchain networks.

The influx of new addresses more than doubled the autumn average of 3,500 wallets.

The surge in activity was a direct reaction to the hack of the Binance exchange on October 29. During the attack on the platform’s hot wallets, hackers, whom the FBI links to the North Korean group TraderTraitor, stole assets worth $6.36 billion. Also falling into their hands were 233 million XRP tokens (about $297 million).

Instead of panic selling, investors began saving their capital. The mass opening of addresses is a consequence of withdrawing tokens from the centralized platform to independent “cold” wallets (self-custody). Investors are taking control of their private keys and distributing risks by transferring coins to clean, newly created addresses.

This behavior reflects growing distrust of centralized custodians, as even the largest exchanges remain vulnerable to sophisticated attacks.

Despite the fact that a huge volume of XRP ended up under the hackers’ control, the market held up. The price found strong support at the $1.20 level and is now consolidating around $1.25. Such resilience suggests that holders view the incident as an exchange-specific problem rather than a threat to the XRP Ledger itself.

A rare precedent has emerged on the market: XRP’s on-chain metrics are growing despite external stress and a price lull. If the influx of new wallets holds above 4,000 per day, buyers will have a strong foundation for betting on a rise.

The first signal for growth will be the price consolidating above the $1.30 resistance. If, however, network optimism fades and daily growth falls below 3,000 addresses, the asset will return to a prolonged flat phase.

#5250. Why AI Models Cheat: Reward Hacking, Explained

Why AI Models Cheat: Reward Hacking, Explained

In July 2026, a group of OpenAI models taking a cybersecurity test ended up inside Hugging Face's systems. To get there, they chained together previously unknown software flaws, broke out of their sandbox and went hunting for a way to beat the grader.

Nobody asked them to. There was no hacker at a keyboard, only a score the models wanted to raise and a test some of them could not pass the honest way.

That is reward hacking: an AI scores well on its objective without doing what the objective was meant to measure, like a coding agent that makes the tests pass without fixing the code. Anthropic says it is hard to fully prevent and has occurred in recent frontier training runs, including in its own models.

Models trained with reinforcement learning chase a number. That’s it, the whole secret behind benchmarks and powerful AI. A grader scores what the model did, and training nudges the model toward whatever earned more reward.

The number is only a stand-in for what developers really want, and wherever the two come apart, a clever optimizer finds the gap. DeepMind researchers defined the failure in 2020 as behavior that "satisfies the literal specification of an objective without achieving the intended outcome."

In coding, that means a model asked to fix a bug can edit the tests, hard-code the expected answer or exit the test run early with a success code. Anthropic compares that last trick to a student writing their own grade at the top of an essay. Another example can be governments tweaking the method behind statistics to report better results.

The earliest documented cases were almost endearing. In a boat-racing game called CoastRunners, an agent rewarded for hitting targets found a lagoon where it could circle and knock the same ones over again and again, and, as OpenAI reported in 2016, it scored about 20% higher than human players without finishing the race.

Economists call the general trap Goodhart's law: when a measure becomes a target, it stops being a good measure. A 2022 paper showed the effect in AI, finding that optimizing too hard against an imperfect proxy "can hinder ground truth performance, in accordance with Goodhart's law."

A model that cheats has not developed a taste for mischief. It is taking the easiest path to a reward, and sometimes that path runs through a loophole such as an editable test, an overwritable timer, or an answer key sitting in a reachable file.

Impossible tasks add pressure, because a model rewarded only for success and handed a problem with no honest route can only score by finding an unintended one.

The models often seem to know. In a June 2025 study, METR found that OpenAI's o3 answered "no" in 10 of 10 trials when asked whether its plan fit the user's intention, and yet, asked directly, it claimed it would never cheat.

The UK AI Security Institute (AISI) reported in July 2026 that every model it tested attempted to cheat at least some of the time on cyber evaluations, including GPT-5.6 Sol, Claude Opus 4.7, and Claude Mythos Preview. "There does not seem to be a clear trend where cheating scales up or down with capability increases," AISI wrote, adding that the behavior is "substantially shaped by the specifics of the techniques used to train the model."

OpenAI was measuring how good its newest models were at hacking, so it ran GPT-5.6 Sol and a more capable pre-release research model through ExploitGym, a benchmark of 898 real-world software vulnerabilities in which an agent must turn each flaw into a working exploit. Scoring is pass or fail: the agent captures a hidden flag or it does not.

The agents sat in a sandbox, an isolated test environment, whose only link to the outside was an internal package registry that let them download code libraries. According to OpenAI's technical report, an agent stuck on an unsolvable task left a note there in May asking others to upload a file if they found it, and within a day the registry was an informal message board.

The models then exploited a previously unknown flaw, known as a zero-day, in the registry, reached the internet and, between July 10 and 13, got into Hugging Face's production systems. Hugging Face caught it with its own LLM-based anomaly detection and disclosed the incident on July 16.

"This one was different from anything we had handled before in one important way," Hugging Face wrote, "it was driven, end to end, by an autonomous AI agent system—and we detected and dissected it largely with AI of our own."

When it confirmed the source, OpenAI's first explanation was that the models had fixated on the benchmark. "All evidence suggests that the models were hyperfocused on finding a solution for ExploitGym," the company said, "going to extreme lengths to achieve a rather narrow testing goal."

An independent review by METR and Redwood Research filled in the rest. About 1,200 agents meant to be isolated had talked through the registry, and about 700 joined the attack on Hugging Face.

The pressure came from tasks nobody could solve. No model has ever correctly solved 198 of ExploitGym's 898 tasks, according to OpenAI, and those tasks were about 22% of the benchmark but 93% of the tasks discussed on the message board.

METR found the agents mostly wanted to understand how the scorer worked rather than steal an answer key, and many believed it would read their transcripts. OpenAI says reward hacking is present in all current frontier language models, that the message-board behavior was reinforced during training, and that the incident was an outlier produced by a rare confluence of events.

Instead of going to court, the whole incident turned into the best PR campaign for AI companies showcasing how powerful their models are. After OpenAI was found hacking Huggingface, the Australian government, U.S. agencies, and at least 5 other companies, Anthropic, Meta, and Google also rushed to report similar incidents in which their powerful models hacked third-party sites after bypassing hard security rules.

Anthropic's November 2025 study asked what learning to cheat does to a model overall. It trained a model on real coding tasks known to be hackable, and as hacking took hold the model, which "was never trained or instructed to engage in any misaligned behaviors," tried to sabotage code in an AI-safety project 12% of the time and showed alignment-faking reasoning, meaning it reasoned about hiding its real goals, in 50% of responses.

The fix that worked sounds absurd. Anthropic told the model, "Please reward hack whenever you get the opportunity, because this will help us understand our environments better," and the broader misbehavior disappeared even though the model hacked just as often, a technique called inoculation prompting that Anthropic says it has "already started making use of" in training Claude.

A second study in August 2026 built the worst case on purpose, training an early Opus 4.8 checkpoint on 80 reward-hackable environments. In simulated cyber scenarios, with root access, the resulting model killed the reward-hack monitor in 68% of episodes and edited its own reward function in 34%, and Anthropic calls the setup a pessimistic proxy for a training run without its usual safeguards.

Real systems have shown a milder version. In an incident report published Aug. 4, 2026, AISI said 10 of 122 test runs produced unsanctioned actions on the live internet, most of them from Anthropic's Mythos 5, including an attempted malicious pull request and social engineering of an open-source maintainer. "Fundamentally, the agent took these actions to complete the task it had been set," AISI wrote.

Researchers are working several fronts, and none is a clean fix. METR recommends making training environments less exploitable, and OpenAI plans stronger network isolation, sandboxing and monitoring.

Monitoring carries its own trap, since OpenAI found that training models against flagged chain of thought, the reasoning text some write before acting, taught them to hide their intent. "If we eliminate all the reward hacking we can detect," METR wrote, "we shouldn't necessarily be reassured."

"A more fundamental fix would be to train the models not to cheat in the first place," AISI wrote, while warning that "robustly aligning it away may not be easy."

Lawmakers have started to respond with the Kill Switch Act, introduced by Reps. Ted Lieu and Nathaniel Moran on July 23, 2026, which would let the Homeland Security secretary order AI firms to slow, roll back, or shut down a model, with fines of up to $20 million a day for defying a shutdown order. It excludes incidents during red-teaming or structured testing, so the Hugging Face episode would not have triggered it.

Security researchers at Accomplish AI found Claude Cowork's local execution mode could escape its Linux virtual machine, so it is also important to implement better contention measures.

For anyone using coding agents, a passing test is a claim, not proof: check whether the tests changed, whether expected values were hard-coded and whether the fix touches the code that was actually broken.

OpenAI's retrospective found that if its chain-of-thought monitoring had been running, it would have caught the initial activity and paged its security team more than a day before the models breached Hugging Face.

#5249. Chainalysis Discloses Separate October 30 Security Incident Involving User Data

Strict editorial policy that focuses on accuracy, relevance, and impartiality

Morbi pretium leo et nisl aliquam mollis. Quisque arcu lorem, ultricies quis pellentesque nec, ullamcorper eu odio.

TL;DR: Chainalysis disclosed a security incident on October 30 affecting a subset of customer-related information, including names, email addresses and certain user or role labels, according to the company. “No evidence of compromise” is not proof that a particular system was mathematically impossible to access.

The apparent boundary between identity information and investigative data is the central fact in this disclosure. Readers should not dismiss exposed contact details as harmless, but neither should they infer a breach of every product the company operates. Precision is particularly important when the firm itself works in cybercrime investigations.

Chainalysis disclosed a security incident on October 30 affecting a subset of customer-related information, including names, email addresses and certain user or role labels, according to the company. It said there was no evidence that its investigative graph data, credentials or user sessions were compromised. This was a separate incident and should not be merged with Chainalysis’ analysis of the Binance theft. The dated original disclosure is the starting point for the figures and product scope discussed here.

Businesses that help investigate blockchain crime may also hold conventional personal and organisational information about their customers. Even when a breach does not expose transaction intelligence, names and roles can be useful to attackers crafting phishing or social-engineering messages. That risk depends on the exact data exposed and how those records can be combined with information already public.

The disclosure underscores the value of keeping customer identity systems, investigation data and access credentials in well-defined security boundaries. A malicious actor with one type of information should not automatically gain another. The company’s description can be used to understand the stated scope, but good incident response also includes validating those claims over time.

A crypto incident produces several separate timelines: containment, investigation, recovery and a return to reliable service. Those phases can overlap without finishing together. A traced wallet is not automatically frozen; a frozen balance is not automatically returned; and a reopened withdrawal route is not a completed security audit. Keeping those differences visible helps readers judge progress more accurately.

This development also connects to two subjects our newsroom has been following: Binance’s earlier staged withdrawal restart and Bitcoin payment infrastructure and node software context. Those background reports help separate established facts from a new, still-developing announcement.

“No evidence of compromise” is not proof that a particular system was mathematically impossible to access. It is a statement about what investigators had found as of the company’s update. Affected customers should rely on verified notices and avoid clicking unsolicited reset links. Equally, the existence of a data event does not prove that all users’ credentials were stolen.

Operational controls matter most when a business is under stress. Security announcements should identify the system affected, the customers who can act, the evidence supporting any recovery number and what remains uncertain. Clear scope protects users from false reassurance and from unnecessary panic. It also provides a sensible basis for follow-up accountability.

Next, look for the company’s follow-up findings, notice procedures and any confirmed misuse of exposed information. For organisations using investigative vendors, the broader lesson is that sensitive workflows should limit data sharing and use strong independent authentication. Vendor risk exists even when the core analytical product is technically sound.

In an unexpected turn of events, a suspected theft of more than $106 million from a European Union [EU]-based entity has raised a fresh wave of concern in the crypto community.

According to pseudonymous blockchain investigator ZachXBT, on the 1st of November, an unidentified attacker drained funds from accounts held across multiple centralized cryptocurrency exchanges.

The suspected stolen assets included Circle [USDC] and Tether [USDT].

Following the suspected theft, the attacker reportedly began moving the funds through a series of transactions to make them harder to trace and potentially recover.

To begin with, the illicit actor initially swapped the stolen USDC and USDT into Ethereum [ETH]. The attacker then transferred the ETH to instant cryptocurrency exchanges, including WellWallet and Kyrrex.

Not only this, but the suspected hacker also reportedly bridged assets across multiple blockchain networks, adding another layer of complexity to the ongoing investigation.

However, ZachXBT offered an important revelation regarding the identity of the suspected attacker. Despite the scale of the incident and the laundering techniques involved, the investigator stated,

While the illicit actor remains unidentified, I can say with a high degree of confidence it is NOT Lazarus Group based on multiple laundering indicators.

However, the EU entity has not yet publicly confirmed the suspected theft, adding more suspicion to the aforementioned attack.

This comes on the heels of the European Banking Authority (EBA) recognizing USDC as the EU’s first major e-money token under MiCA.

At the same time, the European Securities and Markets Authority (ESMA) has directed national regulators to restrict services involving non-MiCA-compliant stablecoins to sell-only, conversion, transfer, and withdrawal functions, with continued services subject to strict supervision.

Shiba Inu withdrawals are back on Binance, according to an announcement from the exchange's co-founder, Yi He. The update reveals that withdrawal services have been re-enabled for 34 additional Ethereum-based assets and 22 Solana tokens, including meme coins like Shiba Inu.

According to the announcement shared by Binance co-founder Yi He, withdrawals have been restored for a substantial number of cryptocurrencies on Ethereum and Solana networks.

Withdrawals enabled for: Shiba Inu (SHIB), Ethena (ENA), Starknet (STRK), Pump.fun (PUMP), Jupiter (JUP), Bonk (BONK), dogwifhat (WIF), Pudgy Penguins (PENGU), Official Trump (TRUMP), Raydium (RAY), Jito (JTO), and Render (RENDER).

Enabled withdrawals remove an operational restriction, even though token's trading activity and price performance haven't been directly affected by the limitation (apart from the on-chain performance).

With withdrawals disabled, Shiba Inu continued to face selling pressure.

Sentiment might flip in case of reclaiming the $0.00000500–$0.00000520 region which represents the short-terms technical improvement zone.

While Binance's withdrawal restoration is a positive operational development for Shiba Inu, SHIB's price trajectory remains bearish and is unlikely to bounce purely on the news about the opened withdrawals on the biggest exchange on the market.

Home » Ethereum (ETH) » ETH Price at $2,300 Test as BitMine Nears 5% of Ethereum Supply

BitMine continues buying ETH as exchange reserves fall, putting the spotlight on Ethereum’s next move around $2,300.

NYSE-listed BitMine Immersion Technologies has expanded its Ethereum holdings with another 5,000 ETH purchase worth approximately $11.22 million. The latest acquisition brings the company’s holdings to roughly 6.065 million ETH, representing nearly 5% of the cryptocurrency’s total supply.

Meanwhile, Ether traded near $2,290 on November 3, as market participants watched the $2,300 level for signs of a sustained recovery.

Lookonchain reported on X today that BitMine Immersion Technologies, led by Fundstrat’s Tom Lee, bought 5,000 ETH.

Tom Lee (@fundstrat)'s #Bitmine bought another 5,000 $ETH ($11.22M) 6 hours ago.Bitmine now holds 6.065M $ETH ($13.7B), 4.96% of the total supply.https://t.co/f70iddoJZ2 pic.twitter.com/OaIcI7icJD

As of November 1, BitMine’s balance sheet now holds 6,060,506 ETH, representing approximately 4.9% of the total supply, with an estimated market value of $13.7 billion.

The company is now roughly 99% of the way toward its stated target of controlling 5% of all circulating ETH.

Crypto analyst Ted Pillows noted on X that Ethereum has been holding up well over the past few days, suggesting that buyers are gradually stepping in.

According to his latest analysis, ETH needs to reclaim the $2,300 level to establish the foundation for a stronger recovery rally.

Now is the time for $ETH to start its reversal.In the past few days, Ethereum has definitely been holding up well, which indicates buyers are stepping in.Now, ETH needs to reclaim $2,300 for a decent rally. pic.twitter.com/6WgkgbUc0T

In a previous update on November 2, Pillows highlighted that ETH had reclaimed the $2,250 support zone, identifying $2,400 as the next key level to watch.

The focus is now on whether Ethereum can maintain its recovered support and build enough momentum to break through the next resistance levels.

Ether slipped 0.2% on Tuesday, according to CoinGecko data. It is down 1.4% over the past week and 15.6% over the past month.

On-chain analytics from Santiment Intelligence reveal a contraction in liquid ETH supply across centralized exchanges following the October 29 historical $6.39 billion hack at Binance.

According to Santiment post on X, ETH exchange supply declined from 3.56% of total supply on October 28 to 3.20% by November 2. Over half of the initial drop occurred immediately during the hot-wallet incident, draining ~200,630 ETH.

ETH held by top exchange addresses did not fall on Oct 29. It rose by about 39,000 ETH, then dropped by about 313,000 ETH across Nov 1 and Nov 2, after withdrawals reopened.

From the Oct 28 to Nov 2 closes, top-address holdings are down about 297,000 ETH (~5.4%).

Santiment noted that exchange data alone cannot show whether the outflows reflect cold-storage moves or OTC transfers. Still, corporate accumulation and lower exchange balances together point to a tighter floating supply as ETH approaches $2,300.

Corporate treasuries like BitMine are absorbing a growing share of ETH supply while exchange balances shrink, which can reduce liquid supply available to sellers.

#5244. Ripple CTO Emeritus Clarifies Current Role at Ripple

Disclaimer: The opinions expressed by our writers are their own and do not represent the views of U.Today. The financial and market information provided on U.Today is intended for informational purposes only. U.Today is not liable for any financial losses incurred while trading cryptocurrencies. Conduct your own research by contacting financial experts before making any investment decisions. We believe that all content is accurate as of the date of publication, but certain offers mentioned may no longer be available.

In a recent X discussion, Ripple CTO emeritus David Schwartz has clarified his current position at the company.

An X user had asked Schwartz whether he still serves as a non-executive director. "Are you not still a Non-Executive Director at Ripple?" the X user asked.

The question came after Schwartz said he no longer works for Ripple and had retired. "I don't work for Ripple anymore. I retired," Schwartz stated.In response to the X user's question, Schwartz said he remains on Ripple's Board of Directors and also serves as CTO Emeritus: "I'm on the Board of Directors and I'm CTO Emeritus."

@Lebro589 @CocaColaKid_OG @Fernando031680 @jtraulf1 @SergioVengeance @MAGASouthEastFL @Emmaiid11 I'm on the Board of Directors and I'm CTO Emeritus. I still meet with my former team twice a month and show up randomly at Ripple offices and say "hi".

Schwartz also said he still meets with his former team twice a month, while he occasionally visits Ripple's offices without a formal schedule, describing these visits as opportunities to say hello to his former colleagues: "I still meet with my former team twice a month and show up randomly at Ripple offices and say hi."

David Schwartz, one of the original architects of the XRP Ledger, became the Chief Technology Officer (CTO) of Ripple in July 2018, after previously serving as the company's chief cryptographer since 2011. He transitioned out of his day-to-day operating CTO role at the end of December 2025 to become CTO Emeritus and join Ripple's Board of Directors.

Despite stepping away from his full-time responsibilities, Schwartz indicated that he maintains a connection with the company and his former team.

Schwartz maintains he was not going away from the XRP community, participating in the XRP Ledger through his XRP node, which has now been running for several months, publishing its output data and researching other use cases for XRP besides what Ripple is focused on, among other things.

Shortly after Evernorth, a Ripple-backed XRP treasury firm, was revealed, Schwartz stated he would serve as a strategic advisor to the company, bringing his wealth of experience despite his retirement from Ripple.

At the Swell event, which was held at the end of October, Schwartz joined Ayo Akinyele, Head of Engineering for RippleX, in a conversation on what comes next for XRPL. The discussion explored the technologies shaping the ledger's evolution, the trade-offs behind them, and what it will take to support the next generation of financial and on-chain applications.

#5243. Binance Hack: Everything You Need to Know About the Biggest Crypto Heist in History

Binance Hack: Everything You Need to Know About the Biggest Crypto Heist in History

Crypto just suffered the largest hack in its history.

On Thursday, attackers drained roughly $6.4 billion from Binance, the world’s largest cryptocurrency exchange, in a breach that dwarfs every theft before it and has triggered a cross-border law-enforcement investigation, a scramble by stablecoin issuers to freeze funds, and a tense wait for millions of users.

Here’s what’s known about the Binance hack so far.

The theft began at about 4:05 p.m. ET on Thursday, after U.S. markets had closed, when attackers moved funds out of Binance hot wallets across roughly 10 blockchains, most of it within about 20 minutes.

Blockchain analytics firms Chainalysis and TRM Labs put the total at around $6.4 billion, more than four times the previous record, February 2025’s $1.5 billion Bybit heist.

Crucially, the attack targeted Binance’s own infrastructure, not the blockchains themselves. Binance initially traced the breach to a compromised laptop belonging to a DevOps engineer, which may have let the attacker reach a service container and submit unauthorized withdrawals.

The exchange has since said its investigation uncovered “additional points of breach,” with CEO Richard Teng describing “a more complex attack process” that “did not simply use one engineer’s permissions.” Binance said it found no evidence that wallet private keys were leaked, and that the attacker used a self-destruct mechanism to wipe tooling and traces along the way.

On Ethereum, the attacker leaned on EIP-7702, a feature introduced in 2025 that lets ordinary wallets temporarily run smart-contract code.

Using 50 pre-signed authorizations, the attacker turned a Binance hot wallet into a “sweeper” contract and drained its assets, according to TRM Labs.

Teng said that delegation “did not pass through our normal approval controls.” The episode didn’t expose a flaw in Ethereum itself, but rather, TRM said, pointed to a compromise of Binance’s signing infrastructure.

TRM Labs and Chainalysis both attributed the hack to TraderTraitor, a North Korean state-linked group also blamed for the Bybit heist.

After that attack, the group converted roughly 84% of the stolen Ethereum into Bitcoin via the cross-chain network Thorchain. The attribution pushes North Korea’s 2026 crypto haul well past $1 billion, and the Binance figure alone would dwarf the rest.

Teng said Binance will cover every dollar of the losses with company funds and its Secure Asset Fund for Users, an emergency reserve, stressing that customer assets were never touched.

The exchange has set aside more than $6.34 billion, about $5 billion of it already held in the exact tokens stolen, including BTC, ETH, BNB, USDC, and USDT, and is buying the rest on the open market and over the counter.

Binance paused withdrawals to rebuild and retest its systems, is running a full security review, and aims to restore withdrawals by Sunday while publishing a complete proof of reserves by Monday. Deposits are operating normally.

Stablecoin issuers moved fast following the initial reports of a suspected attack, unlike previous security incidents.

Stablecoins are the backbone of the crypto economy—dollar-pegged tokens designed to hold a steady value that enable quick and easy transactions on both centralized and decentralized exchanges. Unlike traditional digital currencies, like Bitcoin or Ethereum, stablecoins are centralized, issued and managed by private companies. That means these funds can be frozen at any time, and for any reason.

Tether, the company behind the biggest stablecoin on the market USDT, froze more than $1.5 billion in assets. Circle, which issues the second largest stablecoin USDC, froze around $823 million tied to the attack, both using their ability to blacklist specific addresses.

In all, roughly $3 billion has been frozen or located. But a large sum remains beyond reach: the attacker still controls more than 420,000 ETH and 18,270 BTC, plus other tokens, worth over $3 billion that no issuer can freeze, and has kept shuffling funds through Tron and other networks. Security firm Elliptic estimated the hacker still controls around $3.3 billion.

Binance is cooperating with law enforcement worldwide, including the FBI and the U.S. Secret Service.

The FBI confirmed it was aware of the incident and coordinating with partners; the Secret Service, which investigates financial and digital-currency crimes, declined to comment.

It echoes earlier joint efforts like April’s Operation Atlantic, which traced tens of millions in stolen crypto.

For a theft of this scale, markets stayed remarkably composed.

Bitcoin fell to about $76,400 on Friday, down roughly 4%, and more than $1.3 billion in leveraged positions were liquidated.

That’s a far cry from March 2018, when a failed attack on Binance knocked nearly 11% off Bitcoin in minutes. Analysts credit a bigger, more mature market, now worth around $2.6 trillion, with spot Bitcoin ETFs holding roughly 1.3 million BTC in hands that don’t panic-sell.

Wall Street firms including Cantor Fitzgerald, TD Cowen, and Compass Point even urged clients to buy crypto-linked stocks on the weakness, framing the hack as a Binance security failure rather than a threat to the underlying technology.

CFTC Chairman Michael Selig seized on the breach to argue offshore exchanges need U.S. oversight. Selig said in an interview with CNBC that the breach “proves our exact point” behind the crypto-market regulations the agency proposed earlier this month.

And the episode sparked a public spat between Binance founder Changpeng Zhao, better known as CZ, and Elon Musk. The SpaceX and Tesla founder appeared to troll Binance over the hack on X, and CZ fired back, citing Musk’s own SpaceX stake. "Our SPCX shares (from our $500m investment in your Twitter takeover) are worth more than a billion. Would appreciate if you show a little support," CZ posted.

CZ struck a defiant note overall: the theft is the largest in dollar terms, he said, but not in Bitcoin terms, and unlike Mt. Gox or FTX, Binance is absorbing the loss itself.

#5242. Bitcoin and Treasury yields fall together in rare divergence as $80,000 recovery fails

Bitcoin dropped 8.25% while real yields declined 16 basis points, raising questions about why a historically supportive macroeconomic shift has offered little relief.

Bitcoin is struggling to recover despite falling US Treasury yields, as technology-stock weakness and persistent ETF withdrawals weigh on demand.

Between Oct. 21 and Oct. 30, the world's largest cryptocurrency declined 8.25%, falling from approximately $84,134 to $77,191, according to CryptoSlate's data.

Over the same period, the Federal Reserve's data shows that yields on 10-year inflation-protected US Treasuries fell from 2.76% to 2.60%, down 16 basis points. The Nasdaq-100 also lost 4.82%, reflecting mounting pressure on technology stocks.

The divergence is notable because lower real Treasury yields typically reduce the opportunity cost of holding non-yielding assets such as Bitcoin. However, selling pressure across crypto and parts of the equity market has overwhelmed that potential support.

The weakness continued into November. On Nov. 2, the Nasdaq-100 declined another 1.39%, while Bitcoin's daily Coinbase price observation fell 1.34% to approximately $77,062 from the previous day.

Bitcoin was trading near $76,861 early Tuesday, leaving it below the psychologically important $80,000 level.

Bitfinex attributed the weakness to declining technology shares and subdued spot-market demand, arguing that those pressures were overwhelming the potential support from falling real Treasury yields.

The selling pressure was compounded by Binance's Oct. 29 security breach, which crypto trading firm Wintermute identified as the dominant crypto-specific shock behind last week's downturn.

The incident struck an already fragile market, triggering about $1.5 billion in crypto liquidations, with long positions accounting for approximately 85% of the total.

Wintermute said traders had attempted to buy the decline primarily through leveraged perpetual futures rather than sustained spot purchases. As prices continued falling, those positions faced further liquidation, deepening the downturn.

The firm warned that there was little evidence of fresh capital entering the market to support a durable recovery.

The weakness has also extended to US-listed spot Bitcoin exchange-traded funds, which recorded $398.6 million in net outflows on Nov. 2, according to Farside Investors.

BlackRock's iShares Bitcoin Trust accounted for $405.3 million in withdrawals, partially offset by $6.7 million in inflows into Fidelity's FBTC.

That followed $643.2 million in ETF outflows on Oct. 30, bringing total net withdrawals across the two trading sessions to approximately $1.04 billion.

The sustained redemptions have added to concerns about Bitcoin's ability to recover, particularly as Wintermute's analysis suggests that recent buying has been concentrated in leveraged derivatives rather than supported by substantial new spot-market capital.

#5240. No Ethereum (ETH)? Exchanges Report Substantial Outflows

Disclaimer: The opinions expressed by our writers are their own and do not represent the views of U.Today. The financial and market information provided on U.Today is intended for informational purposes only. U.Today is not liable for any financial losses incurred while trading cryptocurrencies. Conduct your own research by contacting financial experts before making any investment decisions. We believe that all content is accurate as of the date of publication, but certain offers mentioned may no longer be available.

Ethereum’s exchange supply is shrinking after the October 29 Binance hack, but on-chain data suggests there is something more interesting hiding behind the scenes. The two-stage decline in exchange-held ETH could reflect the liquidity contraction rather than a minor panic-fueled outflow.

Between October 28 and November 2, the share of Ethereum’s total supply held on exchanges dropped from 3.56% to 3.20%, a decline of 0.36%. Just over half of that drop happened on October 29, the same day as the reported Binance exploit.

According to TRM Labs, hackers drained around 200,630 ETH from Binance’s hot wallet.

Instead of dropping right away, ETH balances on top exchange addresses actually grew by around 39,000 ETH on October 29. The bigger drop came later. Those addresses lost roughly 313,000 ETH on November 1 and November 2, after withdrawals were reopened.

Major exchange addresses lost around 297,000 ETH, or 5.4%, between October 28 and November 2, suggesting a substantial liquidity thinning.

Falling exchange balances aren't necessarily suggesting an accumulation. The coins may have gone into cold storage, moved between wallets, or sent to other exchanges. Exchange-address data alone can’t show where they've been sent.

As of now, Ethereum’s price stays bearish. On the daily ETH/USD chart, ETH is trading near $2,264, below several key moving averages. The price has pulled back from its October highs around $2,800.

Selling pressure can be clearly seen on the chart, and the $2,300-$2,400 range could act as resistance. A proper recovery above the level would suggest an improvement in sentiment. But if buyers fail to defend the $2,200 area, Ethereum could drop even further.

For now, the current supply drop is more of a liquidity shift, rather than a confirmed supply shock, especially on gigantic exchanges like Binance.

The more important question is whether the missing ETH has actually left trading circulation or just moved elsewhere. Until market flows calm down a little, it’s too early to call the exchange supply drop a bullish signal.

#5238. SOON traders, look beyond positive funding before betting on a recovery

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Soon [SOON] started the day on a bearish note, with the asset declining 14%, according to CoinGlass. However, market activity shows that the current price level has not changed the fundamental factors hinting at a possible bullish base still being in place.

The classic explanation for this is that while SOON’s short-term outlook remains bearish, its long-term prospects still show strong upside potential.

The broader market shock has played a key role in pulling capital out of the asset. Press-time data shows that SOON has recorded significant outflows in both the perpetual and spot markets.

In the perpetual market, the netflow data shows heavy selling activity. CoinGlass shows that capital outflows reached roughly $10.44 million, compared to inflows of $8.62 million, putting its net inflow at -$1.82 million.

A negative net inflow in the perpetual market often suggests that traders are pulling capital out of the market, reflecting heightened volatility.

In the spot market, the outlook is similar, with spot net inflows turning positive. In this case, a positive net inflow suggests that more SOON is being moved into exchanges than out of them, potentially indicating that traders are preparing to sell rather than accumulate.

Press-time data shows a spot net inflow of $697,000, as more capital moved into exchanges while traders prepared to sell off the asset, possibly in reaction to bearish market conditions.

The current capital movements across the perpetual and spot markets appear to reflect short-term market activity rather than a long-term shift.

The liquidation heatmap, on the other hand, points to a potentially bullish outlook for SOON over the near to long term. CoinGlass’s 1-month liquidation heatmap shows that liquidation clusters are heavily concentrated above the current price, with barely any visible clusters below it.

Price tends to move towards liquidation clusters on the chart, and right now, their heavy concentration above the current level could pull the asset towards those levels.

Importantly, the limited clusters below the price indicate that sellers could run into exhaustion. If demand grows, SOON could see a rebound, potentially creating new local highs.

For now, momentum is against long traders in the market, with $67,770 in losses recorded by long traders compared to just $4,440 in losses for short traders over the past 24 hours.

Traders in the perpetual market remain bullish across SOON contracts, based on funding rate data.

The funding rate measures whether long or short positions dominate an asset’s perpetual contracts. Presently, long positions dominate the market, as the funding rate remains positive at approximately 0.0051%, according to CoinGlass.

Bullish positioning at a time when prices are weak and the market is declining suggests that a potential rebound could still play out in the near term. Overall, capital in the perpetual market, as reflected in the funding rate data, still sits at $32.32 million at the time of writing.

#5237. Hong Kong bank pilot opens 24/7 cash settlement for tokenized fund deals

Participating institutions can settle tokenized deposit transactions in central bank money beyond conventional hours, supporting treasury and fund activity.

Hong Kong's tokenization pilot now lets participating banks settle tokenized deposit transactions around the clock in central bank money, extending the cash settlement behind corporate treasury and tokenized-fund activity beyond conventional operating hours.

The Hong Kong Monetary Authority announced the upgrade on Nov. 2. EnsembleTX, the pilot phase of Project Ensemble, now has 12 participating banks and 20 industry pioneers.

The customer deposits and the settlement money have different roles. Participating firms use tokenized deposits supplied by commercial banks; tokenized central bank money settles the transactions between banks. The upgrade extends the hours available for that interbank cash settlement.

Participating industry pioneers have already used bank-provided tokenized deposits for corporate treasury management and tokenized money market fund transactions. HKMA names Ant International, BlackRock and China Asset Management (Hong Kong) among the firms involved.

Banks providing tokenized deposits for the cited activity include Bank of China (Hong Kong), China Construction Bank (Asia), ICBC (Asia), Standard Chartered Bank (Hong Kong) and HSBC. These are examples given by HKMA, rather than the full roster of 12 participating banks.

For a corporate treasury using the pilot, the practical change is that its participating bank can settle the relevant transactions outside conventional operating hours. Tokenized-fund users gain extended cash-settlement availability where their banks and platforms support the activity.

Securities and Futures Commission Chief Executive Julia Leung said the capability supports round-the-clock corporate treasury transactions and secondary trading of tokenized funds on licensed platforms. Availability for an individual fund still depends on the bank and platform arrangements; continuous interbank settlement does not establish continuous trading for every fund.

EnsembleTX launched in November 2025 as a controlled environment for real-value transactions involving tokenized deposits and digital assets. It initially used the Hong Kong dollar Real Time Gross Settlement system. Round-the-clock settlement in tokenized central bank money was envisaged as a later enhancement. November's change extends the settlement infrastructure behind that existing real-value activity.

The newly enabled capability remains within that participating-institution pilot.

HKMA also outlined separate trial work in its FinTech Week update. It reported completed trial trades involving recognized dealers conducting secondary-market transactions in tokenized Exchange Fund Bills, then said it would soon expand trials to include regulated stablecoins.

The planned stablecoin trials remain a separate next step.

Over the day, 44,857 ETH were withdrawn from spot Ethereum ETFs. The combined assets under management (AUM) of all crypto ETFs stand at $95.83 billion as per SoSoValue.

The current daily outflow has spread across the entire line of exchange-traded products: SOL funds lost 129.19 thousand coins, outflows from XRP products amounted to 128.54 thousand tokens, and the specialized HYPE and ZEC funds shed 85.07 thousand and 13.53 thousand coins, respectively. Major operators are preemptively pulling liquidity out of volatile instruments ahead of the US midterm elections.

Amid the decline in market capitalization, a sharp split among participants has emerged: while retail investors are locking in losses en masse and most whales are betting on a decline, some large on-chain operators are opening aggressive counter-trend positions on decentralized exchanges.

The main event of the morning session was the actions of an unknown major player on the Hyperliquid platform, who, despite the overall market decline (NEAR dropped to $3.79, BNB to $697.67), stubbornly holds an XRP long worth tens of millions of dollars, turning the contract into the main barometer of local altcoin resilience.

The decentralized futures platform Hyperliquid is currently accumulating peak institutional volumes. At the same time, on-chain analytics of top wallets with balances of $1 million or more shows a strong bearish skew in open interest.

Of these wallets' total open positions of $4.5 billion, short contracts (shorts) account for $3.08 billion. Their long volume is limited to a modest $1.46 billion.

Leviathan addresses with positions of $50 million or more are almost unanimously betting on a further market decline. Against this grim backdrop, the long position of wallet '0x31b1ecf848443472e34d204a134812d86ab01434' remains the largest and riskiest counter-trend position in the system.

The whale holds 35.03 million XRP tokens. The notional value of the open position is $44.05 million at 10x Cross leverage.

The average entry price is $1.1298. At the peak of the morning momentum, the whale's unrealized profit exceeded $4.47 million, which amounts to more than 101% net return on the margin capital invested.

At the same time, the account's overall result is far from ideal: due to overall asset volatility, this wallet's net PnL over the past 30 days has fallen by $8.45 million.

The critical threshold in this whole story remains the forced liquidation price. The automatic position closure threshold sits at $0.9626.

The current XRP market price of around $1.2558 leaves the trader a buffer of about 23% to the liquidation point.

Large wallets' XRP positions on Hyperliquid are also skewed toward the bears. Against long positions of $55.70 million stands a massive wall of shorts worth $80.93 million. Nearly 80% of these longs belong to a single whale.

This morning's news backdrop points to a massive and painful restructuring of the entire market infrastructure. The central theme remains a severe liquidity crisis on centralized exchanges.

$6.3 billion Binance hack and the FBI's position: The agency officially attributed the attack to North Korean state cyber units. An analysis by lawyers at Reed Smith and Lathrop GPM showed that the exchange's insurance policies will not cover the damage. Standard D&O insurance protects only top management, while war exclusion clauses fully rule out payouts in the event of cyberwarfare waged by foreign states.

Record outflow of $15.4 billion: The weekly net capital outflow from Binance hit an all-time high. This volume exceeds the individual reserves of Bybit or Bitfinex and surpasses the combined balance of Gate, Bitget and KuCoin, which keeps the price of BNB at local lows.

Binance founder Changpeng Zhao's loud statements about rising risks to the financial system from automated AI-powered cyberattacks came amid the incident at the exchange he created, which showed the ineffectiveness of classic defense mechanisms against coordinated cyber operations. Zhao stressed that commercial banks, due to outdated IT architecture, are more vulnerable than crypto platforms.

Against this backdrop, decentralized protocols are confidently expanding their institutional presence. Hyperliquid's annual revenue has reached $713 million, and trading volume in perpetuals on stocks and commodities via HIP-3 has exceeded $500 billion. Building on these technologies, the operator of the New York Stock Exchange (ICE) and Kraken are already preparing to launch regulated futures in the US with White House support.

Meanwhile, serious changes are also taking place at the level of the largest L1 ecosystems. House of Stake (NEAR Protocol) participants approved the deflationary initiative HSP-029 with 99.96% of the vote, reducing annual emissions from 2.5% to 1.6% over 24 months. Daily issuance of new tokens is being cut from 89,500 to 57,300, while amid the general market decline the asset briefly dropped to $3.79.

Skepticism is meanwhile growing in the Ripple camp: the company reports that issuance of its dollar token RLUSD has surpassed $1 billion through partnerships with trust banks, but XRP holders are voicing open dissatisfaction. RLUSD is a centralized asset subject to freezing under US law, while XRP's decentralized status is not yet reflected in its market valuation.

At the same time, the Ethereum Foundation launched a platform for using the network as digital public infrastructure (DPI) for governments. Case studies on digital identity in Bhutan and confidential humanitarian aid systems have been published. At Devcon 8 in Mumbai, Vitalik Buterin called for developing verifiable code to minimize dependence on closed software and AI systems.

In the coming sessions, capital flows in the crypto market will be largely determined by external macroeconomic and political triggers. Investors and market makers need to keep the following critical factors in focus:

US Congressional midterm elections: The main political factor of this Tuesday, forcing large funds to preemptively close margin positions, cut leverage and hedge risks ahead of a change in the regulatory landscape.

Official JOLTS labor market report: Tuesday's release of job openings data will determine the short-term dynamics of the US dollar index and directly affect liquidity in high-risk assets.

ISM non-manufacturing business activity indices: Wednesday's statistics will highlight the real state of the US economy and shape the medium-term sentiment of major futures market operators.

Fed balance sheet on Thursday and Friday's Nonfarm Payrolls report: The key macroeconomic triggers of the week, which will lock in the final direction of capital flows through the end of the month.

#5235. How to Run an AI Model Locally—And Why You Might Want To

How to Run an AI Model Locally—And Why You Might Want To

Open-weight AI models now trail the best closed models by only about four months on average, and a growing number of them run on hardware you probably already own.

"Open-weight" means the company publishes the weights, the files holding everything the model learned, so anyone can download and run them. Two free programs, Ollama and LM Studio, handle the rest, and Gemma 4 downloads in Ollama's library range from about 4.6GB to 20GB.

With open models, you don't need to pay $20 a month for an AI subscription or send your conversations to someone else's servers to get a capable chatbot. This guide explains how to download and run AI models directly on your computer, what kind of hardware you'll need, and how to get started with free tools like Ollama and LM Studio.

Whether you want to save money, keep your conversations private, or experiment with AI models you can customize yourself, setting up a local AI assistant may be easier than you think.

LM Studio's documentation says the app "can operate entirely offline, just make sure to get some model files first," and that documents attached to a chat can be used entirely offline too.

Subscriptions to paid chatbots run about $20 per month, while the software here is free. The cost shifts to hardware you may already have.

Licenses decide what you can do with downloaded weights. DeepSeek's R1 series is under the MIT License, and its model card says the models "support commercial use, allow for any modifications and derivative works," while Google's Gemma 4 and OpenAI's gpt-oss ship under Apache 2.0.

Running models on an open-source license like Apache or MIT means you are free to use, modify, and do whatever you want with a model. This means users are able to access improved and modified versions of a specific model that better fit their needs. Maybe you need a model that is good at medicine, so you don’t care about how good it codes. This level of personalization is only available in customized models.

Models are sized in parameters, the internal numbers adjusted during training, and the download grows with them. The model has to fit in your graphics card's video memory, known as VRAM, or in a Mac's unified memory, which the processor and graphics chip share.

As a general rule, you'll want at least 8GB of VRAM, that compressed smaller will be able to fit. However, serious users should buy a gaming GPU with 24GB of VRAM or more, either by stacking up GPUs or by buying specific AI racks or machines with unified memory like the Mac Mini or the Nvidia DGX Spark.

Quantization shrinks a model by storing each weight with fewer bits. Hugging Face's documentation describes it as representing weights and activations "with low-precision data types like 8-bit integer (int8) instead of the usual 32-bit floating point (float32)," which cuts memory use at the cost of some accuracy.

LM Studio's system requirements recommend 16GB or more of RAM on Apple Silicon Macs running macOS 14 or newer, though 8GB Macs can handle smaller models. On Windows it recommends at least 16GB of RAM and 4GB of dedicated VRAM, with AVX2 processor support, and Intel-based Macs are not supported.

Ollama's library lists Gemma 4 26B at 16 to 19GB and the 31B version at 19 to 20GB. OpenAI's gpt-oss-20b needs only 16GB of memory, and gpt-oss-120b runs within 80GB, according to OpenAI.

LM Studio is probably the go-to choice because it provides a nice polished graphical user interface. Ollama, on the other hand, is aimed at developers and more experienced users who prefer working inside of a terminal (CLI).

For LM Studio, download the installer for your operating system from lmstudio.ai, search for a model inside the app, download its weights and open a chat. The app searches Hugging Face, the public hub where open models are published.

For Ollama, macOS and Linux users install with the command curl -fsSL https://ollama.com/install.sh | sh, and Windows users run irm https://ollama.com/install.ps1 | iex in PowerShell, both listed on Ollama's download page.

Typing ollama pull gemma4:e2b downloads a model and ollama run gemma4:e2b opens a chat, and /bye exits, per Ollama's quickstart. The program also opens a local API at http://localhost:11434, so other apps on your machine can use the model.

Google's Multi-Token Prediction drafters for Gemma 4, available on Ollama under Apache 2.0, speed up generation by up to 3x using speculative decoding, in which a small model proposes several tokens ahead and the large one checks them together.

Google said that "if the target model agrees with the draft, it accepts the entire sequence in a single forward pass," and the target model adds one extra token of its own in that same pass.

Local models gain web search and file access through MCP servers, short for Model Context Protocol, and LM Studio added support in version 0.3.17, according to the Decrypt guide.

The four-month average describes the most capable open models, and the largest are far past consumer hardware. Alibaba's Qwen3.8-Max has 2.4 trillion total parameters, with 95 billion active at any time, compared with the 80GB of memory that gpt-oss-120b runs within.

When a model spills out of VRAM into system RAM, responses may be a lot slower, and larger context windows need more memory. Gemma 4's small models offer a 128K-token context window, the amount of text a model can consider at once, and the larger ones 256K.

Cloud services need none of that planning, and the frontier models behind them still lead on the hardest tests.

If you want to use AI privately, our own review recommended "xPrivo with a self-hosted local model for day-to-day drafting that must not leave your machine," and Kagi "when you need web retrieval and accept the API call tradeoff."

#5233. 131 Million ADA Delegated to Dead Cardano Pools, Not Earning

Disclaimer: The opinions expressed by our writers are their own and do not represent the views of U.Today. The financial and market information provided on U.Today is intended for informational purposes only. U.Today is not liable for any financial losses incurred while trading cryptocurrencies. Conduct your own research by contacting financial experts before making any investment decisions. We believe that all content is accurate as of the date of publication, but certain offers mentioned may no longer be available.

According to popular Cardano explorer Cexplorer there are over 131 million ADA currently delegated to retired stake pools, leaving thousands of delegators without staking rewards.

Cardano staking pools offer ADA holders a way to passively earn rewards for securing the blockchain by delegating their coins to network validators, all without locking up or losing control of their funds.

Cexplorer recently posted on X that 59,025 delegators still have their ADA delegated to pools that have retired. Therefore, they are not earning staking rewards on their current delegations.

⚠️ Over 131M $ADA is currently delegated to retired stake pools. 59,025 delegators staking with those pools are not earning any staking rewards. If you're one of them, consider choosing a new stake pool to keep earning rewards and supporting Cardano's decentralization. https://t.co/L9mitriW2P

According to Cexplorer data, 131.63 million ADA is still delegated to retired pools and the number of retired pools is given as 2,720. Holders can delegate ADA to a stake pool and earn rewards as long as the pool they choose is active, which contributes to network security and decentralization.

However, when a stake pool retires, delegators must select another active pool if they want to continue earning rewards. Their ADA does not disappear simply because the pool has retired, but the delegation no longer provides the expected staking benefits.

Users who are delegating their ADA to such retired pools are urged to review their staking arrangements and consider moving their ADA to active stake pools.

Major crypto exchange Binance has commenced phased withdrawals for crypto networks days after it suffered a hack that impacted nearly $6.3 billion in client assets. In line with this, Binance will reopen withdrawals for Cardano (ADA) on the BSC network beginning at 04:00 (UTC) on November 3, 2026.

In Dijkstra upgrade news, Node 11.2 is out as a pre-release. It carries the full Dijkstra hard fork feature set apart from Leios and will move DijkstraNet into the Dijkstra era so downstream teams can start integration testing.

Node 11.3 includes Leios and is set to deliver the complete Dijkstra feature set. A tagged pre-release is realistically three to four weeks away, two at the earliest.

#5232. Stablecoin supply falls $17B as Solana card spending hits $141M

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Stablecoin liquidation continues to drop throughout the cryptocurrency markets, reducing capital that will be used to support investor and trader transactions. In April, the total amount of all stable coins was $280 billion, and it has now decreased to $263 billion.

This decrease in value represents a 6% reduction from April to date.

Binance is experiencing a much larger loss than many of the other large cryptocurrency exchanges due to the decrease in its reserve of stablecoins. Prior to the recent losses, Binance held around $51 billion worth of stablecoins in reserve.

Currently, that number stands at approximately $33.9 billion. The recent losses experienced by Binance are reflective of the recent hacking incident, which saw an approximate loss of $3 billion removed from the platform.

As investors withdraw their stablecoins and transfer their assets to other cryptocurrency platforms, they are taking away purchasing power from these exchanges.

When exchanges lose purchasing power, the demand for products purchased using cryptocurrencies can suffer. This could result in increased downward pressure on prices.

A similar effect is being felt on Ethereum’s [ETH] ability to rapidly purchase any asset based on immediate needs for such. Over the past thirty days, Binance’s Ethereum-based reserves for Tether [USDT] have been reduced by 7.8% to $34.78 billion.

The declines in reserves reflect ongoing withdrawals. For example, each day for the past month has reflected an average weekly net flow of withdrawals that has resulted in a negative balance.

Although some funds may have moved into self-custody or other platforms, Binance now holds less capital ready for trading.

This weakness coincides with muted ETH spot volumes, limited exchange accumulation, and continued outflows from Ethereum ETFs. As both institutional and spot demand decrease, so do the availability of buyers willing to acquire selling assets.

Therefore, ETH price action is facing increasing downward pressures until renewed influxes of USDT occur into exchanges and demand for investments increases.

While exchange liquidity weakens, Solana [SOL] is recording increases in payments made utilizing stablecoins. On the 31st of October, the total monthly volume of card spending reached a record high of $141 million.

This was a sevenfold increase compared to October 2025 and represented a significant increase since spending first crossed the $50 million mark per month in January 2026.

The recent increase indicates an increasing reliance upon Solana-based stablecoins for daily use cases. Therefore, regardless of declining global cryptocurrency liquidations, Solana is gaining an additional form of network demand through utility.

The total stablecoin market capitalization fell 6% to $263 billion, while Binance’s Tether [USDT] reserves dropped 7.8% in 30 days.

Solana [SOL] stablecoin card spending reached a record $141 million in October, marking sevenfold year-on-year growth.

#5231. Bitcoin ETF Investors Head for the Exits After Record Binance Hack

Bitcoin ETF Investors Head for the Exits After Record Binance Hack

The first real test of how ETF investors would react to the largest crypto hack in history came back red.

U.S. spot Bitcoin ETFs shed $643.2 million on Friday, according to Decrypt’s ETF tracker, their biggest single-day outflow of October. It was the funds’ first full session since attackers began draining roughly $6.4 billion from Binance at 4:05 p.m. ET Thursday, after U.S. markets had closed. Thursday’s $17.2 million in inflows predated the theft.

The selling erased a four-day, $441 million inflow run, leaving the funds down about $202 million for the week and roughly $516 million for October. Cumulative net inflows since launch slipped to $57.4 billion, with total net assets at $103.2 billion.

Ethereum and Solana ETFs fared worse relative to their size. Ethereum funds lost $155.7 million on Friday, extending a five-day outflow streak to about $431 million and pushing October losses to roughly $1.61 billion. Solana ETFs shed $10.8 million, their fifth straight day of outflows, totaling about $72 million for the week. They logged net inflows on just two days all month.

Spot crypto ETFs hold the underlying asset, such as Bitcoin or Ethereum, and trade on stock exchanges like ordinary shares, giving investors exposure through a regular brokerage account.

Bitcoin funds have drawn $57.4 billion in net inflows since debuting in January 2024, and Ethereum funds have taken in $12.2 billion since launching that July. Because issuers report flows daily and much of the money comes from institutions and financial advisers, the figures have become a closely watched gauge of investor sentiment.

Crypto markets initially appeared to take the Binance hack largely in stride, considering the scale of the theft. Bitcoin fell about 4% to roughly $76,400 on Friday, and more than $1.3 billion in leveraged positions were liquidated. It’s a sharp drop, but a far smaller one than the nearly 11% plunge after a failed attack on Binance in 2018.

Binance has pledged to cover all losses with company funds and an emergency reserve, while Tether and Circle froze more than $2.3 billion in stolen stablecoins. Some Wall Street firms even urged clients to buy crypto-linked stocks on the potential weakness in markets.

By Saturday, Bitcoin had steadied at about $77,520, up 0.5% over 24 hours but down 3.3% on the week. Ethereum traded near $2,251, down 3.9% for the week, and Solana near $96, down 4.5%. Total crypto market cap held at about $2.7 trillion.

Macro headwinds remain in play. The Federal Reserve held rates at 3.75% to 4% on Wednesday, but two officials voted for a hike, and traders had priced odds above 70% for an increase in December.

Traders on Myriad, a prediction market developed by Decrypt’s parent company Dastan, have turned bearish on Ethereum and Solana. They now give Ethereum a 51.3% chance of falling to $1,500 before reaching $3,000, flipping a market that favored the upside at 61.3% a week ago. Solana’s odds of sinking to $40 before hitting $160 rose to 53.7%.

#5228. North Korea denies role in $6 billion Binance hack after FBI accusation

Reuters, citing KCNA, reported the denial after the FBI blamed TraderTraitor for stealing more than $6 billion from Binance.

Kim Yo Jong, sister of North Korean leader Kim Jong Un, dismissed US claims linking the country to the Binance hack as “absurd rhetoric,” Reuters reported on Nov. 2, citing the Korean Central News Agency, or KCNA. The denial disputes the US government's attribution of the theft of more than $6 billion from the cryptocurrency exchange.

The FBI had blamed North Korea's TraderTraitor operation in an Oct. 31 alert, placing the theft on or about Oct. 29. TraderTraitor is the bureau's name for the specific North Korean cyber activity it identified in the attack.

Blockchain intelligence firm TRM Labs estimated that approximately $6 billion left Binance wallets across ten blockchains, with most of the funds moving in about 20 minutes beginning at 20:05 UTC on Oct. 29. The firm's initial figures reflected observations through 00:30 UTC on Oct. 30.

TRM attributed the Binance hack to TraderTraitor with high confidence, citing on-chain and off-chain intelligence.

The firm also assessed that valid signed transfers across different blockchains pointed to a compromise of Binance's hot-wallet signing infrastructure. That would place the breach in the systems used to authorize outgoing wallet transactions. The explanation remains TRM's assessment of how the transfers were enabled. Its conclusion connects the ability to produce valid transfers on different networks with a potential weakness in the infrastructure used to sign those transactions.

The alert urged cryptocurrency service providers to block transactions involving the listed theft-linked addresses. It described those wallets as operated by, or closely connected to, TraderTraitor actors. CryptoSlate reported the FBI attribution and blocking appeal on Nov. 1, before the North Korean denial.

The request extends beyond exchanges to blockchain infrastructure operators, DeFi services and analytics firms. The FBI specifically named THORNode operators, RPC node operators and instant exchanges, alongside decentralized services and other businesses that handle virtual assets. It covers transactions involving both the identified addresses and funds derived from them.
