#5225. FBI Blames North Korea for $6B Binance Hack as Exchange Reopens Withdrawals

FBI Blames North Korea for $6B Binance Hack as Exchange Reopens Withdrawals

The FBI has confirmed that North Korea was behind last week's theft of more than $6 billion from Binance, as the exchange began reopening withdrawals and reported billions of dollars leaving the platform.

In an alert published on Saturday, the FBI said the attack was carried out by North Korean hackers it refers to as "TraderTraitor," confirming the attribution blockchain analytics firms TRM Labs and Chainalysis made on Friday.

The FBI is releasing information advising the Democratic People's Republic of Korea (North Korea) was responsible for the theft of more than $6 billion USD in virtual assets from cryptocurrency exchange, Binance, on or about October 29, 2026. The FBI refers to this specific North… pic.twitter.com/TBUvUFln4n

— FBI Cyber Branch (@FBICyberBranch) October 31, 2026

The FBI, together with the Secret Service and IRS Criminal Investigation, has seen the hackers converting stolen assets mainly into Ethereum, and expects them to launder the funds further through DeFi and instant swap services, the bureau said. It urged node operators, instant exchanges, DeFi services, blockchain analytics firms and other crypto businesses to block transactions involving hundreds of Ethereum addresses, 19 Bitcoin addresses and one Solana address it linked to the theft.

The Secret Service, which is also investigating the theft, said on Sunday that its investigators worked with industry partners to identify and freeze "a significant portion" of the stolen crypto within minutes of the attack. That work continues with the FBI, IRS Criminal Investigation, the U.S. Attorney's Offices for Washington, D.C. and the Central District of California, and the Justice Department's computer crime and national security divisions, the agency said.

The laundering is already well underway. After the attack, the hackers moved more than $500 million on Tron through more than 1,000 addresses, over 3,000 deposits and several Tron-based DeFi protocols, blockchain analytics firm Chainalysis said on Sunday. Most of those staging wallets have now been drained, with funds leaving Tron through cross-chain services including Chainflip, Relay, THORChain and NEAR for dozens of fresh wallets on Ethereum, Bitcoin and other chains, and the launderers have started moving onto Solana, the firm said.

Binance began restoring withdrawals in phases at 1 a.m. ET on Sunday, and all 15 networks and assets in its first phase were available by 7:36 a.m. ET. In the first nine and a half hours, it processed $4.649 billion in withdrawals and $1.01 billion in deposits, a net outflow of about $3.64 billion, the exchange said.

That represents 2.1% of the $171 billion in assets Binance reported in its most recent proof of reserves, from October 28, according to the exchange. "After an initial spike, withdrawal volumes have declined and are stabilising as expected," Binance said. It took a new reserves snapshot on October 31 and expects to publish an updated report on Monday.

As withdrawals began, CEO Richard Teng said Binance had replenished approximately 95% of the affected assets. "Binance will absorb the financial impact of the stolen funds, not our users," he wrote.

Today, we're beginning the phased resumption of withdrawals, taking a careful approach to ensure services return safely and securely.

We've now replenished approximately 95% of the impacted assets, and our commitment remains clear: Binance will absorb the financial impact of the… https://t.co/wKvI7Q4fsT pic.twitter.com/uWFGs3sJJ5

— Richard Teng (@_RichardTeng) November 1, 2026

Binance has since reopened withdrawals on more networks, including Zcash, TON and Sui, and on Monday restored fiat withdrawals in U.S. dollars, UAE dirhams, Vietnamese dong, Philippine pesos and Indonesian rupiah.

"Our top priority right now is to ensure the environment is secure, after which users can withdraw their funds and Binance can resume operations," Binance co-CEO Yi He said on X on Monday, Reuters reported. Binance said it has raised $6 billion to cover the losses from its corporate treasury, an insurance fund and its SAFU emergency fund, according to Reuters, which also reported that Circle had frozen more than $1.1 billion in USDC.

Data from DefiLlama, which tracks the public wallets of exchanges, put Binance's total assets at about $127.6 billion on Monday. More than $6 billion left wallets associated with Binance over 24 hours after withdrawals reopened, CoinDesk reported, citing DefiLlama, though those movements can include transfers between Binance's own wallets. Rival exchanges OKX and Bybit recorded net inflows of about $450 million and $134 million, respectively, during Binance's reopening.

The hack has also rattled institutional investors. Spot Bitcoin ETFs shed $643.2 million on Friday, the first full trading session after the hack, leaving the funds with roughly $516 million in net outflows for October. "A hack at a crypto exchange is bank robbery at the speed of the internet," TRM Labs' Ari Redbord told Reuters.

#5224. Hyperliquid (HYPE) Rallies as CFTC Approves First-Ever US Stock Perpetuals

Disclaimer: The opinions expressed by our writers are their own and do not represent the views of U.Today. The financial and market information provided on U.Today is intended for informational purposes only. U.Today is not liable for any financial losses incurred while trading cryptocurrencies. Conduct your own research by contacting financial experts before making any investment decisions. We believe that all content is accurate as of the date of publication, but certain offers mentioned may no longer be available.

Hyperliquid’s native token (HYPE) responded with confident growth to the historic decision of the U.S. Commodity Futures Trading Commission (CFTC), which for the first time allowed trading of perpetual futures on individual stocks (Equity Perpetuals).

The regulatory shift de facto legitimizes a product that developed predominantly in DeFi ecosystems and opens the way for the institutional expansion of Hyperliquid’s infrastructure, which served as the trigger for the asset’s rally.

According to the CFTC release of November 3, 2026, equity perpetual contracts received the status of security futures under joint CFTC and SEC oversight, with the possibility of trading on dual-registered exchanges.

Today, equity perps can begin trading for the first time on @CFTC registered exchanges. This move reflects my commitment to ensuring that America’s markets remain the most innovative, liquid, and well-regulated in the world. Instead of being forced to turn to offshore, unregulated venues to access equity perpetual futures, Americans may now access equity perps on CFTC-regulated markets subject to robust safeguards. I appreciate the continued partnership of @SECGov and @SECPaulSAtkins as we work to further harmonize our agencies' regulatory initiatives.

The agency’s goal is to bring back into the U.S. legal field billions in volume that had been flowing to offshore crypto platforms. The instrument is being launched under the Commodity Futures Modernization Act of 2000 (CFMA), which eliminates the need to pass new laws.

The regulator’s pragmatic step directly boosts Hyperliquid’s capitalization. Through its HIP-3 framework (in particular, the trade[XYZ] platform), the project already controls the largest on-chain market for perps on Apple, Nvidia and Tesla, with daily turnover in the hundreds of millions of dollars.

The new CFTC classification fully matched the structure that the Hyperliquid Policy Center advocated in official submissions to the authorities.

Now financial giant ICE (owner of the NYSE), which previously invested in trade[XYZ], gets a legal basis for creating a regulated American platform on Hyperliquid technology, while Payward (Kraken’s parent company) is accelerating the launch of a similar product through the regulated exchange Bitnomial.

Despite legalization in the U.S., Hyperliquid maintains a strict geoblock for American residents, continuing to serve the global market thanks to its key trump cards: on-chain liquidity, 24/7 trading and flexible margin conditions.

Local exchanges in the U.S. will inevitably face the limitations of traditional Wall Street sessions and strict SEC leverage limits in the early stages.

This moves Hyperliquid from the status of an offshore experiment into the role of the main technology donor of the American financial market, with the possibility of monetizing its infrastructure through fee opportunities from the trades of regulated partners.

The market instantly priced these prospects into the asset’s value. TradingView charts recorded a vertical surge in the HYPE/USD pair immediately after the release was published at 14:02 UTC: the price impulsively broke out of the $84.9-$85.30 corridor tested a local high above $87.40 and consolidated around $86.83, holding a net gain of more than 2%.

#5222. Crypto spent $55 million defending a senator who already backed CLARITY

One of crypto's most expensive election bets is playing out in Ohio, where winning could leave the industry's biggest problem in Washington untouched.

Defend American Jobs, a crypto-backed political group, spent more than $55 million supporting Republican Jon Husted and opposing Sherrod Brown in Ohio between Sept. 21 and Oct. 26, according to Americans for Financial Reform. Husted had already voted to advance the CLARITY Act.

Americans vote today, Nov. 3, in midterm elections that will determine control of Congress for the next two years. Republicans currently control both chambers.

Congress has to agree on crypto legislation, which means winning over enough lawmakers in both parties to get it passed.

The midterms will shape committee leadership, hearings and the path to a vote. Presidents appoint regulators and set enforcement priorities, but a later administration can change those policies.

Crypto-backed groups have poured money into congressional races, often backing candidates based on their crypto positions rather than their party.

Fairshake and its affiliated groups have become some of the biggest spenders in American politics, with a Reuters analysis estimating that crypto groups had spent roughly $176 million across more than 100 races by late October.

Ohio has become a major focus of that strategy.

Husted already voted in September to advance the CLARITY Act, a major proposal intended to establish clearer federal rules for crypto. The bill failed to clear a 60-vote Senate hurdle, leaving its supporters short of the backing needed to move forward. Brown, meanwhile, wasn't even in the Senate when that happened, having lost his previous seat in 2024 before launching a comeback campaign against Husted for Ohio's other Senate seat.

Keeping Husted in Washington would protect a vote the industry already has, while defeating Brown would prevent a prominent crypto critic from returning to Congress. Both outcomes would be politically valuable, especially with Senate control potentially at stake, but neither will resolve the disagreements that stopped the legislation in September.

That's because lawmakers are still fighting over stablecoin rewards, banking risks and the protections customers should receive when crypto companies handle their money, and the industry's expensive election campaign can't settle those disputes on its own.

Republicans currently hold 53 seats, so even a united Republican caucus would need support from outside the party to reach the 60-vote threshold used in September.

Susan Collins, Josh Hawley, and Jerry Moran all voted against moving ahead. Thom Tillis recorded a no as well, but did so to preserve the option of asking the Senate to reconsider, a procedural step that would allow another attempt rather than an expression of opposition to the bill itself.

Collins wanted further study of whether community banks and credit unions could lose deposits used to support “loans for mortgages, small businesses, and other family needs.” The dispute centers partly on stablecoin rewards. Crypto platforms offer rewards to customers who keep stablecoins in an account, giving those customers another reason to leave their money there, so banks see those offers as competition for money that might otherwise sit in a deposit account. Banking groups argue that losing those deposits could make it harder to fund lending, and they have pushed lawmakers to restrict rewards that work like interest.

Start with the stories that matter. Get the context behind the headlines and a clear view of what to watch next.

Thanks for signing up to The Catalyst. Look out for your first daily email. If you already receive it, you’re all set.

The September proposal would restrict payments by third parties, such as exchanges, when those payments function like interest on bank deposits. It would still allow rewards tied to transactions or activity, which draws a distinction between paying someone to leave a balance untouched and rewarding them for actually using it.

Banking associations argue that the proposed distinction leaves ways to keep paying what is effectively interest, even when the payment is described or structured differently.

Under the September draft, Treasury could impose further restrictions if stablecoin yield caused substantial harm to community-bank deposits within 18 months of enactment. The sponsors presented that provision as protection for smaller banks and the businesses that rely on them.

Banking groups objected that waiting until significant damage had occurred would make the protection come too late. Their response called for tighter restrictions in the legislation itself, rather than relying on regulators to act after deposits had already moved.

Nevada Democrat Catherine Cortez Masto raised a different set of objections, saying the September version left unresolved problems involving illicit finance, prediction markets, and ethics. She supported the separate GENIUS stablecoin legislation, but said this proposal didn't do enough to help law enforcement address crypto crime or resolve concerns about political conflicts.

The sponsors said they incorporated 126 substantive changes requested by Democrats and revised the restrictions governing public officials’ crypto interests. Cortez Masto’s response was that important problems remained and that Republican Senate leaders had ended negotiations before they were resolved.

Those objections would still need answers after a Husted victory, unless the sponsors could find enough support elsewhere.

Even after an agreement in the Senate, the House would have to approve the same legislative text before it could go to the president. Democrats could ask for different terms from those accepted by the current chamber if they won a House majority, including in a scenario where Republicans held the Senate.

Congress can also act after the election and before its current term ends on Jan. 3, 2027. That period gives lawmakers another opportunity to negotiate.

Bills that have not become law by the end of a Congress don't carry over automatically. The legislation would need to be introduced again and work through the process, leaving the newly elected House and Senate to decide which parts of the September proposal they were willing to keep.

#5221. Ripple Treasury Links To SWIFT With XRP-Linked Hook

Home » Ripple (XRP) » Ripple Treasury Links To SWIFT With XRP-Linked Hook

SWIFT’s ISO 20022 gold standard covers a lot of territory in terms of Ripple-linked banking systems.

A former SWIFT standards veteran with more than 27 years at the messaging network says ISO 20022 gives banks a practical way to handle digital assets without rebuilding their back offices.

At the same time, Ripple Treasury’s documented partnerships with SWIFT keep XRP in the conversation as traditional and blockchain rails continue to overlap.

Tom Alaerts, who spent the bulk of his career working on the standards that underpin global payments, has pushed back on the idea that blockchain will simply replace SWIFT.

In a recent conversation with SMQKE, the popular blockchain researcher, he argued that decades of trust, security, neutrality & governance still make the cooperative hard to displace.

My full conversation with Tom Alaerts, who spent over 27 years at SWIFT working on the standards the global payment system runs on.😶‍🌫️Every technical difficulty has been cut out. What is left in this video below is the complete conversation from start to finish, for anyone who… pic.twitter.com/WAFSDof848

What has changed, he said, is the standard itself. Updates around 2022 and 2023 made it easier to represent digital token identifiers, blockchain addresses & higher numerical precision inside the securities messages banks already use.

That means institutions can process tokenized assets through existing systems rather than standing up entirely new infrastructure.

SWIFT’s Tom Alaerts noted that ISO-compliant networks and fintechs face fewer hurdles when banks and central-bank systems evaluate them.

Because they already use terminology and data structures familiar to traditional finance, security reviews, resilience testing, and regulatory checks become less friction-heavy.

He fundamentally expects a hybrid environment in which traditional messaging and blockchain settlement coexist rather than one displacing the other.

🚨A former SWIFT expert with 27 YEARS of experience says ISO 20022 makes it EASIER for $XRP to integrate with GLOBAL BANKING. pic.twitter.com/VijDbUdsAS

For digital assets, SWIFT networks that already map cleanly to ISO 20022, that hybrid path is shorter.

Another SWIFT – Ripple related discussion has focused on Ripple Treasury, the platform that emerged after Ripple’s acquisition of GTreasury. Materials highlight strategic partnerships with leading banks, SWIFT, Refinitiv & other providers to support end-to-end corporate treasury workflows.

The platform sits inside SWIFT’s Certified Partner Program and offers connectivity options such as Alliance Lite2 along with tools for IBAN and ABA lookups. It also lets corporate users manage both traditional fiat balances and digital assets including XRP and RLUSD in one place.

The connection is at the treasury-platform level rather than a direct pipe from the XRP Ledger into SWIFT messaging, but it still places XRP inside a workflow that already touches the network banks rely on daily.

Taken together, the two threads point to the same practical outcome: banks do not need to abandon existing rails to experiment with digital assets.

SWIFT’s ISO 20022 gold standard supplies a shared language, while platforms such as Ripple Treasury supply the operational layer that can sit alongside those rails. SWIFT remains central because of the trust and governance already built around it.

Networks and assets that align with the standard, and that appear inside the tools corporate treasurers already use, are simply better positioned as the two systems continue to converge.

#5220. 99% Cold Wallet Rule Mulled by South Korea Following Binance Hack

South Korean financial regulators are reportedly considering introducing much stricter cryptocurrency storage requirements for local exchanges following the massive $6.3 billion Binance hack.

According to a Nov. 3 report by South Korean news outlet News1, the country’s Financial Services Commission (FSC) is discussing raising the minimum proportion of customer cryptocurrencies that exchanges must store in cold wallets from the current 80% to approximately 99%.

The potential regulatory change comes just days after Binance, the world’s largest cryptocurrency exchange, suffered one of the biggest security breaches in cryptocurrency history.

However, the proposed 99% threshold remains under discussion, and no official decision has been made.

Under South Korea’s existing Virtual Asset User Protection Act, cryptocurrency exchanges are required to store at least 80% of customers’ digital assets in cold wallets.

Unlike hot wallets, which are connected to the internet and used to process everyday transactions, cold wallets are kept offline, making them significantly less vulnerable to remote hacking attacks.

However, the proportion of assets stored offline varies significantly among South Korea’s leading cryptocurrency exchanges.

According to Financial Supervisory Service data cited by local media, Upbit currently has one of the highest cold wallet storage ratios in the country.

As of the end of September, the exchange held approximately 99.17% of customer assets in cold wallets.

Meanwhile, rival cryptocurrency exchange Bithumb stored approximately 94.76% of its assets offline, while Coinone’s cold wallet ratio stood at 85%.

#5219. BTCPay Tells Merchants to Switch Off Servers Amid Hot Wallet Attack Reports

BTCPay Tells Merchants to Switch Off Servers Amid Hot Wallet Attack Reports

BTCPay Server, a free, open-source tool businesses use to accept Bitcoin payments, told operators to switch off their servers on Sunday after reports that attackers were draining the hot wallets connected to them.

"We are receiving reports of hot wallets on BTCPay Servers being swept by unauthorized attackers," the project tweeted at 5:25 p.m. ET. "If you're running an instance, please shut it down (./btcpay-down.sh) until we complete the investigation."

🚨 We are receiving reports of hot wallets on BTCPay Servers being swept by unauthorized attackers.

If you're running an instance, please shut it down (./btcpay-down.sh) until we complete the investigation.

— BTCPay Server (@BtcpayServer) November 1, 2026

About two hours later, BTCPay founder Nicolas Dorier said the reports appeared to involve releases older than version 2.4.2, and that the attacker had logged in to affected servers with administrator access.

Later on Sunday, the project said its investigation had identified an attack vector targeting instances running version 2.4.1, released on July 23, or older. "We have no credible reports of hot wallet drains for users running the latest 2.4.7 release," it said, urging everyone to keep their software updated and asking affected users to send details and logs to its security team.

Some hosting providers, including LunaNode, have shut down BTCPay instances automatically to stop older versions from being exploited, the project said. BTCPay has not said how many servers were affected or how much Bitcoin was taken.

Because BTCPay is self-hosted, each merchant runs the software on their own server, and there is no central operator that can switch instances off. Merchants can choose to run a hot wallet, which stores the wallet's seed on that internet-connected server so it can sign transactions automatically. BTCPay's own documentation labels the feature "DANGER" and warns that it "comes with security implications."

The attacks come days after BTCPay released version 2.4.7 on Thursday as an emergency update fixing one critical and several medium-severity vulnerabilities, which it said were brought to its attention through Anthropic's frontier open-source software scanner. BTCPay has not said whether the attacks exploited any of those flaws.

It is not the first security scare for the project this year. In August, BTCPay warned that a critical vulnerability in all versions before 2.4.2 was being actively exploited, and told users to update or shut down their servers. Supporters of the project later funded a recovery bounty of 10% of any stolen funds returned, capped at 3 BTC.

#5216. Binance says thinly traded tokens are delaying full replacement of stolen assets

Binance attributes its last reported 5% replenishment gap to less-liquid assets, while its November 3 withdrawal plan reopens routes progressively.

Binance said in its Nov. 2 recovery update that less-liquid tokens were slowing the replacement of stolen assets. It put the outstanding replenishment at approximately 5% of the impacted amount, while a separate withdrawal phase was scheduled for Nov. 3.

The new phase was scheduled to begin at 04:00 UTC. In Binance's Nov. 3 update, the exchange said availability would expand progressively as security and readiness checks were completed for individual assets and networks.

Together, the disclosures explain why a commitment to cover stolen funds can coexist with unfinished recovery. Funding the purchases, obtaining the specific tokens and restoring the routes through which customers withdraw them are separate tasks.

The replenishment percentage, dated reserve proof and asset-network reopening answer different questions.

Binance said proof for ten tokens at the October 31 snapshot was complete on November 2. This concerns backing at that snapshot.

The November 3 plan reopens asset-network pairs progressively after security checks. These examples do not establish current availability.

A replenishment percentage does not establish that a particular withdrawal route is available.

Binance's statements are attributed reporting. The denominator's valuation basis is unspecified. No live route status or unreplenished token identity is established; the October 31 proof concerns ten named tokens.

Source-bound comparison of Binance's disclosures. The approximate 95/5 bar represents the stated shares of the impacted amount, not token counts, dollars or withdrawal routes. Named pairs are examples from the planned November 3 table.

Binance's statements are attributed reporting. The denominator's valuation basis is unspecified. No live route status or unreplenished token identity is established; the October 31 proof concerns ten named tokens.

Binance reported approximately 95% replenishment of the impacted amount in its Nov. 1 recovery update. It repeated that figure on Nov. 2, attributing the outstanding portion to tokens outside the ten assets it said were fully backed: BTC, ETH, USDT, BNB, SOL, USDC, XRP, USD1, RLUSD and ZEC.

Start with the stories that matter. Get the context behind the headlines and a clear view of what to watch next.

Thanks for signing up to The Catalyst. Look out for your first daily email. If you already receive it, you’re all set.

The exchange said the remaining assets were less liquid and took longer to source in the required quantities. It described continued purchases through market and over-the-counter channels, alongside work with industry partners to finish the acquisitions.

In its Oct. 31 update, Binance identified corporate treasury, a planned partial deployment of its Secure Asset Fund for Users and a portion of its futures insurance resources as recovery funding sources.

The percentage also has a narrower meaning than a general recovery score. Binance describes the denominator as the impacted amount. It does not specify the valuation basis in those disclosures, so the remaining 5% cannot safely be converted into a dollar shortfall. Nor does 95% describe the proportion of tokens or withdrawal routes restored.

Binance's Nov. 2 update said the zero-knowledge proof for its Oct. 31, 00:00 UTC reserve snapshot was complete. It said holders of the ten named tokens could independently verify that their balances were backed at least one-for-one.

The proof concerns a defined set of assets at a dated snapshot. Backing at that snapshot does not establish present availability of every withdrawal route.

The latest withdrawal table illustrates why the network matters alongside the token. It includes RLUSD on Ethereum and XRP, and USDC on Sui, among routes planned for progressive reopening. Those entries identify the scope of the rollout, not which tokens remain unreplenished or whether each route is currently operational.

Binance said it was prioritizing tokens according to demand over the preceding week and restoring each network-asset pair after security checks.

For holders, the immediate check is the chosen asset and network. Binance directed users to its withdrawal page or network-status page to confirm availability, and warned that rollout traffic could cause temporary delays or longer processing times.

A separate transparency milestone is still ahead. Binance said it planned a Nov. 4 reserve snapshot covering more tokens, followed by proof generation estimated to take approximately 40 to 50 hours.

Binance reiterated that it would bear the financial impact of the stolen funds rather than pass losses on to users.

#5215. Ex-XRP Treasury Reveals Exit From $5 Million Stake in Ripple

Disclaimer: The opinions expressed by our writers are their own and do not represent the views of U.Today. The financial and market information provided on U.Today is intended for informational purposes only. U.Today is not liable for any financial losses incurred while trading cryptocurrencies. Conduct your own research by contacting financial experts before making any investment decisions. We believe that all content is accurate as of the date of publication, but certain offers mentioned may no longer be available.

A fresh annual report of British company VivoPower PLC, filed with the U.S. Securities and Exchange Commission (SEC) on November 2, 2026, officially disclosed the parameters of the closure of its cryptocurrency strategy.

The documents recorded the creation and subsequent liquidation of a $5 million stake in the private company Ripple Labs, while also making public the price of their buyback at $300 apiece.

According to the filing, in May 2025, VivoPower announced a large-scale treasury expansion with a target investment volume of up to $100 million in XRP tokens and Ripple Labs equity. In practice, the investments turned out to be more modest: the XRP treasury allocated $5.0 million to the direct acquisition of 41,810 Ripple Labs shares in January 2026.

For institutional investors, buying stakes in the fintech giant itself was a key way to make a large bet on the ecosystem's success.

However, the experiment lasted only nine months from its announcement. As early as February, VivoPower's board of directors decided to fully wind down the crypto direction. Management recognized holding digital assets as less profitable than developing artificial intelligence infrastructure and redirected capital to the purchase of a 41.5 MW data center in Mo i Rana, Norway, for $37.7 million.

The process of liquidating this $5 million stake brought the crypto market an important price insight. In April 2026, Ripple Labs conducted a targeted buyback, taking 882 shares from VivoPower at $300 apiece. The deal brought the British company $264,600 in cash and $159,122 in net profit, giving the community a rare and hard marker of how Ripple's management itself values its business in private trades.

A direct sale of the remaining part of the stake on the open market proved impossible due to the non-public status of the securities. As a result, VivoPower entered into agreements to sell the economic rights to the shares to two South Korean partners, KWeather Co. and Lean Ventures.

As of the filing date, the Korean deals have not yet been legally completed. The remaining 40,928 shares are locked in the Korea Securities Depository (KSD) under a strict one-year lock-up restriction. Due to this freeze and the lack of market liquidity, independent appraisers lowered the book value of this stake to $4.3 million (about $106.16 per share).

The VivoPower story clearly showed the crypto industry the flip side of institutional investments: a direct purchase of stakes in Ripple Labs comes with strict lock-ups and low liquidity, where the only fast source of fiat for exiting the ecosystem is the issuer itself through a private buyback procedure.

#5213. Strategy Resumes Bitcoin Buys With $66M Purchase

Strategy Resumes Bitcoin Buys With $66M Purchase

Strategy bought 811 BTC for $65.5 million last week, the company said on Monday, resuming its Bitcoin purchases after a one-week pause.

The Bitcoin treasury firm paid an average of $80,846 per coin between October 26 and November 1, funding the purchases by selling 1,178,552 shares of MSTR stock for $164 million in net proceeds, according to an SEC filing. Of that, $65.5 million went to Bitcoin and $98.5 million to its USD Cash account.

Strategy has acquired 811 $BTC and repurchased $208M of $STRC. As of 11/1/26, we hold 849,327 BTC and $4.9B of USD Assets. $MSTRhttps://t.co/fk7kWJxf3L

Strategy now holds 849,327 BTC, acquired for a total of $64.08 billion at an average of about $75,451 per coin.

The company also bought back 2,092,035 shares of STRC, its variable-rate preferred stock, for $208.2 million, all paid from USD Cash. That is up from $204.9 million the previous week, when Strategy paused its Bitcoin buying for the first time since mid-September. The week before that, it bought 267 BTC for $22.1 million.

USD Cash, the pool Strategy keeps for general purposes including buying Bitcoin, fell to $132.7 million from $242.4 million a week earlier. Its separate USD Reserve, which covers preferred dividends and interest on its debt, stood at $4.79 billion after paying out $43.4 million in dividends. About $748.6 million remains available under its program for repurchasing preferred stock.

Strategy put its combined USD assets at $4.9 billion and its "USD Duration," its measure of how long those assets could cover dividends and interest, at 3.3 years, it tweeted.

The purchases come as STRC moves to a new dividend schedule. Shareholders approved daily dividends on Strategy's four preferred stocks last week, with STRC's first daily record date falling on November 1.

Mizuho Financial Group maintained its Outperform rating and $165 price target on MSTR following Strategy's third-quarter earnings call, pointing to a stronger balance sheet with net leverage down to 2.4% from 8.4%, according to a summary of the note. But analyst Dan Dolev also flagged dilution concerns, noting that Bitcoin per share has fallen 3.7% this year.

Strategy also faces possible removal from MSCI's equity indexes under a rule the index provider finalized in October, which treats companies dominated by a single asset as ineligible. MSCI will publish the list of affected companies on November 11.

#5211. Tesla, Amazon Deal Fresh Blow to OKX Tokenization Push

OKX has suffered another big blow to its grand aspirations of tokenizing stocks after Tesla, Amazon, and four other big names turned down plans for the tokenization of their shares on the cryptocurrency exchange’s joint venture with New York Stock Exchange owner Intercontinental Exchange (ICE).

Six new companies have raised objections to their shares being tokenized on the OKXICE platform.

Among the new objectors are Elon Musk’s Tesla and SpaceX, e-commerce giant Amazon, streaming giant Netflix, semiconductor equipment maker Lam Research and fintech firm SoFi Technologies.

The new objections bring the total number of companies that have objected to the initiative to 56, further damaging OKX’s push into the realm of traditional finance.

In particular, five of the Magnificent Seven tech companies have turned down the proposal to have their stocks tokenized on the OKXICE platform. These include Tesla, Amazon, Microsoft, Alphabet and Nvidia.

What makes this rejection even more noteworthy is that the total number of targeted companies involved in this OKXICE joint venture is relatively low.

Back in early October, OKX and ICE announced their plan to offer tokenized versions of 62 major companies’ stocks.

However, 49 out of those 62 companies have formally opposed the proposal. Hence, about 79% of the initially targeted companies have rejected the plan. The other seven objections came from companies not in the initial selection.

Some of the most famous names in American finance and tech, including JPMorgan Chase, Goldman Sachs, Walmart, Coca-Cola, Intel, Oracle, AMD, Nike and Costco, have also expressed their objections.

As of the Nov. 2 notice, only 13 out of the original selection had yet to raise a publicly disclosed objection.

These names include Apple, Coinbase, Robinhood, Strategy, Circle and Palantir.

However, the fact that they have not raised a formal objection doesn’t necessarily mean they approve of the tokenization plan.

It is worth noting that Nov. 3 marks the end of the 30-day objection period for the companies that received their initial notice on Oct. 4.

Nonetheless, with almost four-fifths of the initially targeted companies raising objections, OKXICE faces an uphill battle to get sufficient corporate involvement to make its tokenized stock market appealing to investors.

OKXICE has not set a definite launch date yet. It also has not disclosed the number of tokenized stocks it will offer when it starts trading.

#5209. Bitcoin investors could pay the price as the Federal Reserve tightens bank lending rules

The Fed's Nov. 2 survey shows tougher approvals alongside stronger nonbank demand, with no evidence of forced Bitcoin sales.

Banks tightened loan approval standards for nonbank financial firms while reporting stronger demand from business lenders and private-equity funds, according to the Federal Reserve's Nov. 2 lending survey.

That combination points to a financing constraint that could limit investors' appetite for Bitcoin beyond changes in the Fed's policy rate.

For business lenders, classified as business credit intermediaries in the survey, 12 of 50 responding domestic banks tightened standards, one eased, and 37 left them unchanged. The response table shows 24% tightening versus 2% easing, for a net tightening of 22%.

The October Senior Loan Officer Opinion Survey covers the preceding three months, generally the third quarter. Responses were due Oct. 5, well before the late-October spread widening.

Domestic banks reported net tightening across all five nonbank borrower categories surveyed. They most often cited a less favorable or more uncertain economic outlook and deterioration in borrowers' financial strength.

At the same time, banks reported stronger demand from business credit intermediaries and private-equity funds in a question measuring funds actually disbursed.

Among banks reporting stronger nonbank demand, common reasons included working-capital or liquidity needs, refinancing, and financial investment opportunities. Banks can become more selective even as borrowers seek financing for both cash needs and investments.

A minority of banks tightened business-lender standards, producing a 22-point net balance. The survey and market indicators describe different periods.

Approval tightening is real, but neither its bank-share measure nor these mismatched dates establishes Bitcoin selling.

Survey covers Q3, with responses due Oct. 5. NFCI describes the week ending Oct. 23; spreads compare Oct. 28 and 30, the latest data exposed Nov. 3 morning. These separate measures do not establish Bitcoin selling.

Method: One square represents one of 50 banks answering Q29B: 12 tightened, 37 unchanged, 1 eased. Verified arithmetic: 12/50=24%, 1/50=2%, and (12−1)/50=22 percentage points net. Spread change: (3.44−3.14)×100=30 basis points. Date positions are schematic, not a time-proportional or causal scale.

Approval standards determine access to credit, so financing can become harder to obtain without a new policy-rate increase. The Fed held its target range at 3.75% to 4% on Oct. 28, a separate decision from the earlier lending conditions captured by the survey.

If financing becomes harder to secure while liquidity needs rise, investors may have less capacity or willingness to hold risky assets. A 2023 IMF working paper found that US monetary tightening historically reduced a common crypto-price factor through that channel.

The ICE BofA US high-yield option-adjusted spread rose from 3.14% on Oct. 28 to 3.44% on Oct. 30, a widening of 30 basis points.

The Chicago Fed National Financial Conditions Index was -0.487 for the week ending Oct. 23, indicating conditions looser than average.

A stronger signal of spillover would be continued widening in credit premiums alongside weakening Bitcoin demand, while narrowing spreads and resilient demand would weaken that case. For now, the survey shows a financing constraint worth watching.

#5208. Ripple CEO Brad Garlinghouse Breaks Silence on XRP vs. RLUSD Debate

Ripple CEO Brad Garlinghouse addressed the ongoing debate over the potential impact of Ripple’s RLUSD stablecoin on XRP’s future, asserting that both assets are complementary rather than competitive.

On Nov. 3, Brad Garlinghouse offered his view in response to a comprehensive X post from a notable member of the XRP Ledger community named Vet, who outlined the key differences between the two assets associated with Ripple.

“Well said, Vet. Complementary, not competitive,” the Ripple chief executive tweeted.

The brief statement comes amid pushback as some XRP holders are increasingly concerned that Ripple’s aggressive foray into the stablecoin market may negatively impact demand for XRP in the future.

As part of his viral post, Vet explained that the key difference between the two assets revolves around the purposes they serve on the XRP Ledger.

“RLUSD is optional. XRP isn’t,” the commentator explained.

As the XRP Ledger’s native currency, XRP is essential for the core functions of the network. First, every account must have an XRP reserve. Second, transaction fees are paid in XRP.

RLUSD, on the other hand, is a dollar-denominated stablecoin issued by Ripple's regulated stablecoin business. The key difference is that, unlike XRP, the value of RLUSD is supposed to remain stable at roughly $1.

Another key difference lies in control over the assets.

Ripple has the ability to freeze RLUSD under certain conditions, for example, to meet legal obligations. There is no central entity that can freeze XRP, the native cryptocurrency, on the ledger.

According to Vet, another key advantage of XRP is its privileged status on the XRP Ledger's DEX.

The XRP Ledger has an automatic bridging function. In this way, if it makes sense to trade between currencies through XRP for a better exchange rate, trades can be automatically routed through XRP.

This is why XRP is so valuable for liquidity providers and market makers as an intermediary asset.

On the other hand, RLUSD would be much more suitable in cases where price stability is important, for example, for payroll, invoicing, corporate payments, etc.

At the same time, some XRP investors are worried that one day RLUSD may effectively replace XRP for some payment use cases.

The crucial question is this: If you can send a dollar-denominated stablecoin directly, why would you need to use an intermediary like XRP?

It’s also worth noting that RLUSD isn’t exclusive to the XRP Ledger. In fact, RLUSD also works on Ethereum and other supported blockchains. So increasing demand for RLUSD doesn’t necessarily lead to more use of the XRP Ledger.

And even if we’re seeing more RLUSD transactions on the XRP Ledger, this doesn’t automatically mean that increased demand for XRP to pay transaction fees necessarily guarantees a significant price surge for the cryptocurrency.

It’s not the first time Garlinghouse has been forced to comment on Ripple's changing priorities.

During Ripple’s Swell 2026 conference in New York on Oct. 28, Ripple’s CEO directly addressed rumors that Ripple is becoming increasingly focused on RLUSD rather than XRP.

“We will never give up on XRP,” Garlinghouse said.

#5207. Morning Minute: Binance Hack Leads to Mass Outflows While FBI Opens Probe

Morning Minute: Binance Hack Leads to Mass Outflows While FBI Opens Probe

Morning Minute is a daily newsletter written by Tyler Warner. The analysis and opinions expressed are his own and do not necessarily reflect those of Decrypt. And check out our daily news show 'FOMO HOUR' covering all of the top stories and market action.

💸 The Binance Hack Just Cost Bitcoin ETFs Their Whole Month

Through Thursday, October was a quiet, slightly positive month for Bitcoin ETFs. Then North Korea took $6.4 billion out of Binance five minutes after the closing bell, and Friday’s single session erased the entire month.

To recap the Binance hack, attackers drained roughly $6.4 billion from Binance’s hot wallets across about 10 blockchains, with most of it inside 20 minutes on Thursday afternoon. That’s more than four times the old record, the $1.5 billion Bybit theft in February 2025. Both firms attribute it to TraderTraitor, the North Korea-linked group that also hit Bybit.

The entry point has changed since Friday. Binance first blamed a compromised laptop belonging to a DevOps engineer, then found “additional points of breach.” On Ethereum, the attacker used 50 pre-signed authorizations to turn a Binance hot wallet into a contract that emptied itself—a failure of Binance’s own signing approvals, not of Ethereum. No private keys leaked. The attacker wiped its tooling on the way out.

Friday was the aftermath, and it was bloody. Bitcoin ETFs bled $643.3 million on Friday, the biggest day of the month, ending a four-session buying streak. That one day took October from +$127 million to −$515.9 million. September was +$2.65 billion. The ETH ETFs were worse: −$1.61 billion for October, their worst month since launch, and an 11-session losing streak into the weekend.

Now the biggest players in the U.S. government are reacting. CFTC Chairman Michael Selig told CNBC the hack “proves our exact point.” “The real issue here is that these are unregulated offshore exchanges, and they don’t have the same sorts of safeguards.” He’s pitching a “federal option”—let offshore venues register with the CFTC and meet US cybersecurity standards. He also notes the hole came in “from someone’s laptop,” not from a blockchain. The FBI and Secret Service have also both opened probes.

Binance CEO Richard Teng says the company covers every dollar from corporate funds and SAFU, with $6.34 billion already set aside. CZ: “It hurts, but we survive.” Withdrawals were turned back on over the weekend and over $3.6B has exited so far according to QCP.

So what to watch from here? Monday’s inflows (or outflows) will be telling. If Friday’s bleeding continues to Monday, it’s a signal that the pain might just be beginning. Also, watch Binance. They have to buy roughly $1.3 billion of BTC, ETH, and BNB on the open market and OTC to top off the reimbursement pool—a forced bid underneath a market that just got hit. And then there’s Selig and the FBI; be on the lookout from updates from those agencies as the Binance hack aftermath carries forward.

#5204. Binance relied on AI to fight fraud before hackers exploited internal tool in $6 billion theft

Attackers exploited an internal AI system to reach wallet engineers, prompting the exchange to seek security partnerships with leading AI companies.

Hackers exploited Binance's internal artificial intelligence tools to compromise engineers before stealing $6.34 billion in cryptocurrency, the exchange disclosed.

In its Nov. 3 security report, Binance said attackers used a compromised employee computer and an internal AI tool on Oct. 20 to take control of two engineers responsible for its wallet infrastructure.

The attackers subsequently obtained access to a container deployment permission token, which helped them penetrate the exchange's wallet-signing operations on Oct. 29 and initiate unauthorized transactions across several blockchain networks.

The disclosure puts AI at the center of one of the largest cryptocurrency thefts on record, adding to concerns about how increasingly connected AI systems can introduce new vulnerabilities into corporate infrastructure.

Binance's investigation does not establish that an AI model autonomously planned or executed the theft. Rather, the attackers exploited an internal AI tool as part of a broader campaign involving stolen credentials, compromised developer software, and social engineering.

In June, a Binance engineer downloaded a tampered software installer while attempting to install a popular AI coding tool. Binance detected the suspicious activity, replaced the device and rotated associated credentials, although information about the employee and colleagues had already been exposed.

In September, another engineer fell victim to a fake recruitment operation involving staged job interviews and a malicious coding assignment. The malware compromised the employee's computer and gave the attackers access, which they later used to move deeper into Binance's systems.

The FBI has attributed the broader attack to North Korea, whose hacking operations have increasingly targeted software developers and cryptocurrency businesses.

The Binance incident follows months of warnings from cybersecurity researchers that artificial intelligence is expanding both hackers' capabilities and the systems available for exploitation.

In February, Google's Mandiant reported that North Korean hackers targeting the cryptocurrency industry had incorporated AI-assisted techniques into their operations.

The researchers documented an intrusion involving a fake video conference, malicious software, and a suspected AI-generated video impersonating a corporate executive. They could not independently confirm the deepfake in that particular incident but identified the group's use of AI tools for reconnaissance and social engineering.

By September, Google's Threat Intelligence Group reported that attackers were moving beyond using AI to generate malicious content toward automated operations that could execute multiple stages of an intrusion.

In one case, hackers compromised a cloud resource and organized an AI-assisted mass credential-harvesting campaign in less than six hours. Google also identified attackers attempting to manipulate AI coding assistants and security scanners during software supply-chain compromises.

The findings closely resemble one aspect of the Binance breach: developers and the AI-powered tools they depend on are becoming targets attackers use to reach sensitive systems.

Separately, AI developer Anthropic disclosed in September that criminals and suspected state-sponsored operators had used its Claude models to conduct reconnaissance, develop exploitation tools, and automate data theft.

Some operations involved AI agents carrying out multiple stages of an attack with limited human supervision, reducing the technical expertise and manpower traditionally required.

The exchange is now seeking support from the same industry whose technologies were featured in the attack.

Binance said it is working to establish a security research fund and develop targeted initiatives with leading AI companies, blockchain industry participants and cybersecurity firms.

The proposed collaboration will focus on strengthening AI security in blockchain environments, although the exchange has not disclosed the fund's proposed size, participating companies or launch date.

It also plans to expand spending on AI-enabled threat detection, endpoint protection, developer-environment security and behavioral anomaly monitoring.

The exchange said it has already rebuilt the affected internal AI tool and wallet infrastructure, replaced compromised employee devices and strengthened access controls.

Binance has also commissioned an independent cybersecurity review to validate its investigation and identify additional safeguards.

The company said approximately $2.85 billion of the $6.34 billion in affected digital assets has been frozen or intercepted, while 98% of impacted token assets had been replenished as of Oct. 31.

The independent review has yet to establish the full extent of the weaknesses that allowed attackers to exploit the exchange's internal AI environment. Its findings will inform the security changes Binance plans to implement as it develops the proposed industry fund.

XRP's correction keeps dragging on. The token is trading near $1.28, down from its September highs around $1.65. After a strong recovery in August and September, XRP has struggled to keep its bullish momentum. Selling pressure hasn't eased, and sentiment keeps getting worse.

The daily chart shows a series of lower highs and lower lows, so sellers are firmly in control of the short-term trend. XRP is also trading below its major moving averages, which makes the bearish picture even stronger. Any recovery attempt could run into heavy resistance.

The closest support is between $1.24 and $1.25, where buyers have recently tried to set a temporary floor. A clear break below this zone could push XRP down to $1.20, and then to the psychologically important $1.15 level.

The Relative Strength Index (RSI) is hovering in the lower half of its range. Momentum is bearish, but selling pressure may be close to running out. However, there's no clear bullish divergence yet, so it's too early to talk about a proper reversal.

Trading volume has dropped a lot compared with the spike during August's rally. This points to fewer active traders and weak buyer conviction.

The short-term outlook stays bearish. XRP needs a proper move above $1.35 to show any real improvement.

If buyers defend $1.24, XRP could recover toward $1.3-$1.35. If this support breaks, XRP could drop further toward $1.20 and possibly $1.15. Until XRP reclaims its key moving averages and starts making higher lows, it's too early to call a recovery.

NEAR Protocol (NEAR) has come a long way down from its October peak near $6.00 and is now trading around $3.91. After a strong September, NEAR is heading down. Selling pressure is growing, and buyers are losing confidence.

On the daily chart, the short-term picture keeps getting worse, with lower highs and lower lows. NEAR has fallen below its short- and medium-term moving averages, so bears are in control for now. However, the price is still above its longer-term moving averages, which means the bigger recovery isn't fully broken yet.

The closest support is between $3.75 and $3.85, where buyers are trying to stop the drop. A clear break below $3.75 could send NEAR to the next support around $3.60, and possibly down to $3.30.

On the upside, the first resistance zone is between $4.20 and $4.35, where several moving averages meet. A proper recovery above this zone could bring buyers back and push NEAR toward $4.60 and then $4.80.

The Relative Strength Index (RSI) has slipped toward the lower half of its range. Momentum is weakening, but the market isn't deeply oversold yet. Trading volume is also moderate compared with September's breakout, a sign that speculators have cooled off.

The short-term outlook stays bearish, especially while NEAR is below $4.35. Still, the longer-term moving averages nearby could act as support and trigger a technical bounce.

If buyers defend $3.75, the price is more likely to stabilize. A confirmed break below it could speed up the correction toward $3.60.

For now, it's too early to expect a proper bullish reversal without a solid reaction at support and stronger buying volume.

Zcash (ZEC) is trading around $1,208 after a huge rally that sent the coin from around $500 in August to a peak near $1,700 in late September. Since then, ZEC has been moving sideways, with buyers and sellers fighting over the next move.

On the daily chart, ZEC is still above its longer-term moving averages, which is a good sign that the bigger bullish trend is intact. However, short-term momentum is getting weaker. ZEC is struggling to hold above the $1,300 resistance zone.

The closest support is between $1,150 and $1,180, where buyers have stepped in several times. A clear break below this area could speed up the selling toward $1,100, and then toward the psychologically important $1,000 level.

On the upside, the first resistance is at $1,250, followed by a stronger $1,300-1,350 supply zone. A daily close above $1,350 could give bulls more momentum and open the way to $1,450 and $1,550.

The Relative Strength Index (RSI) stays near the neutral 50 level, which shows the market hasn't picked a direction yet. Trading volume has also dropped compared with September's rally, suggesting that speculative trading activity is back to it.

The short-term outlook stays mildly bullish as long as the $1,150 support holds. A proper recovery above $1,300 would confirm that buyers are back. A drop below $1,100 could mean a deeper correction.

Cardano (ADA) just can't catch a break, it's trading near $0.1908 after a long slide from its October highs around $0.28. The daily chart keeps getting worse, with lower highs, lower lows, and repeated failures to get back above key moving averages.

After a short recovery in September and early October, ADA hit strong resistance between $0.25 and $0.28. The rejection brought the bears back and pushed ADA below several important technical indicators.

ADA is now trading below its short-, medium-, and long-term moving averages, which makes the bearish outlook even stronger. These averages are pointing down, so sellers are clearly in control.

The closest support is around $0.185–$0.190, where the price is trying to find a temporary floor. A confirmed break below $0.185 could send ADA to the $0.175 support, and then to the stronger $0.160–$0.165 demand zone.

Buyers need to reclaim $0.200 to show the first signs of recovery. Stronger resistance sits between $0.215 and $0.230, where several moving averages meet. A proper breakout above $0.230 would be a real improvement for the chart.

The Relative Strength Index (RSI) is getting close to oversold territory, which shows the downside momentum isn't fading. This can come before short-term relief rallies, but on its own it doesn't confirm a bullish reversal.

Trading volume is still fairly low, so buyers don't seem convinced even after the recent drop. The short-term outlook for ADA stays bearish as long as the price is below $0.215.

If buyers defend $0.185, ADA could bounce toward $0.2-$0.215. If this support breaks, ADA is more likely to drop further toward $0.175 and possibly $0.160.

Until ADA makes higher lows and reclaims its major moving averages, any move up looks more like a short bounce than a proper bullish reversal.

#5202. How T7X Is Building the Infrastructure for a New Era of Capital Markets

How T7X Is Building the Infrastructure for a New Era of Capital Markets

Most tokenized stocks aren't natively issued on a blockchain. They begin as conventional shares, sit with a broker or custodian, and are then represented by tokens issued separately. In effect, the market has largely replicated traditional securities structures on-chain instead of rethinking them from the ground up. The token "may track the value of the underlying shares," writes Ivan Kan, CMO of tokenization platform T7X, in an essay shared with Decrypt, "but the token holder is not necessarily the shareholder of record."

While the holder owns the price movement, whether they own the share is another matter altogether. The SEC set out three models in January: issuers tokenizing their own shares, third parties issuing tokens against shares held in custody, and firms issuing a separate security for synthetic exposure.

Writing for Forbes' Technology Council in August, Kan cited Pantera Capital research covering 593 tokenized assets across 11 asset classes, of which 77.6% were what Pantera called "digital wrappers around traditional infrastructure" rather than securities in their own right.

"What does the token legally represent, and who authorized it?" Kan asks, setting out an alternative model that T7X Equities is pursuing, which they dub "native issuance." A security exists because an issuer authorizes it, they argue, and "blockchain does not eliminate those corporate and securities-law requirements."

Under the native issuance model, the token is part of the authorized issuance from the start, running from issuer authorization to investor verification to transfer-agent validation, aiming at "one security, one authorized issuance, one ownership record, one digital representation."

Wyoming, Delaware, and Nevada corporate law recognizes a "certificate token," allowing shares to be represented electronically on a blockchain. T7X is incorporated in Wyoming and structures its issuances to operate within this framework. The blockchain then becomes "part of the infrastructure supporting the capitalization table" instead of being just another instrument layered on top.

The transfer agent keeps the official master securityholder file and the blockchain records transactions, while the legal record of ownership stays where regulators expect it.

In T7X's administrated offerings, that transfer agent is T7X Equities. Transfer agents register with the SEC and hold the authoritative record of who owns a security, processing transfers and maintaining the master file, which puts the company inside the model it is advocating as well as behind it. Its first example is Tranquil Healthcare Fund I, a Tier 2 Regulation A+ offering of up to $75 million that the SEC qualified on June 29. T7X Equities issues tokens representing its shares on Trusted Smart Chain and delivers them to investors' wallets, the company said.

"There's a registered, SEC-regulated gatekeeper that determines who owns which security at any point in time," T7X Chief Executive Pablo Penaloza said at Consensus in Miami earlier this year. "As long as you have a transfer agent involved, there is no way that securities can disappear without trace."

Kan calls the broader category "RWA+." The plus doesn't refer to any single rule, he argues, but the requirement that "compliance and oversight are real," and it rests on three elements. Native issuance creates the token as part of the security's authorized issuance. Integrated compliance writes investor verification and disclosure into the instrument from the start, not as an afterthought. Legally enforceable ownership puts the token holder on the transfer agent's record.

"A receipt is not an asset," Kan wrote in Forbes. "It is a claim wrapped around one, dependent on an intermediary to honor it." Synthetic "PreStocks" tied to SpaceX and Anthropic were never valid claims, he noted, because neither company authorized the transfers. Done properly, he argues, "ownership, compliance and settlement are the same fact."

Whether a token carries the rights of a share is becoming the dividing line elsewhere, too. The SEC's new innovation exemption covers tokenized stocks carrying dividends and voting rights while excluding price-tracking synthetics, and Nasdaq's arrangement with Kraken turns on tokens conveying the same voting rights as ordinary shares.

Penaloza pins the near-term opportunity on companies raising under $100 million, usually between $50 million and $75 million, through Regulation A offerings.

Regulation A+, Kan argues, suits this model because it permits public solicitation and non-accredited participation while requiring audited financials and continuing disclosure. Most tokenized assets rely instead on Regulation D placements, limited to accredited investors. Reg A also opens the door, Penaloza notes, to retirement accounts. Unregistered securities can legally sit in a self-directed IRA, but many brokers won't hold them and most investment advisers steer clients away. "The money is sitting in the older generations' retirement accounts," he said, and a registration attached to the investment is what makes an individual retiree comfortable holding it.

For investors already holding these securities, the benefit is an exit. Shareholders in Reg A and Reg D placements typically receive a paper certificate and then "have to go around and find somebody who will want to give them liquidity," Penaloza said. A native digital security can trade on a secondary market, he said, settling wallet to wallet without a custodian in between.

Giving Reg A and Reg D holdings somewhere to trade would, he argued, "incredibly speed up not only the rate of capital formation, but also the liquidity in the market." Within five years, he predicts, "the total tokenized portfolio will far surpass the existing market capitalization of what is currently trading on all the public markets."

To find out more about T7X’s real-world asset tokenization platform, head to t7x.com.

#5199. Coinbase XRP looked cheap on Nov. 2 despite identical market value

At the Nov. 2 close, an independent comparison narrows XRP’s Coinbase discount from 0.168% to 0.006% after converting USDT into dollars.

XRP’s apparent Coinbase discount at the Nov. 2 close shrinks from about 0.168% to 0.006% when Binance’s Tether (USDT) quote is converted into dollars. The independent exchange-price comparison separates the quote-currency effect from the remaining difference in XRP prices.

The user Arab Chain on CryptoQuant reported an approximately -0.16% XRP Coinbase Premium Index reading on Nov. 2, describing it as an eight-month low and interpreting it as relatively weaker Coinbase demand.

The post also cautioned that the index alone was not definitive evidence of continued downside pressure.

The completed Nov. 2 candles put Coinbase XRP/USD at $1.2481, Binance XRP/USDT at 1.2502 USDT and Coinbase USDT/USD at $0.99838.

The adjustment multiplies Binance’s XRP price by USDT’s dollar price, then compares Coinbase against that converted figure. Expressed as a percentage, the formula is:

100 × [Coinbase XRP/USD ÷ (Binance XRP/USDT × USDT/USD) − 1].

This uses Coinbase’s USDT/USD market as the conversion reference. It is a venue price, rather than a universal redemption rate or evidence about why USDT traded below one dollar.

At the November 2 close, the gap shrinks after converting USDT into dollars. October 31 shows why the adjustment does not always leave near parity.

November 2, 2026 UTC close: raw -0.168%; dollar-adjusted -0.006%. The remaining dollar-price gap is close to zero.

October 31, 2026 UTC close: raw -0.200%; dollar-adjusted -0.078%. A residual discount remains after conversion.

Normalize the quote currency first. Then ask whether a remaining venue-price discount persists.

Closing trades are asynchronous, not executable quotes. Coinbase USDT/USD is a venue reference. Fees, spreads and rounding matter. This does not reproduce CryptoQuant’s proprietary index or identify selling counterparties.

The raw percentage compares Coinbase’s dollar close directly with Binance’s USDT close. The adjusted percentage first multiplies Binance’s close by Coinbase USDT/USD, then divides Coinbase XRP/USD by that product, subtracts one and multiplies by 100. Completed UTC days only. Labels and bars use three-decimal percentages on a shared scale from minus 0.25 percent to zero.

Closing trades are asynchronous, not executable quotes. Coinbase USDT/USD is a venue reference. Fees, spreads and rounding matter. This does not reproduce CryptoQuant’s proprietary index or identify selling counterparties.

Across all seven completed UTC daily observations, the adjustment narrowed the discount, but did not eliminate it every day:

Thanks for signing up to The Catalyst. Look out for your first daily email. If you already receive it, you’re all set.

Negative values mean Coinbase was cheaper, while positive values mean it was more expensive. Figures are rounded to three decimal places from the three exchange datasets.

Oct. 30 and 31 adjusted discounts remained about 0.066% and 0.078%, respectively, and the Nov. 2 finding supports checking the currency conversion.

A separate check used 60 matched one-minute buckets from Nov. 2, 12:00-12:59 UTC, combining Coinbase XRP/USD, Binance XRP/USDT and Coinbase USDT/USD. Its median raw gap was about -0.128%, and the median dollar-adjusted gap was approximately +0.006%.

XRP was near $1.28 on Nov. 3. Around 13:15 UTC, Coinbase’s ticker reported a $1.276 last trade, above its $1.2481 Nov. 2 close, while Binance’s rolling ticker showed 1.2780 USDT.

A relative-demand warning would be more persuasive if a discount persisted after currency conversion alongside comparatively heavier seller-initiated XRP trading over equivalent windows.

The measurement stops short of an executable arbitrage test. Coinbase defines candle closes as the last trade within each bucket, while Binance’s candles use matching time intervals. Rounding, bid-ask spreads, fees, and differences between venues are important when a residual gap is this small.

An August 2025 XRP liquidity study exposed a similar disconnect: XRP prices remained closely aligned across Binance and Coinbase even as trading activity was concentrated in different quote currencies.

Price consistency does not guarantee equally accessible liquidity, and traders may see nearly identical quotes but face substantially different execution costs and slippage, particularly during periods of market stress.

On Nov. 2’s closing observations, the unadjusted gap by itself substantially overstates the remaining dollar-price difference.

#5198. Bitmine Buys More Ethereum as Tom Lee Calls the Binance Hack Bullish

Bitmine Buys More Ethereum as Tom Lee Calls the Binance Hack Bullish

Bitmine Immersion Technologies kept its weekly Ethereum buying streak intact, adding 8,573 ETH worth roughly $19 million, while chairman Tom Lee used the record Binance hack to argue that crypto has grown more resilient, not more fragile.

The NYSE-listed company said Monday its holdings now total 6,060,506 ETH, worth roughly $13.7 billion at a reference price of $2,261 per coin.

That represents 4.9% of Ethereum’s 122.2 million token supply, leaving Bitmine, by its own count, 99% of the way to its goal of owning 5% of the network, a target it brands the “Alchemy of 5%,” reached in just 15 months. The firm has bought Ethereum every week since launching its treasury strategy on June 30, 2025.

Most of Lee’s commentary focused on last week’s roughly $6.4 billion theft from Binance, which the FBI has attributed to North Korea. Lee called it “a terrible development” but argued the market’s “relatively muted” reaction was “very constructive.” (Disclosure: Tom Lee is one of several investors in Dastan, Decrypt’s parent company.)

Lee noted that when North Korea’s Lazarus-linked hackers drained $1.5 billion from Bybit in February 2025, Ethereum fell 8% that day and 20% within a week, whereas ETH slid just 4% after the Binance breach, despite the theft being roughly four times larger.

Lee laid out six reasons for the muted response: Binance’s ability to cover all customer losses, the absence of a cascade of DeFi failures like the one that followed Bybit, swift industry action that cut the potential loss in half, the FBI naming North Korea within two days rather than the months such attributions have taken historically, the fact that the exploit was a “corporate infiltration” of Binance’s infrastructure rather than a blockchain flaw, and the limited price downside itself, which he read as a sign a “crypto bull market is underway.”

Taken together, he said, the episode shows the industry is “increasingly resilient and more capable of absorbing these nation-state assaults.”

Counting cash, other tokens and what it calls “moonshot” investments, Bitmine’s total holdings stood at $14.5 billion as of Nov. 1, including 216 Bitcoin, $498 million in cash and marketable securities, a $180 million stake in Beast Industries and an $82 million position in Eightco Holdings, which it bills as a rare public proxy for indirect OpenAI exposure.

Bitmine remains the world’s largest Ethereum treasury and the second-largest crypto treasury overall, trailing only Michael Saylor’s Strategy, which the release said holds about 848,517 BTC.

Strategy recently resumed its own Bitcoin buying with a $66 million purchase after a brief pause.

Bitmine has 5,227,037 ETH, about 86% of its holdings, staked through its MAVAN platform, with projected annualized staking revenue of roughly $358 million.

#5196. Binance stopped 57 North Korean cyber threats but fell for a fake job interview

The exchange's defenses thwarted dozens of suspected attacks before hackers used a staged recruitment process to infiltrate its wallet systems.

Binance said it thwarted 57 suspected North Korea-linked cyber threats in 2026, yet a fake interview compromised one of its engineers.

The disclosure, in the exchange's Nov. 3 security report, reveals how attackers used a recruitment scam involving staged interviews, fabricated identities, and malicious software to access systems involved in the $6.34 billion cryptocurrency theft on Oct. 29.

“Throughout 2026, Binance's security team detected and prevented 57 suspected North Korea-linked cyber threat cases. These attempts reflect the persistent threat posed by state-sponsored actors targeting the cryptocurrency industry. The techniques include fraudulent job interviews, malicious software disguised as popular developer tools, compromised software packages and browser extensions, fake video conferencing applications, and social engineering campaigns designed to steal credentials or deploy remote access malware. We intercepted many of them, but the cost of failing to stop even one was severe—and this should never have happened.”

The operation succeeded despite Binance's security team identifying and preventing dozens of suspected North Korean intrusion attempts during the year, including campaigns using fraudulent job interviews and compromised developer tools.

The FBI attributed the theft to North Korea's TraderTraitor operation on Oct. 31, describing an ongoing effort to steal cryptocurrency and launder the proceeds through decentralized finance platforms.

Binance's findings expose a troubling gap between recognizing a persistent threat and preventing its execution.

The campaign's earliest identified stage occurred in June, when a Binance wallet infrastructure engineer downloaded a tampered Homebrew installer while attempting to install a popular AI coding tool.

Binance's security team detected the suspicious software, replaced the device, and rotated related credentials. However, the incident exposed information about the engineer's responsibilities and colleagues.

In September, someone impersonating a project director at a fictitious company approached another software engineer through a legitimate recruitment platform.

An individual posing as a human resources representative conducted a live video interview and shared a social media profile containing personal photographs and videos to establish credibility.

Start with the stories that matter. Get the context behind the headlines and a clear view of what to watch next.

Thanks for signing up to The Catalyst. Look out for your first daily email. If you already receive it, you’re all set.

A second interview followed with someone claiming to be the company's chief executive.

Near the end of that conversation, the engineer was instructed to download a coding assignment onto his work computer. The repository contained malware that executed during setup, replacing a legitimate developer-tool component and establishing communication with an attacker-controlled server.

Binance said the previously unknown malware evaded its endpoint security software and concealed traces of the intrusion.

By Oct. 20, the attackers had used the compromised computer and an internal AI tool to gain control over two additional engineers, including the employee targeted in June.

They subsequently obtained a container deployment permission token. Nine days later, the attackers used stolen credentials to deploy a malicious container and reach Binance's wallet-signing infrastructure.

The exchange said the attackers initiated unauthorized transfers outside its normal withdrawal process, draining assets across several blockchain networks.

The tactics closely resemble a North Korean cyber operation that security researchers and US authorities have tracked for years.

In September 2024, the FBI warned that North Korean hackers were using highly personalized employment opportunities, elaborate fictional identities, and prolonged conversations to infiltrate cryptocurrency businesses.

The agency noted that even technically sophisticated employees could be deceived by campaigns tailored to their professional backgrounds.

That playbook remained active throughout 2026.

In June, Kudelski Security researchers documented North Korea's Contagious Interview operation, in which hackers impersonated recruiters on LinkedIn, WhatsApp and Discord before persuading developers to execute malicious code.

By September, Atlassian researchers had identified fake technical assessments hosted on established development platforms, where malicious instructions were hidden within otherwise legitimate-looking software projects.

The similarities raise questions about why Binance's existing threat detection measures failed to identify the successful recruitment campaign.

The exchange has not disclosed how many of the 57 prevented cases involved fake interviews or whether any were directly connected to the group behind the October breach.

Following the attack, Binance said it replaced compromised employee devices, revoked exposed credentials, and rebuilt affected wallet infrastructure. It also strengthened developer-environment protections and expanded AI-based behavioral monitoring.

Get The Block’s latest crypto news and price alerts for the coins you follow, right in Chrome. Add our free extension here.

Strive has acquired another 124 BTC for approximately $10 million, bringing its total holdings to 43,124 BTC.

Bitcoin treasury company Strive Strive, Inc. (ASST) bought another 124 Bitcoin (BTC) between Oct. 24 and Oct. 30, bringing its total treasury to 43,124 BTC, according to a Monday filing with the Securities and Exchange Commission.

Strive also continues to hold 505,000 shares of Strategy's STRC preferred stock, valued at approximately $50.1 million as of Oct. 30. Its SATA preferred shares outstanding remained unchanged at just over 14 million.

#5192. Brazil's Largest Exchange Mercado Bitcoin Halts Withdrawals After $40 Million Hack

Brazil's Largest Exchange Mercado Bitcoin Halts Withdrawals After $40 Million Hack

Brazil's largest crypto exchange, Mercado Bitcoin, suspended deposits and withdrawals on Monday after identifying a security incident in one of its systems in the early hours of the morning. The exchange said no customer lost money and no data leaked.

"We identified and quickly contained a security incident in one of our systems early this morning," Mercado Bitcoin said in a Portuguese-language statement on X. "As a preventive protection measure, deposits and withdrawals are temporarily suspended."

Blockchain security firm Hypernative flagged more than $40 million in suspicious transfers that may be linked to the incident, according to a preliminary analysis that spans at least 10 blockchains, including Bitcoin, Ethereum, Solana, and Tron. Mercado Bitcoin has not confirmed that figure and has not disclosed how the attackers got in.

In a follow-up post, the exchange published the destination addresses it identified on five networks: EVM chains (blockchains built to run the same code as Ethereum), Bitcoin, Solana, Stellar, and Tron. It said the addresses were released only so others can identify and track the funds on-chain.

Tether CEO Paolo Ardoino said in a post on X that the stablecoin issuer has frozen more than 4.5 million USDT connected to the incident.

Deposits and withdrawals at Mercado Bitcoin remain suspended, and the exchange has not given a date for resuming them. It said it will post regular updates on X.
