#5189. Justin Sun demands court sanctions over Trump-linked World Liberty Financial’s hidden UAE owners

Justin Sun alleges World Liberty withheld foreign ownership details before using its UAE citizenship to challenge the jurisdiction of an American federal court.

Justin Sun accused the President Donald Trump-backed crypto firm World Liberty Financial of concealing UAE citizenship to escape his federal lawsuit.

The Tron founder said on Nov. 3 that his lawyers had requested sanctions against World Liberty Financial and its attorneys after the company disclosed foreign ownership connections months into litigation over his $45 million investment.

The disclosure introduced another controversy surrounding the president's cryptocurrency business, which has promoted itself as an American financial venture despite a reported $500 million investment from an Abu Dhabi-backed entity tied to a senior UAE government official.

Sun accused World Liberty of selectively invoking its national identity depending on whether it was raising money or defending itself in court.

“World Liberty was proudly American when it was selling tokens. It discovered its UAE roots only when it needed a way out of an American federal courtroom.”

The accusation followed an August court filing in which World Liberty expanded its ownership disclosure from one entity to 22, identifying citizenship in Florida, Delaware, Puerto Rico and the UAE.

Sun alleged the company initially disclosed only Florida citizenship, despite rules requiring it to identify the members of its ownership structure.

The revised filing came six days after an Aug. 20 hearing over World Liberty's attempt to move the investor dispute into arbitration. Sun said the judge had indicated that certain claims should remain in open court, while others could proceed through arbitration.

World Liberty subsequently argued that its UAE citizenship deprived the federal court of jurisdiction because foreign parties were present on both sides of the lawsuit.

Sun, who sued the company in April for allegedly inducing his investment through fraud and unlawfully freezing his tokens, said his lawyers had already spent hundreds of thousands of dollars contesting World Liberty's arbitration request.

He accused the company of failing to disclose ownership information that could have determined the court's jurisdiction at the beginning of the case.

The ownership dispute carries broader political significance because World Liberty's ties to the UAE were already attracting scrutiny over potential conflicts involving the president's family.

In January 2025, days before Trump's inauguration, an investment vehicle backed by UAE National Security Adviser Sheik Tahnoon bin Zayed Al Nahyan agreed to acquire a 49% stake in World Liberty for $500 million.

The agreement, signed by Eric Trump, directed approximately $187 million to Trump family-controlled entities, according to reporting on the transaction.

Sheikh Tahnoon, a brother of the UAE president, also oversees an investment empire with interests in artificial intelligence and advanced technology.

The transaction subsequently drew scrutiny because the Trump administration expanded the UAE's access to advanced American AI chips. Democratic lawmakers raised questions about potential conflicts between the president's business interests and US foreign policy.

World Liberty has rejected suggestions that its investment arrangement influenced US government decisions. President Trump has also said he was unaware of the transaction and that his sons manage the business.

The company's public messaging has nevertheless emphasized its American identity.

Its Gold Paper declares that World Liberty is not organized offshore and describes it as an American corporation seeking to bring decentralized finance to the US.

Those statements are not necessarily inconsistent with foreign ownership. A US-organized company can have foreign investors and acquire foreign citizenship for purposes of federal diversity-jurisdiction rules.

The reported UAE investment also came after Sun's initial November 2024 token purchase, and it became public months before World Liberty amended its court disclosure.

The unanswered question in Sun's sanctions request concerns why the company did not identify the relevant foreign ownership connections when the federal litigation began.

Sun contends that World Liberty either failed to provide its attorneys with accurate information or that its lawyers failed to adequately investigate the company's ownership structure.

World Liberty has previously rejected Sun's fraud allegations and accused him of misconduct in a separate defamation lawsuit.

Sun said he would pursue the original dispute in California state court after the federal judge addresses his sanctions request, leaving the court to determine whether the delayed ownership disclosure warrants penalties against the company or its legal team.

#5187. Binance completes latest proof of reserves as it works to restore assets, withdrawals after record hack

Get The Block’s latest crypto news and price alerts for the coins you follow, right in Chrome. Add our free extension here.

Binance is planning another reserves snapshot on Wednesday and said it expects to begin progressively restoring withdrawals for additional networks and assets on Tuesday.

Binance said it has completed a new zero-knowledge proof of reserves snapshot, allowing users holding BTC, ETH, USDT, BNB, SOL, USDC, XRP, USD1, RLUSD and ZEC to independently verify that their balances are backed 1:1.

In its latest recovery update published Monday morning, the exchange said it replenished about 95% of the assets affected by last week's hack with its own funds, with the remaining 5% in less-liquid tokens it is still working to acquire.

"We've completed the latest Proof of Reserves verification, giving users another way to independently check that their assets are backed," Binance Co-CEO Richard Teng wrote in a post on X. "There's still work to do, and we'll continue to communicate our progress openly."

Binance plans another reserves snapshot on Wednesday and said it expects to begin progressively restoring withdrawals for additional networks and assets on Tuesday.

"Computation is required from the snapshot to the updated full zero-knowledge proof, and the generation time depends on the number of tokens," Binance co-founder and co-CEO Yi He said on X. "It is currently estimated that generating the zero-knowledge proof will take about 40 to 50 hours after the snapshot is completed."

In the largest known crypto exploit on record, onchain investigators say that North Korean attackers exploited Binance's internal systems and wallet-signing mechanisms to move more than $6 billion in funds across multiple blockchains. On Sunday, SomaXBT said North Korean hackers laundered about $242 million of the funds stolen from Binance on Tron.

"The response from issuers, decentralized finance (DeFi) protocols and Binance itself was faster and better coordinated than anything the industry had previously managed, which we regard as a point of validation for several of the business models we cover, regulated custody most of all," StoneX analysts wrote Monday in a note to clients.

Binance's 24-hour net outflows fell to approximately $3.29 billion, down more than 50% from an earlier peak of around $7 billion, according to DeFiLlama. The data tracker estimates that Binance holds approximately $127.78 billion in assets across its linked onchain wallets.

#5184. Elon Musk Mocks Sam Altman Over Claim Home PCs Are Wasting RAM

Elon Musk Mocks Sam Altman Over Claim Home PCs Are Wasting RAM

Elon Musk has mocked OpenAI CEO Sam Altman for arguing that the world's limited supply of computer memory would be better used in AI data centers than in people's home computers.

Altman made the comments in an interview with the YouTube channel Cheeky Pint, while discussing the shortages of memory and other components caused by the AI boom.

"A lot of people want local inference, and somehow they assume that the problem is solved now because we're going to have open-source models," Altman said, referring to running AI models on a user's own hardware. "But from like a global allocation perspective, actually, it's quite terrible to have everybody have a bunch of the world's limited supply of DRAM sitting in their own computer and being used 5% of the time."

"You want maximum utilization in a cloud if you're trying to deal with this very supply-constrained environment," he added. "And somehow, many people's intuition is the opposite of that."

Musk responded on X on Sunday with a meme showing Hans Landa, the Nazi officer played by Christoph Waltz in the film Inglourious Basterds, captioned: "You are withholding DRAM from the cloud, are you not?" "The people deserve to have their RAM too!" Musk wrote alongside the image.

The people deserve to have their RAM too! pic.twitter.com/q6dwauALw4

In the interview, Altman blamed the shortage partly on memory makers' caution. For years, a DRAM producer "would invest $20 billion of CapEx to make $19 billion," he said, and companies that nearly went bankrupt building too many factories in past cycles have been slow to expand, even as they now stand to generate huge profits.

Altman said he would like to see prices for energy, chips and the rest of the supply chain fall. "If it doesn't go down, I'll be very sad," he said, adding that for prices to fall, supply needs to grow.

The jab is the latest in a long-running feud between the two men, who co-founded OpenAI with others in 2015 before Musk left in 2018.

Musk sued OpenAI and Altman in 2024, accusing them of abandoning the organization's founding nonprofit mission. In May, a federal jury in California rejected the $150 billion lawsuit, finding OpenAI, Altman and president Greg Brockman not liable on all claims. OpenAI's lawyers had argued that the suit was motivated in part by competition, after Musk launched rival AI company xAI in 2023.

#5181. DTCC’s tokenization launch could accelerate adoption and boost Securitize, StoneX says

Get The Block’s latest crypto news and price alerts for the coins you follow, right in Chrome. Add our free extension here.

DTCC's permissioned service targets different markets than Securitize's public-blockchain tokenization, StoneX analysts said, keeping a buy rating on SECZ.

The Depository Trust & Clearing Corporation's (DTCC) new tokenization service validates rather than threatens Securitize's business model, according to StoneX analysts, who argue that the two platforms serve different markets.

While DTCC's permissioned service is initially limited to certain securities, participants and blockchain networks, Securitize (SECZ) focuses on public-blockchain tokenization, including tokenized funds, equities and assets that can be used in decentralized finance.

"We acknowledge that DTCC has opened a debate about where tokenized U.S. equities should ultimately be anchored," analyst Mark Palmer wrote Monday in a note to clients. "At the same time, we believe SECZ is well positioned to emerge as a winner of that debate because it is the only tokenization platform that already holds the full regulatory stack that an issuer needs to sponsor its own on-chain shares, an SEC-registered transfer agent, broker-dealer and ATS."

StoneX also pointed to the potential for tokenized assets to serve as collateral across markets, arguing that the ability to move tokenized Treasuries around the clock could drive demand for tokenized fund shares and equities as collateral. Securitize is well positioned to benefit, the analysts said, given its regulatory infrastructure and role as a transfer agent for a large pool of tokenized assets.

That use case is already developing in decentralized finance. Tokenized private credit on Solana reached $1.44 billion, with credit tokens from Securitize and Maple being accepted as collateral in lending markets such as Kamino, according to The Block's latest Tokenization Briefing.

Securitize is also expanding its tokenized equity business. On Monday, the company expanded its tokenized U.S. stock offering to 25 equities, adding 13 companies, including ExxonMobil, GameStop, Salesforce and Disney, to its existing lineup. The tokens trade on Solana and are backed 1:1 by underlying shares, providing eligible investors with associated economic benefits and rights, including dividends and voting rights where applicable.

Last month, StoneX argued that Securitize Stocks could help the company move beyond integration fees and generate transaction and servicing revenue, with collateral-related revenue a potential longer-term opportunity.

The firm reiterated its "buy" rating and $18 price target on SECZ shares. The stock traded around $7.30 at publication time, down 24% since going public in July 2025.

#5179. Nvidia and Nike Lead Biggest Wave Yet of Tokenized Stock Objections

Nvidia and Nike Lead Biggest Wave Yet of Tokenized Stock Objections

Even Nvidia doesn’t want its shares on OKX and ICE’s planned tokenized stock venue.

The chipmaker is among 12 new companies listed as objecting issuers in an updated notice from OKXICE TSV dated Friday. The others are Costco, Nike, Micron, CrowdStrike, Palo Alto Networks, Reddit, Hewlett Packard Enterprise, Corning, Bloom Energy, IREN, and USA Rare Earth. The additions bring the total to 50 companies.

It’s the largest single batch yet. The list stood at 38 in the venue’s Oct. 28 notice, when Walmart and Eli Lilly joined, and at nine when the first objectors appeared on Oct. 15. It has more than quintupled since.

The newcomers span chips, cybersecurity, retail, and energy. Nvidia, Micron, and Corning join hardware peers such as AMD, Intel, and Broadcom, while CrowdStrike and Palo Alto Networks bring cybersecurity heavyweights to the list. IREN, a Bitcoin miner that has expanded into AI cloud computing, adds a name from the crypto industry itself.

Under the SEC’s innovation exemption, which the agency unveiled in September after the Clarity Act stalled in the Senate, a venue must notify a company before offering a version of its shares tokenized by an unaffiliated third party. If the company objects in writing within 30 days, the venue can’t make that stock available for trading, according to the SEC’s order. The objection applies only to that venue.

OKXICE TSV, a 50-50 joint venture between NYSE owner Intercontinental Exchange and OKX’s U.S. holding company, plans to let approved users trade tokenized U.S. stocks around the clock. Trades would run through permissioned Uniswap v4 pools on OKX’s XLayer blockchain, paired with stablecoins such as USDC.

None of the objecting companies have explained their decisions, but Nasdaq offered one possible rationale when it opted its own shares out of the exemption.

A spokesperson told The Wall Street Journal that Nasdaq doesn’t want its stock trading on a venue that doesn’t show the best prices in real time or report trades to the consolidated tape.

Further, Crypto in America reported that Nasdaq may be playing a role behind the scenes in encouraging companies to opt out of the OKX-ICE venture. “Our Nasdaq rep had reached out to us regarding OKX listing, suggesting we should object and that they would hold a call to discuss why,” an executive at a Nasdaq-listed company told the publication. “Nasdaq is definitely partly behind this,” the exec reportedly said.

OKXICE’s notice acknowledges it isn’t subject to Regulation NMS, the rules designed to ensure investors get the best displayed prices. It also warns that broker-dealers face unsettled questions about trade reporting and best execution on the venue.

Some of the market’s biggest names, including Apple, Tesla and Amazon, still haven’t appeared on the list.

#5177. Binance Reopens Withdrawals as Hack Recovery Enters Second Week

Binance has reopened withdrawals after its $6.3 billion North Korea hack, covered 95% of losses, and has reserves for the remaining 5%, with post-mortem reviews to stay open.

Binance restarted customer withdrawals on Sunday, four days after hackers drained roughly $6.3 billion from its hot wallets in the largest crypto theft on record.

The exchange reopened in stages over the weekend, starting with bitcoin and expanding to 15 networks. The move marked the first real test of whether the platform could absorb a loss of that size without freezing its customers out for longer.

The recovery push began Saturday. In its third recovery update, Binance said it had replenished 90% of the impacted amount with company funds. The exchange drew on part of its Secure Asset Fund for Users, a portion of its futures insurance fund and its corporate treasury to buy replacement tokens.

The futures fund contribution drew particular attention, since Binance described the remaining balance as a regulatory requirement. Binance also published a new proof-of-reserves snapshot dated October 31, giving users a baseline to check their balances against.

Sunday’s reopening began at 06:00 UTC with BTC. Binance then added ETH, BNB Smart Chain, Solana, XRP, Dogecoin, Tron, Polygon, Avalanche, Arbitrum, Base, Optimism, Zcash, TON and Sui. Between 06:00 and 15:30 UTC, the exchange processed about $4.65 billion in withdrawals against roughly $1.01 billion in deposits.

That produced a net outflow near $3.64 billion, equal to about 2.1% of the $171 billion in assets Binance reported in its late-October proof of reserves.

Deposits stayed open throughout the rollout, and the exchange said temporary delays were possible during the initial surge in volume.

Binance said its Sunday update raised coverage to 95% of the impacted amount. The remaining 5% sits in less liquid tokens outside the top ten assets on its proof-of-reserves list. Sourcing those tokens at the required scale takes more time, and the exchange said it is working with industry partners and over-the-counter desks to complete the purchases. Binance has committed to covering the full amount, and it has repeated that users will not bear the financial impact of the theft.

On Monday, Binance said the full zero-knowledge proof for the October 31 snapshot is complete. Users holding BTC, ETH, USDT, BNB, SOL, USDC, XRP, USD1, RLUSD or ZEC can run the exchange’s verifier to confirm their balances are backed at least 1:1.

The verifier is open source, and the exchange has pointed users to a step-by-step guide.

The exchange plans another snapshot covering more tokens on November 4, with a proof expected to take 40 to 50 hours to generate. A further withdrawal phase is scheduled for Tuesday, and the exchange said it would share details once the rollout is ready.

On the law enforcement front, the FBI has attributed the attack to North Korea, and the U.S. Secret Service is working with Circle to trace and freeze funds. Tether reported about $1.57 billion in USDT frozen, and Circle said it had frozen more than $800 million in USDC in coordination with authorities.

The market reacted sharply to the breach. Bitcoin fell about 5% to below $77,000 in the hours after the attack, and shares of Coinbase,Robinhood, Strategy, and Strive all declined as investors weighed the risk of exchange-level losses across the sector.

Binance has said it found additional points of breach beyond the initial compromise, which it originally attributed to a single engineer’s laptop. The exchange has not yet published a full post-mortem explaining how the attackers prepared and executed the operation, which some estimates suggest took at least 36 hours of groundwork.

The destination of roughly $3.3 billion in stolen funds that were not frozen has also not been fully explained, and the exchange has not detailed what it expects to recover from that pool. Investigators continue to track wallet activity across ten blockchains.

#5174. Mercado Bitcoin, Brazil’s largest crypto exchange, hit by $46 million security breach

Get The Block’s latest crypto news and price alerts for the coins you follow, right in Chrome. Add our free extension here.

The exchange paused deposits and withdrawals after the incident, while Tether froze more than 4.5 million USDT linked to the attacker.

Mercado Bitcoin, Brazil's largest cryptocurrency exchange, confirmed Monday that it suffered a security incident involving approximately $46.3 million in assets, according to Arkham data.

"We identified and swiftly contained a security incident in one of our systems early this morning," the exchange said in a post on X. Mercado Bitcoin temporarily suspended deposits and withdrawals as a precaution while its teams investigate the incident, track the assets, and coordinate with authorities.

The exchange said the incident did not affect customers or result in a data breach. It also published the destination wallet addresses associated with the transactions to help blockchain security firms and other parties monitor and track the assets.

Mercado Bitcoin's incident comes amid a string of security breaches and exploits across the crypto industry in recent days, following Thursday's $6 billion hack of Binance. That same day, Polymarket was hit by a $2.65 million exploit, in which it said a third-party provider was compromised.

Late Friday evening, blockchain analytics firm Chainalysis said its own customer data was accessed by an AI-assisted hacker. Of note, the firm said the incident was not related to the theft at Binance.

On Saturday, hackers hijacked a dozen DeFi websites registered with Name.com and stole about $138,000. Blockaid said the sites were rigged with a malware kit that tricks visitors into signing away their funds.

#5171. Proton Users Revolt After CEO Calls Security Caution 'Loser Thinking' Amid AI Push

Proton Users Revolt After CEO Calls Security Caution 'Loser Thinking' Amid AI Push

Proton users are reporting subscription cancellations after revelations that CEO Andy Yen pressured engineers to embrace AI and dismissed a request for more security testing as “loser thinking.”

The backlash cuts into Proton’s main selling point—trusting it with information people want kept private. Its encrypted email service scrambles messages to protect their contents, and customers questioning its development practices are asking whether faster releases could come at their expense.

A Proton employee asked the company in August for a few days to pentest new code, meaning specialists would try to break into it before release, per 404 Media. Yen replied: “That’s bullshit and loser thinking. Of course it’s possible to be fast and secure.”

He also urged staff to stop writing code by hand and use more AI, and mentioned that he’d be reinterviewing its staff to see if and how they are using AI to do their job, the outlet reported. Its investigation drew on nine current and former employees, internal messages, and meeting recordings.

This didn’t land well among users, and customers have responded with cancellation notices and demands for new leadership on social media such as Reddit. A proposal on Proton’s feedback forum calling for Yen to step down had 2,020 votes when reviewed, alongside comments from users saying they had canceled subscriptions or would not renew.

Besides the blind use of AI, some former and current Proton employees on Reddit and social media have documented a toxic work culture, especially from the CEO.

Those posts document anger, rather than a verified count of departing customers. Canceling a paid plan also does not necessarily mean deleting an account.

Proton’s AI ambitions predate this dispute. Its Lumo chatbot comes with protections against using conversations to train AI, but internal use of AI was not among Proton’s selling points, even in the peak of the chatbot hype.

To address concerns over irresponsible use of AI, on November 1, technology chief Bart Butler published an engineering manifesto arguing that developers should direct large language models, AI systems that generate text and code, rather than treat them as occasional assistants.

Butler compared manual coding to memorizing phone numbers, and envisioned eventually ending most human code review. He also insisted that expertise and testing remain essential, with engineers accountable for work that models cannot reliably handle.

“Your job has fundamentally changed. It is no longer to write code, and LLMs are not here to help you write better code faster,” the manifesto reads. “Your job is to help the LLM write better code faster.” Doubling on its stance, Proton sees avoiding AI as an “existential threat” to the success of its very mission.

“‘Programming’ is dead. Engineering is not,” Buttler told his own team. “Your old job is gone”.

The document also calls for company-wide investment in automation, arguing that generating code faster achieves little if approvals and other development steps remain slow. Butler described that overhaul as the next phase of adoption.

AI tools can introduce security problems of their own. For example, Microsoft researchers said a now patched Claude flaw could expose secret access credentials through malicious instructions hidden in material the coding assistant reviewed.

They can also help find weaknesses: Google’s PageBreak system searches its applications for exploitable bugs and checks whether attacks actually work.

Proton acknowledged to 404 Media that management had sometimes communicated too bluntly and that employees were anxious. Its public manifesto nevertheless argues that failing to adopt AI quickly threatens the company’s ability to compete.

Yen canceled the re-interviews on October 30. The assessments would have required staff to explain their AI use, with employees fearing their answers could determine whether they kept their jobs.

#5167. Kim Jong-Un’s sister rejects FBI claims linking North Korea to record $6 billion Binance hack: reports

Get The Block’s latest crypto news and price alerts for the coins you follow, right in Chrome. Add our free extension here.

On Monday, Yonhap News Agency reported that hackers from North Korea were not behind the over $6 billion hack of Binance, citing the sister of North Korea's leader Kim Jong-Un.

A North Korean official has denied the country was behind the largest crypto hack on record, despite onchain investigators and the FBI attributing the $6 billion exploit to North Korean hackers.

On Monday, Yonhap News Agency reported that Kim Yo-jong, the sister of North Korean leader Kim Jong-Un and a senior government official, rejected the FBI's assessment that North Korea was responsible for last week's Binance hack.

"The FBI is trying to brand the DPRK as a 'cyber criminal state', claiming that it obtained information that a hacking organization 'supported' by the DPRK was involved in the incident," Kim said in a statement, according to Yonhap.

The FBI did not immediately respond to a request for comment from The Block.

Ahead of the attack last week, an attacker deployed and tested malicious code on three wallets before later executing the attack on Binance on Thursday. On Ethereum, the attacker used EIP-7702 to turn a Binance hot wallet into a sweeper controlled by the attacker. It prepared 50 pre-signed authorizations to ensure one would remain valid when executed, according to TRM Labs.

Three firms, including TRM Labs, attributed the attack to North Korean bad actors. Then, over the weekend, the FBI confirmed that North Korea was responsible for the hack. In its release, the FBI referred to this specific North Korean malicious cyber activity as "TraderTraitor."

Earlier on Monday, Binance published its first set of proof of reserves and said it replenished about 95% of the assets affected by the hack with its own funds.

#5165. North Korea Is Laundering Binance Hack Loot at One New Wallet Every 25 Seconds

North Korea Is Laundering Binance Hack Loot at One New Wallet Every 25 Seconds

North Korean hackers are moving the $6.4 billion they stole from Binance at a blistering clip: roughly one new crypto wallet every 25 seconds.

In under four days, the attacker has spread the funds across about 12,000 wallets on 11 blockchains in more than 40,000 transactions, according to an analysis published Monday by blockchain analytics firm Elliptic.

But stablecoin issuers moved even faster. About $2.85 billion, or 44% of the haul, has been frozen or seized, compared with roughly 3% after North Korea’s $1.5 billion hack of Bybit in February 2025.

Speed mattered because stablecoins made up $3.46 billion, or 54%, of the haul, and their issuers can freeze them. Tether froze the stolen USDT on Ethereum 37 minutes after it reached the attacker’s wallet, and Circle froze all $823 million in stolen USDC before any of it could be swapped, Elliptic found.

Before the freezes landed, the hacker swapped 434 million USDT for ETH on decentralized exchanges such as Uniswap, borrowed 19,762 ETH on lending protocol Aave against another 59.9 million USDT, and traded 234 million USDT for Tron’s TRX token. In all, about $786 million in stablecoins got away.

Attackers began draining Binance’s hot wallets late Thursday. The FBI has since attributed the theft to TraderTraitor, a North Korean state-linked hacking group, and published hundreds of linked addresses.

Binance, which has pledged to cover all losses, reopened withdrawals Sunday and saw about $3.64 billion in net outflows in the first nine and a half hours.

The laundering that followed has been relentless. On Tron, which became the main route, activity peaked at about 1,300 transactions in a single hour.

That pace fueled speculation that AI agents were doing the work. Elliptic disagreed, pointing to fixed timing, set amounts, blind retries and pauses that resemble human working hours. The firm wrote that while the laundering is clearly automated, “nothing we have seen shows an AI agent making decisions.”

About $3.24 billion remains in addresses linked to the theft, mostly ETH and the 18,270 stolen Bitcoin, which haven’t moved since the day of the attack. Elliptic believes those funds are very likely still held by North Korea, while money already being laundered may have passed to Chinese money-laundering networks.

Only a negligible amount appears to have been cashed out. About $16 million has also flowed to addresses holding proceeds of September’s Bitget exploit.

Based on the Bybit playbook, Elliptic expects the attacker to swap its ETH for Bitcoin in small batches and cash out through over-the-counter brokers over a period of months. Further freezes will be harder, the firm said, since ETH and Bitcoin can’t be frozen. The best remaining chances lie with centralized services, where the funds will eventually have to surface.

The Binance theft caps a punishing stretch for crypto security. BTCPay Server urged merchants to switch off their servers amid reports of hot wallet attacks on Monday, and Brazilian exchange Mercado Bitcoin halted withdrawals after a $40 million hack. It’s unclear whether either incident is connected to the Binance attack.

#5162. Nvidia, Nike Join 50 Companies Blocking OKX–ICE Stock Token Push

Fifty companies, including Nvidia, Microsoft and JPMorgan, have blocked OKX and ICE from tokenizing their shares under the SEC’s new Innovation Exemption.

Fifty public companies have now objected to having their shares tokenized on OKXICE, the joint venture between crypto exchange OKX and New York Stock Exchange parent Intercontinental Exchange (ICE), as the venue’s first 30-day notice window closes Tuesday.

The venue’s Oct. 30 notice added 12 objectors, including Nvidia, Costco, Nike, Micron and Reddit. They join Microsoft, Alphabet, JPMorgan Chase, Goldman Sachs, Walmart, Broadcom, Verizon and Intel, among others. Of the 62 companies OKXICE initially proposed to tokenize, 43, or about 69%, have objected, according to a CoinDesk tally.

The list has grown fast: nine objectors on Oct. 15, 16 on Oct. 19, 38 on Oct. 28 and 50 two days later.

The objections stem from the SEC’s five-year “Innovation Exemption,” issued Sept. 17, which lets tokenized securities venues (TSVs) trade blockchain versions of U.S. stocks through automated market makers without registering as exchanges. When an unaffiliated third party tokenizes a company’s stock, the venue must notify the issuer, which then has 30 days to object in writing. Silence counts as consent, and the order offers no way to object after the window closes.

OKXICE plans 24/7 trading of tokens backed one-for-one by shares, paired against stablecoins such as USDC in permissioned pools on OKX’s X Layer blockchain.

The objecting companies have not given reasons. JPMorgan said it remains encouraged by tokenization but wants to be thoughtful about how its shares are represented and traded. “Given how easy the opt-out is, the list of objectors is likely to keep growing,” Olivia Vande Woude, head of tokenization at Ava Labs, said.

The fight has also taken on a competitive edge. An executive at a Nasdaq-listed company told Crypto in America that a Nasdaq representative urged the company to object and offered a call to explain why. Nasdaq declined to comment on the claim but reiterated that TSVs are not the best path to tokenization.

Nasdaq argues that venues outside the National Market System could fragment liquidity and lack real-time best-price display and consolidated-tape reporting. It is building its own issuer-centric Nasdaq Equity Token with Kraken, targeted for the second quarter of 2027.

A full-page Wall Street Journal ad urging CEOs to opt out, headlined “YOUR STOCK. THEIR TOKENS. YOUR PROBLEM.,” linked to Nasdaq’s FAQ via QR code. Conservative-leaning nonprofit Common Sense Leadership Fund paid for it. Nasdaq said it had no connection to the ad and never funded the group.

OKXICE co-chair and former New York Gov. Andrew Cuomo has called the objections a “natural response.”

“We are at the very beginning of the rollout of fundamentally different market structure,” he said last week.

For context, a tokenized stock is a blockchain token backed one-for-one by a real share, which a third party holds. OKXICE lets it trade around the clock against stablecoins like USDC. Under the SEC’s Innovation Exemption, the venue can list these tokens without the issuing company’s consent, unless the company objects within 30 days.

#5159. Tom Lee Calls Bottom on AI Stock Selloff, Citing Tight Supply and Crypto Resilience

Tom Lee Calls Bottom on AI Stock Selloff, Citing Tight Supply and Crypto Resilience

Fundstrat co-founder and Bitmine chairman Tom Lee says the AI stock selloff has reached its bottom, arguing that demand remains constrained by supply and that crypto’s response to a multibillion-dollar hack suggests investors have already done much of their selling.

“I think we're at the bottom,” Lee said in a CNBC interview on Squawk Box. His call follows a decline that has spread beyond individual AI companies: large technology stocks also sit inside the funds many people use to invest for retirement. (Disclosure: Tom Lee is one of several investors in Dastan, Decrypt’s parent company.)

Lee acknowledged how difficult it is to identify the point where prices stop falling. “Bottoms are dangerous because you don't know how much more downside you have at the bottom,” he said.

His case rests on a distinction between an industry struggling to expand fast enough and one that has built more capacity than customers need.

“The market for AI is still very tight. It's not an overcapacity market,” Lee said. Constraints on spending, including political opposition, are slowing growth without signaling that the boom has peaked, he argued.

The distinction matters for companies building the infrastructure behind AI, including data centers, the facilities that house the computers running these systems. A shortage can leave customers waiting, but it can also prevent suppliers from turning orders into revenue.

Intel offered a particularly sharp example on Friday, when its shares plunged nearly 30% to $65.76 after a disappointing fourth-quarter forecast. Its data center and AI revenue had risen 78% from a year earlier to $7.3 billion, yet manufacturing constraints limited its ability to meet demand.

Intel projected quarterly revenue of $15 billion to $16 billion, with the midpoint about $1.6 billion below analysts’ expectations. Its weaker PC business added pressure, showing why healthy AI demand can coexist with a falling share price.

Lee’s second argument looks outside the stock market. He pointed to crypto’s relatively limited response to the Binance security breach, which the exchange initially estimated had an impact of about $6.3 billion.

“If crypto is a leading indicator for risk, you've already seen people sort of washed out,” he said. In his reading, a market that absorbs bad news without a much larger decline may have fewer sellers left to push prices down.

Bitcoin did fall about 4% to roughly $76,400 on Friday, and more than $1.3 billion in positions funded with borrowed money were forcibly closed. U.S. spot Bitcoin exchange-traded funds, which hold Bitcoin and trade like stocks, also shed $643.2 million that day, their largest daily outflow of October.

That said, a 4% drop can happen even during bull runs and it is considered a relatively normal occurrence among crypto traders.

Binance said on Friday that about $3 billion of the affected assets had been frozen and pledged to cover losses using its emergency reserve and company funds.

#5157. Bitcoin Price Trades Near $77,500 as Midterm Voting Begins

Bitcoin price trades near $77,500 as midterm voting begins, pressured by a record $6.3 billion Binance hack, ETF outflows, a Fed leaning toward a December hike and oil above $100 on Iran tensions.

Bitcoin price traded near $77,500 on Tuesday morning as polls opened for the U.S. midterm elections, down about .6% on the day and roughly $30,000 below its price one year ago, according to Bitcoin Magazine Pro data.

The vote decides control of Congress and, with it, the fate of the CLARITY Act, the market structure bill that passed the House 294-134 in July 2025 and failed a Senate cloture vote 49-50 on Sept. 15.

Prediction markets on Kalshi give Democrats a 94% chance of taking the House and 74% for the Senate. A Democratic Senate would likely put Sen. Elizabeth Warren in line to chair the Banking Committee. If Congress does not act before the new session convenes on Jan. 3, the bill dies and must be reintroduced.

Crypto groups spent more than $200 million on the midterms, CNBC reported. Analysts at TD Cowen see a Democratic sweep as the most probable outcome and the least favorable one for the industry; a divided Congress, in their view, leaves room for a narrower deal.

The bitcoin price action into the vote has been one-directional. Bitcoin price opened November near $78,500 and has lost about $2,000 since. October closed down about 7%, the second straight October decline.

On Oct. 28, after a hawkish Federal Reserve hold and weak earnings from Alphabet and Samsung, the price fell below $80,000 for the first time in weeks, with about $356 million in leveraged positions liquidated.

The larger shock came the next day. On Oct. 29, attackers the FBI attributes to North Korea drained about $6.3 billion from Binance’s hot wallets, the largest crypto theft on record.

Bitcoin price held strong but fell about 4% on Friday and more than $1.3 billion in leveraged positions were liquidated. U.S. spot Bitcoin ETFs shed $643.3 million that day, their largest single-day outflow since June 25, according to SoSoValue data. Another $399 million left on Monday.

Bitcoin recovered to $78,491 late Sunday before sliding again on Monday and Tuesday. Traders are watching support between $76,264 and $76,476, which lines up with the 200-day exponential moving average. Below that sits the September low of $74,967.

The 50-day EMA near $80,800 is the first resistance above.

The Crypto Fear & Greed Index read 37 on Monday, in “fear” territory.

The macro backdrop offers little support. The Fed held rates at 3.75% to 4.00% on Oct. 28 by a 10-2 vote, with two officials dissenting in favor of a hike, after raising rates in September for the first time since July 2023.

Polymarket puts the odds of a December hike near 71%. September PCE inflation ran at 3.6% year over year and third-quarter GDP grew at a 3.4% annualized pace.

The 10-year Treasury yield touched 5.36% in early October, its highest since 2002, before easing to about 4.91% on Monday.

Energy sits at the center of the inflation picture. Brent crude fell 7.7% on Monday to $104.66 a barrel after Iran and the U.S. signaled openness to talks, its biggest one-day drop since July, CNBC reported.

The move reversed in part on Tuesday, with Brent back near $106.60 after reports of a U.S. strike on Iran’s Qeshm Island. Disruption around the Strait of Hormuz has kept crude above $100 and headline inflation elevated, which keeps the Fed on a tightening path.

Equities have added to the pressure. The Nasdaq fell 2.9% on Friday, its worst day since June, after results from Intel and Amazon raised questions about AI capital spending, and lost 5.3% in October. Bitcoin price has moved with technology stocks through the selloff rather than against them.

The calendar is full. The October jobs report lands Friday, with consensus near 65,000 after September’s 29,000. October CPI follows on Nov. 10 and the Fed’s next decision on Dec. 9. Binance’s next proof-of-reserves snapshot is due Wednesday.

Options markets are calm, with Deribit’s DVOL index near 36.5, a sign traders expect no large swings. Seasonality cuts the other way: Bitcoin price fell 36.6% in November 2018 and 16.2% in November 2022, the two most recent midterm years, both during bear markets.

#5155. Consensys launches cross-ledger settlement and routing stack for institutional networks

Get The Block’s latest crypto news and price alerts for the coins you follow, right in Chrome. Add our free extension here.

The Sovereign Network Interoperability stack supports securities settlement, cross-currency payments, and collateral mobility while allowing institutions to retain control of their ledgers.

Consensys on Monday announced a new technology stack designed to let banks and financial institutions settle transactions across independently operated blockchains without moving assets off their native ledgers.

Dubbed "Sovereign Network Interoperability," the product was launched at Devcon in Mumbai. It supports collateral mobility, securities settlement, wholesale and cross-currency payments, as well as  funds and asset servicing, according to a statement.

Consensys said the software stack includes a Cross Ledger Interoperability Protocol, built on a decade of research into zero-knowledge proofs. This architecture ensures bridgeless interoperability, meaning assets stay on their native ledgers while cross-ledger state and execution are verified cryptographically, it added.

Under the system, an institution specifies its transaction requirements and approved counterparties. The software selects an eligible route, coordinates execution on the participating ledgers and tracks the outcome.

"We are bringing deep protocol expertise, transaction coordination and complete support into one product," Consensys President David Cunningham said in the statement. "The result is a simpler path to each new connection, with verifiable outcomes and clear accountability for keeping the full stack working for years to come."

The product will also support the Ethereum Economic Zone standard, the Inter-Blockchain Communication protocol, and other interoperability technologies, per the statement. EVM-based networks are available now, while Canton connectivity is planned.

The launch follows Consensys' Oct. 8 partnership with ClearToken to develop infrastructure for round-the-clock settlement of tokenized securities and cash. In September, the company also announced plans to separate its consumer and institutional businesses, with MetaMask becoming an independent consumer-focused company and Consensys retaining its protocols and institutional infrastructure operations.

#5152. Claude-Built Linux Exploit Targets Bug That Ubuntu Still Hasn't Patched

Claude-Built Linux Exploit Targets Bug That Ubuntu Still Hasn't Patched

Another day, another exploit from ChatGPT or Claude that gives us the chills.

Anthropic says its AI wrote a working attack that lets a stranger take full control of a Linux server over the internet, no password needed. The bug behind it, CVE-2026-98432, has been hiding in Linux since version 4.4 and scores 9.8 out of 10 for severity.

Linux runs countless servers, and Ubuntu is one of its most popular flavors. Ubuntu's own tracker still lists every version from 16.04 to 26.10 as vulnerable.

Anthropic described the attack on Oct. 27 in a research post covering five targets: Chrome, Safari, Android, Windows, and Linux. Claude wrote the Linux exploit after initial prompting, using an unreleased internal model.

The attack takes less than an hour to find a hidden spot in the computer's memory, then five more minutes to run a command with the highest level of access, known as root.

The flaw works like reading a sticky note after someone already shredded it. One part of the system throws away a piece of information while another part is still using it, per AlmaLinux. A specially crafted message that opens an internet connection can set that off.

Failing costs the attacker almost nothing. "An enabling property of this race condition is that a lost race only fails the handshake without crashing the whole system," Anthropic wrote, "so a determined attacker can retry indefinitely until they succeed."

Anthropic's version needs a server using a feature called multipath TCP, which lets one connection travel over several routes at once. Programs written in Go have switched it on by default since Go 1.24, per the Go Security Team.

But the bug is not limited to that feature. "While the demonstrated exploit requires the target system to expose an MPTCP listener, the underlying bug is not specific to MPTCP," the Go Security Team wrote.

Red Hat released fixes for RHEL 8, 9 and 10 on Sunday, including RHSA-2026:93412. Debian, SUSE, Oracle Linux and Amazon Linux have also shipped fixes, at least for some versions.

For anyone who can't update yet, Red Hat published a temporary firewall rule. It "reduces exposure to this vulnerability but cannot fully prevent it," Red Hat says in its security bulletin. A full working solution is still under development.

Anthropic's earlier Claude Mythos model helped Mozilla find 271 bugs in Firefox, as Decrypt reported in April.

Ubuntu's tracker shows fixes in progress for 22.04, 24.04, 26.04 and 26.10. Versions 20.04, 18.04 and 16.04 show only "Vulnerable," and the tracker gives no release date for any fix.

#5150. NEAR governance approves proposal to cut token issuance ceiling to 1.6%

Get The Block’s latest crypto news and price alerts for the coins you follow, right in Chrome. Add our free extension here.

NEAR's House of Stake passed HSP-029, which lowers the network's maximum annual issuance rate from 2.5% to 1.6% over 24 months.

NEAR Protocol's House of Stake governance body has approved a proposal to lower the network's maximum annual issuance rate from 2.5% to 1.6% over 24 months.

HSP-029, which passed the ballot on Monday with overwhelming support, retains the existing 90/10 split of newly issued tokens between staking participants and the protocol treasury, and changes only that single protocol parameter. This would not alter the emissions mechanism.

Once implemented, the issuance ceiling declines each epoch linearly at roughly 3.75 basis points per month, reaching the 1.6% target at the 24th month. This is essentially to slow dilution and reduce long-term sell pressure of the NEAR token NEAR Protocol (NEAR), as the network said its bootstrapping phase is largely over.

"This is how NEAR's economics evolve transparently and programmatically: token holders put a core parameter to a public vote, and the new rate rolls out epoch by epoch on a published schedule," NEAR said in an X post. "For NEAR's tokenomics, that means lower long-term issuance and a supply schedule anyone can verify."

The proposal estimates the ramp would avoid roughly 11.8 million NEAR of issuance over the first two years and 66.1 million over six years, leaving the supply about 4.36% smaller than under the existing 2.5% ceiling. Daily issuance would fall from approximately 89,500 NEAR to 57,300 NEAR at full implementation, while gross staking yield is projected to decline from 5.268% to 3.372% over the same period, assuming the staking participation rate measured in September remains unchanged.

For the proposal to go into full effect, the House of Stake Security Council will need to review the result before ratification. A 90-day grace period follows, after which validators must adopt a protocol upgrade for the change to become binding. NEAR previously cut the maximum issuance from 5% to 2.5% in October 2025.

#5148. New Jersey Drops Coinbase Staking Order After Three Years

New Jersey’s securities regulator has rescinded its 2023 cease-and-desist order and $5 million penalty against Coinbase’s staking service.

New Jersey’s securities regulator has withdrawn the 2023 cease-and-desist order that barred Coinbase from offering staking to new customers in the state, ending a three-year standoff that cost residents an estimated $15 million in forgone rewards.

The Bureau of Securities notice, dated Oct. 30 and signed by Acting Bureau Chief Keith A. Alt, rescinds the order and the $5 million penalty that came with it. The withdrawal is without prejudice and without a ruling on the merits.

Coinbase disclosed the development in its third-quarter report filed with the Securities and Exchange Commission, which states that the regulator informed the company of its intent to rescind on Oct. 29.

Coinbase CEO Brian Armstrong said New Jersey users would regain access to staking “very soon.”

As of Monday, the exchange’s eligibility page continued to list New Jersey among the states where customers cannot stake new principal, and no reopening date has been announced.

The order traces to June 6, 2023, when the New Jersey bureau joined securities regulators in nine other states — Alabama, California, Illinois, Kentucky, Maryland, South Carolina, Vermont, Washington and Wisconsin — in a coordinated action against Coinbase’s staking program.

The states alleged the service amounted to an unregistered securities offering under state law.

California led the task force; its action covered more than 600,000 accounts. In July 2023, Coinbase agreed with California, New Jersey, South Carolina and Wisconsin to block customers in those states from staking new funds pending final adjudication. Maryland reached a similar agreement in October 2023.

Customers who had staked before the orders kept earning rewards, but could not add principal or compound the rewards they received.

The state cases ran parallel to the SEC’s June 2023 lawsuit against Coinbase, which named staking among its claims. The SEC dismissed that case in February 2025. Alabama, Kentucky, Illinois, South Carolina and Vermont dismissed, vacated or withdrew their own actions in March and April 2025. New Jersey is the sixth of the original ten states to step back.

Rescinding the order ends the restriction on new staking in New Jersey. It does not resolve the underlying legal question. The bureau made no finding on whether Coinbase’s staking program is a security, and the “without prejudice” language preserves its ability to file a new action. Coinbase’s own filing calls the state matters “preliminary” and says an adverse resolution “could have a material impact” on the business.

The filing describes a planned rescission rather than a completed one, which explains the gap between Armstrong’s statement and the unchanged eligibility page. The change for New Jersey residents arrives when Coinbase updates its systems, not when the notice was signed.

Coinbase has framed the state restrictions as a competitive disadvantage. In an April 2025 post, the company’s Ryan VanGrack argued the orders singled out one provider while residents could stake through other platforms. Coinbase put forgone rewards across the four restricted states at more than $130 million as of October 2025.

Four states remain. California, Maryland and Wisconsin continue to bar new staking on Coinbase while their cases proceed; Washington permits staking but is pursuing a $4 million fine. Coinbase estimates California residents have missed more than $89 million in rewards since 2023.

The decision lands as federal policy has shifted but not settled. The SEC has withdrawn from the registration-based cases it brought in 2023, and the CLARITY market structure bill passed the House in 2025 but stalled in the Senate. State securities regulators are not bound by the SEC’s enforcement priorities, which is why the staking cases outlived the federal one by more than a year and a half.

A future SEC, or a future bureau chief in Trenton, could revisit the question.

#5147. Mystery $106 Million Crypto Theft Hits EU Entity Days After Binance Hack

Mystery $106 Million Crypto Theft Hits EU Entity Days After Binance Hack

Another nine-figure theft has hit crypto, just days after the biggest heist in the industry’s history.

An unnamed entity in the European Union is suspected of losing more than $106 million from several of its accounts on centralized exchanges on Sunday, pseudonymous onchain investigator ZachXBT said Monday in a post to his Telegram channel.

The funds were withdrawn as USDC and USDT stablecoins by what he called an “illicit actor.”

ZachXBT published 11 Ethereum addresses tied to the theft.

Instant exchanges let users quickly swap one cryptocurrency for another, often with little identity verification, while Monero hides senders, receivers and amounts, making it a popular tool for obscuring stolen funds.

Swapping out of USDC and USDT also matters because the companies behind those stablecoins can freeze them.

ZachXBT said the culprit remains unidentified, but that he could say “with a high degree of confidence” that it isn’t Lazarus Group, citing multiple laundering indicators. Lazarus is the umbrella name often used for North Korea’s state-backed hackers. ZachXBT didn’t name the victim.

That sets the theft apart from the record-setting $6.4 billion Binance hack, which the FBI has attributed to TraderTraitor, a North Korean hacking group.

In that case, stablecoin issuers moved within minutes. Tether froze stolen USDT on Ethereum in 37 minutes, and Circle froze all $823 million in stolen USDC, helping lock up about 44% of the haul, according to blockchain analytics firm Elliptic.

North Korea’s launderers have since spread the remaining funds across about 12,000 wallets. It’s unclear whether any funds in the EU case have been frozen.

The incident adds to an already unprecedented week for crypto security. Beyond the record-breaking Binance hack, Brazilian exchange Mercado Bitcoin just today reported halting withdrawals after a $40 million hack of its own. Likewise today, BTCPay Server told merchants to switch off their servers amid reports of hot wallet attacks.

#5145. Liquid Network restores full bitcoin backing following $320 million exploit

Get The Block’s latest crypto news and price alerts for the coins you follow, right in Chrome. Add our free extension here.

Samson Mow, a Liquid Federation board member, said Blockstream provided the missing bitcoin after the September exploit left nearly 600 BTC unreturned.

The Liquid Federation announced Tuesday that the sidechain's bitcoin reserves are back to full 1:1 backing for all LBTC, nearly two months after an exploit in which approximately $320 million in bitcoin was withdrawn from the network.

Users have been able to swap Bitcoin (BTC) and LBTC again for several weeks through participating providers. Direct Federation peg-outs were previously restricted pending additional security checks, although some semi-automated peg-outs have since resumed. Tuesday's announcement did not say whether the remaining restrictions had been lifted. Reserve balances are published at liquid.network, where Liquid node operators can also verify the backing themselves.

"We extend our sincere appreciation to the Bitcoin community, Liquid users, and ecosystem partners for their patience, understanding, and continued engagement throughout the recovery process," the Liquid Federation said. "Your support and involvement has been instrumental as we worked to restore the network's reserves and strengthen its underlying infrastructure."

About 4,000 BTC was taken from Liquid's Federation wallet in the Sept. 6 exploit. The attacker sent back roughly 3,400 BTC the next day after Blockstream, the developer of Liquid Network's underlying technology, patched the affected bridge nodes, but withheld about 600 BTC. Adam Back-led Blockstream later rejected the attacker's demand for a 10% bug bounty in exchange for returning the remaining coins.

"We will not be a party to the precedent that open-source software developed for the good of the Bitcoin community should subject its developers to paying a ransom that far exceeds their economic participation," Blockstream said at the time.

Liquid Federation board member and former Blockstream executive Samson Mow reported a 50 BTC deposit into the Federation wallet on Oct. 28, leaving 550 BTC outstanding. Another 300 BTC arrived on Monday, followed by a further 250 BTC later that day, bringing the reserves back to full backing. Mow identified Blockstream as the source of the funds. "As stated many times, Blockstream," he wrote on X when asked where the bitcoin was coming from.

Liquid's September incident report traced the exploit to a bug involving cached range proof verifications in Elements, the underlying software used to run the Liquid Network. The attacker was able to create approximately 4,000 unbacked LBTC, which were then used to withdraw bitcoin through Liquid Network wallet and trading platform SideSwap, a Federation member with a peg-out authorization key. Liquid said its network operators were not hacked and no keys were compromised.

Elements v23.3.4 was released on Sept. 9 to address the vulnerability. Liquid resumed producing blocks later that day, with user transactions returning on Sept. 10, although Federation peg-outs remained disabled at that stage.

In its latest update, the Federation said it was preparing changes to governance and oversight, as well as additional technical and operational safeguards. It expects to share further details in the coming weeks.

As they departed, they were gifted that signature Pentreath chair—a miniature keychain version, cast in polished brass—a charming symbol of the decades to come.

#5142. CFTC Opens U.S. Stock Market to Equity Perpetual Futures

The CFTC has cleared perpetual futures on individual stocks to trade on dual-registered U.S. exchanges for the first time.

Perpetual futures on individual stocks can begin trading on U.S.-regulated exchanges for the first time, the Commodity Futures Trading Commission said Tuesday, extending a product that was confined to offshore crypto venues a year ago into the equity market.

In a statement, CFTC Chairman Michael S. Selig said the contracts, which he called “Equity Perps,” were deemed approved under Commission Regulation 40.3 as security futures products.

They may list on exchanges that hold a CFTC designated contract market registration and a notice registration with the Securities and Exchange Commission as a national securities exchange for security futures.

“This expansion marks a significant step towards allowing U.S. market participants, consistent with applicable law, to avail themselves of the advantages of perpetual futures,” Selig said. He said bringing the products inside the agency’s framework means Americans no longer need to “turn to offshore, and often unregulated, venues.”

The contracts fall under the Commodity Exchange Act, federal securities law and the rulebooks of both agencies. The statement names no exchange, lists no contracts and sets no launch date.

Those details are expected to come from the designated contract markets themselves.

Perpetual futures have no expiration date. Instead of settling on a fixed day, longs and shorts exchange periodic funding payments that keep the contract price tied to spot. The structure has dominated crypto derivatives for years, with most volume on platforms outside U.S. jurisdiction.

The CFTC approved the first U.S.-listed bitcoin perpetual, Kalshi’s BTCPERP, on May 29, and Kalshi went live with the product soon after. The same day, staff granted no-action relief that lets Coinbase Financial Markets route U.S. clients into offshore perp and options liquidity. Kalshi added gold and silver perpetuals in September.

In his statement Tuesday, Selig described the equity contracts as an extension of that May decision “beyond digital commodities and into traditional securities.”

The legal basis is older. The Commodity Futures Modernization Act of 2000 created a joint CFTC-SEC regime for security futures products, and the two agencies signed a memorandum of understanding on March 11 to coordinate rulemaking and enforcement.

Selig cited both, and repeated SEC Chairman Paul S. Atkins’s description of security futures as stranded in a regulatory “no man’s land” for lack of harmonization. The agencies had set out a joint crypto asset taxonomy earlier in the year.

Not everyone accepts the CFTC’s reading. CME Grou  sued the agency in June over the Kalshi approval, arguing that a contract built on exchanged funding payments is a swap under Dodd-Frank, not a future.

That case is pending. An equity version adds the SEC’s jurisdiction to the mix and may draw a new set of objections from securities-side participants.

The approval lands five days after the largest theft in crypto’s history. On Oct. 29, attackers that TRM Labs and Chainalysis link to North Korea’s TraderTraitor group drained about $6.3 billion from Binance’s hot wallets in roughly 20 minutes.

Binance reopened withdrawals on Sunday and says it has replenished about 95% of affected assets.

Selig used the breach to press the case he made in Tuesday’s statement.

“The real issue here is that these are unregulated offshore exchanges, and they don’t have the same sorts of safeguards that we have in our financial markets here in the United States,” he told CNBC on Oct. 30.

He said the hack “proves our exact point” about the agency’s October proposal, an advance notice of rulemaking that would create a voluntary federal registration path for crypto exchanges with cybersecurity, proof-of-reserves and custody requirements. Selig has said the CFTC cannot make that registration mandatory without Congress.

The equity perp approval works from the same premise. Perpetuals built the offshore derivatives business; moving them onto dual-registered U.S. exchanges puts the margin, clearing and surveillance rules of two federal agencies around them.

Selig framed it as a choice between regulated access and “being forced to turn to offshore, and often unregulated, venues.”

#5141. Bitcoin Firm Strive Climbs Up the Treasury Ranks With Another Big Buy

Bitcoin Firm Strive Climbs Up the Treasury Ranks With Another Big Buy

Strive, the Dallas-based company co-founded by Vivek Ramaswamy, now holds 43,124 Bitcoin after buying 124 BTC more last week. That stash is now worth roughly $3.3 billion, and good for about 0.2% of the 21 million coins that will ever exist.

That leaves it fourth among publicly traded companies, 390 coins behind Twenty One Capital (which holds 43,514 BTC) and 876 BTC behind Metaplanet (which holds 44,000 BTC), according to Bitcoin Treasuries data.

At Bitcoin’s price of about $77,000, those gaps are worth roughly $30 million and $67 million, respectively.

Strive is a Bitcoin treasury company—a publicly listed firm that keeps Bitcoin on its balance sheet, so anyone with a brokerage account can get exposure by buying its stock. Michael Saylor’s Strategy popularized the playbook back in 2020, and several other companies followed suit, including Strive.

Its coins cost an average of $87,785 each, or $3.8 billion in total, against a market value of $3.5 billion on Oct. 23, per its prospectus. Bitcoin has slipped since, so the pile is now worth a bit less than Strive paid for it.

The latest purchase covers Oct. 24 through Oct. 30 and was disclosed today in an SEC filing, the report US-listed companies must submit when something material happens. Strive paid about $10 million, an average of $80,526 per Bitcoin including fees, CEO Matt Cole said on X.

Strive acquired 124 $BTC for $10M at an average cost of $80,526 per bitcoin, bringing total holdings to ₿43,124.⁰⁰- Existing cash funded most purchases⁰- SATS ATM used for first time, representing 100% of weekly ATM proceeds⁰- Amplification Ratio: 62.7%⁰⁰$ASST $SATA $SATS pic.twitter.com/rFrS6y4AVc

— Matt Cole (@ColeMacro) November 2, 2026

Most of the money came from existing cash, which fell from $504.4 million to $490.4 million over the week. Strive also raised money for the first time by selling shares of a new security called SATS on the open market, Cole said, and that made up all of the week’s fundraising. It still holds 505,000 shares of Strategy’s preferred stock, known as STRC, worth about $50 million.

SATS is a preferred stock—a type of share that pays a fixed dividend before common shareholders see anything. Strive sold 7 million of them at $90 each in an October offering that raised about $630 million, and they carry a 15% annual dividend on a $100 stated value, paid every business day from Nov. 9.

On Oct. 26, Strive opened an at-the-market program—a way to sell new shares in small batches at the going price—for up to $1 billion more SATS. The prospectus says Strive expects to pay the dividends mainly by raising more capital, including selling more shares. The company has set aside 18 months of dividends in a reserve and reported no debt as of Oct. 23.

Strive’s amplification ratio, the preferred stock and debt it carries measured against the value of its Bitcoin, stood at 62.7%, or about $62.70 of obligations for every $100 of Bitcoin.

The company’s CEO has said ending 2026 as the second-largest public Bitcoin holder is possible. “I wouldn’t say that’s my base case,” Cole said on the One Chair podcast in September, “but I would say it’s in the possible set of cards.”

Saylor’s Strategy holds 849,327 Bitcoin, nearly 20 times Strive’s pile.

Twenty One Capital still holds the same 43,514 Bitcoin it had when co-founder Jack Mallers stepped down as CEO in July. His exit came as a plan by Tether to merge three Bitcoin businesses collapsed. The stock had fallen more than 80% from its highs, and his successor, Raphael Zagury, has said Twenty One should be judged on cash flow and capital discipline.

Metaplanet, a Tokyo-listed company that pivoted from hotels to Bitcoin in 2024, holds 44,000 Bitcoin after overtaking mining firm MARA in April. It has a stated goal of 100,000 by the end of 2026.

At last week’s pace of 124 Bitcoin, Strive would pass Twenty One in four weeks and Metaplanet in eight, assuming neither company buys more.

#5139. Ethereum Layer 2 Aztec gets Tierra V6 upgrade to support faster private apps

Get The Block’s latest crypto news and price alerts for the coins you follow, right in Chrome. Add our free extension here.

Tierra V6 fixes a critical V5 security issue and speeds up private transaction proofs, with new apps launching or preparing to launch on Aztec.

The upgrade resolves vulnerabilities found in earlier versions, including a critical issue in V5, the foundation said. It also adds a new AI-based system for security testing.

When asked why the critical issue wasn't fixed sooner, an Aztec spokesperson said the fix required the whole network to move to a new version.

"Because Aztec is a decentralized network, if a critical vulnerability is discovered, the entire network must upgrade to the new version to implement the fix," the spokesperson told The Block.

Aztec has no central operator that can make the change on its own, the spokesperson said, adding that there are plans to make future upgrades faster if vulnerabilities are discovered.

Aztec said its network has more than 3,000 validators, no centralized sequencer, and no privileged viewing keys.

Besides the security fixes, Tierra makes private transaction proofs twice as fast, taking one second to generate on a native device, according to the foundation.

Messages from Ethereum can now reach Aztec in as little as 12 seconds, compared with an average of two minutes before, the foundation said, noting that the change speeds up deposits and other transactions between the networks.

Developers on Aztec can choose which functions of their apps are private and which are public, the foundation said, adding that private functions run and generate proofs on the user's device, while public functions run on the network.

The upgrade comes shortly after Aztec Labs relaunched zk.money, a self-custodial wallet for private crypto payments, three years after discontinuing the original product.

"With Aztec Tierra and zk.money, users can deposit directly from any Ethereum wallet and make lightning-fast private transactions without complicated bridging workflows," Joe Andrews, co-founder and CEO of Aztec Labs, said in a statement.

Other apps launching or preparing to launch include Galactica for private AI inference, Nemi for private perpetuals trading, and the private browser wallet Nulo, according to the announcement.

Xochi supports private intents and trading, while Nyx lets users make private payments to any Ethereum wallet. Olla offers liquid staking for the Aztec token.

Rolling Chat, a private message board based on Ethereum co-founder Vitalik Buterin's Aztec experiments, is also on the list.

Aztec also introduced a new brand and website alongside Tierra, describing its aim as becoming the platform for "unstoppable private apps."

#5138. Where Does Brock Purdy Live? Exploring the Homes of the San Francisco 49ers Quarterback

He spends plenty of time at Levi Stadium as the quarterback of the San Francisco 49ers, but where does Brock Purdy live when he’s off the field? The football player was born and raised in Queen Creek, Arizona, and played football at Perry High School in nearby Gilbert. While Purdy left Arizona in 2018 to play at Iowa State University, he’s still fond of his home state. “I love the state of Arizona and everyone that’s been a part of my life,” he told reporters ahead of the 2024 Super Bowl. “And obviously, I’m trying to represent them as best as I can for as long as my career goes.”

At Iowa State, Purdy was a three-time All-Big 12 quarterback and was the winningest quarterback in the school’s history. The University’s Midwest environment suited Purdy. “My favorite part is the people, just because of how down-to-earth and real everyone is,” he told the Iowa Stater in 2024. “Everyone wants to help each other out. There’s no selfishness. I don’t know what it is about the culture in Iowa, but everybody just loves one another authentically. And when you go back, you feel that every time.” Purdy also met his future wife, Jenna, his freshman year at Iowa State. The couple got engaged in 2023 and tied the knot back in Iowa in 2024.

In 2022, the San Francisco 49ers selected Purdy as the final pick of the NFL Draft. This pick typically earns the harsh nickname Mr. Irrelevant, but Purdy quickly proved the moniker wrong, becoming starting quarterback in his rookie season. Now in his fifth season with the 49ers, Purdy is also building a successful life off the field. Read on to learn about Brock Purdy’s homes over the years.

Teammates—and onetime roommates—Alfredo Gutierrez, Nick Zakelj, and Purdy in the locker room in 2022

At the beginning of his NFL career, Purdy lived more like a recent college grad than a pro football player. During his rookie year, Purdy shared a house with offensive linemen Alfredo Gutierrez and Nick Zakelj. The trio even gave out a Christmas card from the household to teammates. In an interview with NBC Sports Bay Area, Gutierrez revealed that Purdy did most of the cooking out of the three and that they spent downtime watching football, movies, and playing Mario Super Slugger.

By 2023, Purdy had only one roommate: Zakelj. “So he and I are still splitting rent,” Purdy told the TODAY Show. “I still drive my Toyota Sequoia. Other than that, it’s pretty simple.”

Purdy with his wife, Jenna, and daughter, Millie Joleen, in 2025. They now have a second child on the way.

Purdy left his shared home in 2024 when he married his wife, Jenna. They now live in a rental home in the Bay Area with their daughter, Millie Joleen, who was born in July 2025. The couple announced in July 2026 that they would soon be welcoming a second child.

The couple focused on making the home family-friendly, adding plenty of storage for their daughter’s toys, and durable furniture for hosting. “Becoming new parents, we really wanted this house to feel like a home where our family is going to continue to grow,” Jenna said in a video unveiling the couple’s living room and bedroom makeovers, which they worked on with Pottery Barn.

In May 2025, Purdy signed a five-year, $265 million deal with the 49ers, making him the fifth-highest-paid quarterback in the league. The hefty pay raise led Purdy to start his real estate portfolio. His teammate, 49ers tight end George Kittle, revealed on a podcast in June of that year that Purdy had purchased a home in Nashville.

Purdy with teammate George Kittle in 2025

“He did purchase a house in Nashville, Tennessee,” Kittle said. “He will be kind of a neighbor to me at some point. That will be pretty fun, because I’ll have a quarterback that can throw to me all the time, that’s actually on my team.” That summer, Purdy was seen training at Kittle’s football compound in Nashville.

#5135. Binance says hackers used fake job interview, internal AI tool in $6.3 billion breach

Get The Block’s latest crypto news and price alerts for the coins you follow, right in Chrome. Add our free extension here.

Binance said it detected and prevented 57 suspected North Korea-linked cyber threats in 2026, including similar attempts involving fraudulent interviews and malicious software.

Binance said hackers used a fraudulent job interview to compromise an employee’s computer, then used an internal AI tool to access its wallet infrastructure and steal more than $6.3 billion in digital assets.

In an initial incident report published Tuesday, the world's largest crypto exchange detailed how attackers used malware and stolen credentials to access its wallet-signing systems. Binance said it has frozen or intercepted about $2.85 billion in assets and replenished 98% of impacted assets.

"This wasn't one mistake or one breach — it was a months-long campaign. We're publishing the full report because the lessons go far beyond Binance," co-CEO Richard Teng said on X. "The speed of coordination could not be matched in traditional finance."

Binance said the FBI had linked the attack to North Korea, while its own investigation found that hackers used social engineering, malware, and compromised internal AI tooling to gain access to its wallet-signing infrastructure and authorize unauthorized transactions.

According to the report, the attackers first targeted a Wallet DevOps engineer in June through a tampered Homebrew installation downloaded while he was trying to install an AI coding tool. Binance said its security team detected the anomaly, replaced the device, and rotated the affected credentials, but the incident exposed information about the engineer's role and colleagues. In September, attackers then targeted another employee through a fraudulent recruitment process, posing as company executives before tricking him into downloading a malicious coding assignment that infected his work computer and gave hackers unauthorized access.

On Oct. 20, the attackers used the compromised employee's computer and an internal AI tool to gain control of two other engineers' devices, discovering a container deployment permission on one of them. Nine days later, they used the stolen credentials to deploy a container and run a malicious script that launched the wallet attack.

Binance said it detected and prevented 57 suspected North Korea-linked cyberattacks throughout 2026, many involving similar tactics such as fraudulent interviews, malicious developer tools, and social engineering. On Monday, North Korean leader Kim Jong-un's sister called FBI claims linking North Korea to the hack a "grave political provocation."

Binance said it has since closed the entry points used in the attack, rebuilt or replaced affected systems, strengthened device controls, and expanded AI-based behavioral anomaly detection.

Separately, the exchange continued restoring normal operations, reopening withdrawals in phases for dozens of assets across BSC, Ethereum, Solana, XRP, Sui, Arbitrum, Optimism, TRON and Base networks starting at 4 a.m. UTC on Nov. 3. Binance said it was prioritizing tokens based on user demand over the past week. The exchange later reported that hourly deposits exceeded withdrawals, describing the shift as a sign that flows were stabilizing and user activity was improving.

#5134. Claude Fable 5.5, GPT 6.2 Astra Could Be Right Around the Corner, Leaks Suggest

Claude Fable 5.5, GPT 6.2 Astra Could Be Right Around the Corner, Leaks Suggest

Users on X say Anthropic is quietly testing an unreleased version of Mythos and Fable, and OpenAI is about to launch GPT 6.2 Astra.

Nobody has confirmed anything but the rumors are based on a handful of showcase demos, plus a catalog entry and a hidden website label that were both removed soon after appearing.

Fable is the top commercial tier of Claude, the chatbot that competes with ChatGPT, and the priciest flagship Anthropic sells for writing software and handling office work. Mythos is a restricted model that Anthropic offers to a select group of clients, including government and cyber giants due to its capabilities.

Anthropic filed for an IPO, its first sale of shares to the public, on October 26, reporting $16.29 billion in revenue for the first half of 2026 against $1.16 billion a year earlier.

Anthropic launched Fable 5 on June 9, pulled it offline for 18 days under U.S. export controls, which are government limits on who may use a technology, and released Fable 5.1 on September 1. Opus 5.5 followed on September 22, and Anthropic says it performs at the level of Fable 5.1 on most tasks at a fraction of the price.

The chatter began on October 1, when users posted screenshots of Claude still labeled Fable 5.1 beside outputs they said looked stronger. On October 11, pseudonymous user Chetaslua, known for sharing insights on unreleased models and spotting changes in outputs, logs and other signals of new models before they are officially announced, wrote that “Claude Opus 5.5 is routing to a new model,” adding that it could be either Fable 5.5 or a new Opus model.
